## Family: agent-sdk

### `claude/agent-sdk/config-loading`

```yaml
id: claude/agent-sdk/config-loading
provider: claude
family: agent-sdk
name: Claude Code configuration loading (settingSources)
what: The SDK can load the same filesystem-based Claude Code features an interactive session uses — CLAUDE.md and rules, skills, filesystem hooks, and permission settings — controlled by the settingSources option. Omitting it loads user, project, and local settings (CLI parity); passing [] limits the agent to what you configure in code.
surfaces: [sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  setting_sources/settingSources accepts "user" (~/.claude/), "project" (<cwd>/.claude/
  plus CLAUDE.md/rules/skills up the tree), and "local" (CLAUDE.local.md, settings.
  local.json). Loaded inputs are additive; no hard precedence between levels. Skills
  discovered via setting sources and gated by the skills option ("all", a name list, or
  []); no programmatic skill-registration API. Filesystem hooks from settings.json run
  alongside programmatic hooks. Some inputs load regardless of settingSources: managed/
  server-managed policy settings, ~/.claude.json global config, auto memory at
  ~/.claude/projects/<project>/memory/ (CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY=1 to disable),
  and claude.ai MCP connectors — documentation warns not to rely on default options for
  multi-tenant isolation. systemPrompt is the alternative to CLAUDE.md for injecting
  context in code.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/claude-code-features
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: settingSources option, per-source load locations, skills/hooks loading, inputs read regardless of settingSources, multi-tenant isolation warning
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  The engine authors exactly the filesystem inputs settingSources governs and
  relies on the host loading them under the "project" scope: CLAUDE.md (with
  @-imports of .engine/conduct/defaults.md and .engine/conduct/operator.md),
  AGENTS.md (Codex analog, engine-managed-block structure), .claude/skills/*
  (13 skills), .claude/settings.json (hooks), .claude/settings.local.json
  (permissions). No settingSources option is ever set because no SDK is driving.
note: >
  This is the one agent-sdk record whose subject-matter the engine most depends
  on — its files must load correctly under the host's default project/user/local
  loading. But the SDK's settingSources control itself is unused; the engine
  never programmatically scopes config loading. Cross-provider load parity
  (CLAUDE.md vs AGENTS.md) is enforced by .engine/tools/provider_parity_check.py
  / provider_vocab_check.py, hand-built machinery.
```

**Assessment:** action mode observes; enforcement none — signal only; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `OBSERVATION ONLY`** — The one SDK record the engine depends on by proxy: its files must load under the host's default setting sources; parity is tooling-enforced.

### `claude/agent-sdk/custom-tools`

```yaml
id: claude/agent-sdk/custom-tools
provider: claude
family: agent-sdk
name: Custom tools (in-process SDK MCP server)
what: Define your own functions as tools Claude can call, running in-process rather than as a separate server. A tool has a name, description, input schema, and async handler; tools are bundled into an in-process MCP server and passed to query(). Handlers return a content array (text/image/audio/resource blocks), optional structuredContent, and an optional isError flag.
surfaces: [sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: none
mechanics: >
  TypeScript: tool(name, description, zodSchema, handler, extras) wrapped by
  createSdkMcpServer({name, version, tools}). Python: @tool(name, description,
  schema, annotations=...) decorator wrapped by create_sdk_mcp_server(name, version,
  tools). Server passed via mcpServers option; tools named mcp__<server>__<tool> and
  auto-approved by listing that name (or wildcard) in allowedTools. Optional MCP tool
  annotations (readOnlyHint, destructiveHint, idempotentHint, openWorldHint) —
  metadata only; readOnlyHint gates parallel calls. Uncaught handler exceptions are
  caught and returned as error results (loop continues); isError composes the message
  Claude reads. structuredContent returns machine-readable JSON (not forwarded by the
  Python @tool decorator — use a standalone MCP server for that). Tool search defers
  SDK MCP tool schemas by default; alwaysLoad keeps a schema in the initial prompt.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/custom-tools
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: tool()/@tool definition, createSdkMcpServer/create_sdk_mcp_server, schemas, annotations, error handling, content/structuredContent return shapes
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  Engine defines tools via standalone MCP servers, not the in-process SDK
  tool()/@tool decorator. .engine/tools/knowledge_mcp_server.py and
  .engine/tools/memory/mcp_server.py use FastMCP (from mcp.server.fastmcp),
  registered in .mcp.json (Claude) and .codex/config.toml (Codex).
note: >
  The engine deliberately uses out-of-process FastMCP servers (the mcp>=1.28.1
  dependency) rather than the SDK's in-process createSdkMcpServer/create_sdk_mcp_server.
  This is exactly the "standalone MCP server" path the custom-tools record names as
  the way to get structuredContent the Python @tool decorator won't forward.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — The engine rides the host agent loop; embedding the SDK would re-home the loop without gaining a guarantee the hooks path lacks. Revisit only if the engine ever needs to own the loop.

### `claude/agent-sdk/hooks`

```yaml
id: claude/agent-sdk/hooks
provider: claude
family: agent-sdk
name: Hooks (programmatic lifecycle callbacks)
what: Register callbacks that run at defined agent events to block, modify, or observe behavior. PreToolUse can deny or rewrite a tool call before it runs; PostToolUse can append context or replace output; other events cover prompts, compaction, permission decisions, subagent start/stop, session and file lifecycle. Programmatic (in-process) callbacks and shell-command hooks from settings files run in the same lifecycle.
surfaces: [sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  Passed in options.hooks keyed by event name, each an array of matchers ({matcher,
  hooks, timeout}); HookMatcher in Python. Matchers are exact-string (letters/digits/
  _/-/space/,/|) or unanchored regex; * or empty matches all. Callback receives
  (input_data, tool_use_id, context) and returns an output object: {} allows;
  hookSpecificOutput sets permissionDecision (allow/deny/ask/defer),
  permissionDecisionReason, updatedInput (PreToolUse) or additionalContext/
  updatedToolOutput (PostToolUse); top-level systemMessage and continue/continue_.
  Multiple hooks run in parallel; deny > defer > ask > allow. Async outputs
  ({async/async_:true, asyncTimeout}) for fire-and-forget side effects. Event set
  differs by SDK — Python supports a core subset (PreToolUse, PostToolUse,
  PostToolUseFailure, UserPromptSubmit, Stop, SubagentStart/Stop, PreCompact,
  PermissionRequest, Notification); TypeScript adds many more (SessionStart/End,
  PostToolBatch, MessageDisplay, PermissionDenied, FileChanged, Worktree*, etc.).
  Hooks fire in the main agent and inside subagents (agent_id/agent_type on input).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/hooks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: full event table, matcher rules, callback input/output schema, priority, async outputs, Python vs TypeScript event availability, timeouts
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  The engine's entire control layer is hand-built as filesystem shell-command
  hooks, the settings-file analog of SDK programmatic hooks — NOT the SDK
  options.hooks callback API. /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.claude/settings.json
  wires SessionStart (boot.py, memory/erasure_observer.py, memory/backup_vault.py,
  projects_sync/projects_sync.py, telemetry.py run-ambient/drain-inbox), PreToolUse
  (modes.py, knowledge_gen.py, self_map.py, product_design/obligation_matrix.py,
  validate.py), PostToolUse (modes.py accept-hook, validate.py accept-hook), Stop
  (close.py), UserPromptSubmit (scent.py), PreCompact (memory/compact.py). Codex
  mirror: .codex/hooks.json via .engine/tools/codex-hook-runner.sh.
note: >
  Substantial hand-built machinery serving the same purpose the SDK hooks
  capability would (block/modify/observe at lifecycle events). modes.py at
  PreToolUse is the engine's own deny/allow gate — the SDK's PreToolUse
  permissionDecision equivalent done in a shell hook. The engine gets these
  events from the host runtime, not from an SDK it drives.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — The engine rides the host agent loop; embedding the SDK would re-home the loop without gaining a guarantee the hooks path lacks. Revisit only if the engine ever needs to own the loop.

### `claude/agent-sdk/managed-agents`

```yaml
id: claude/agent-sdk/managed-agents
provider: claude
family: agent-sdk
name: Claude Managed Agents (hosted agent harness)
what: A hosted, configurable agent harness where Anthropic runs the agent loop, tool execution, and sandbox instead of you. A separate product from the Agent SDK, exposed as a REST API. Best for long-running and asynchronous work; sessions are stateful by design with persistent filesystem and server-side history.
surfaces: [sdk-api, cloud-web]
ownership: vendor-managed
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Four concepts: Agent (model, system prompt, tools, MCP servers, skills — created once,
  referenced by ID), Environment (Anthropic-managed cloud sandbox or self-hosted
  sandbox), Session (a running instance), Events (user turns, tool results, status,
  exchanged over SSE; history persisted server-side and fetchable). Built-in tools:
  Bash, file operations, web search/fetch, and MCP servers; built-in prompt caching and
  compaction. Steer or interrupt mid-run by sending events; scheduled deployments run on
  a cron schedule. Beta — all endpoints require the managed-agents-2026-04-01 beta header
  (the SDK sets it automatically); enabled by default for API accounts. MCP tunnels and
  dreaming are a more limited research preview. Sessions can be deleted; not currently
  eligible for Zero Data Retention or HIPAA BAA. Also offered on Claude Platform on AWS
  with some feature/session differences.
sources:
  - url: https://platform.claude.com/docs/en/managed-agents/overview
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: hosted harness definition, Agent/Environment/Session/Events model, built-in tools, SSE event flow, beta header, statefulness and data-retention caveats
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/overview
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: comparison table positioning Managed Agents as a separate hosted product ("Anthropic runs the agent and the sandbox") versus the in-process SDK
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: none
note: >
  No hosted-harness usage: no REST calls, no managed-agents beta header, no
  Environment/Session/Events model. Unattended runs are instead hand-built as
  the engine-routine skill (.claude/skills/engine-routine/SKILL.md) entering
  .engine/operations/routine-entry.md, driven by a Claude Desktop routine or
  Codex Automation and one GitHub cron (audit-prep.yml). The engine keeps the
  agent loop on the local host, not on an Anthropic-managed sandbox.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

### `claude/agent-sdk/mcp`

```yaml
id: claude/agent-sdk/mcp
provider: claude
family: agent-sdk
name: MCP server connections (external tools)
what: Connect the agent to external MCP servers so it can use their tools without custom implementations. Servers can run as local processes (stdio), connect over HTTP or SSE, or be defined in-process. MCP tools require explicit permission before Claude can call them.
surfaces: [sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Configured in the mcpServers option in code, or in a .mcp.json file loaded when the
  "project" setting source is enabled. Transports: stdio ({command, args, env}),
  http/sse ({type, url, headers}) — "streamable-http" is a .mcp.json alias for
  "http". Tools named mcp__<server>__<tool>; auto-approve via allowedTools (wildcards
  after a literal mcp__<server>__ prefix). Non-blocking connection by default
  (MCP_CONNECTION_NONBLOCKING, MCP_CONNECT_TIMEOUT_MS, alwaysLoad, MCP_TIMEOUT);
  system init message reports per-server status (pending/connected/failed/needs-auth/
  disabled). OAuth 2.1 supported but the SDK runs no interactive flow — pass a bearer
  token in headers. Output over 25,000 tokens (MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS) is spilled to a
  file. Tool search on by default withholds tool schemas until needed.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/mcp
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: transport types, mcpServers/.mcp.json config, naming/allowlisting, connection timing and status, auth, output limits
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >

```

### `claude/agent-sdk/permission-callback`

```yaml
id: claude/agent-sdk/permission-callback
provider: claude
family: agent-sdk
name: canUseTool permission callback
what: A runtime callback invoked when Claude wants a tool that no earlier permission step approved, and when Claude asks a clarifying question via the AskUserQuestion tool. It pauses execution until it returns an allow (optionally with modified input, or a persisted permission rule) or a deny (with a reason Claude reads). It never fires for tools already auto-approved.
surfaces: [sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: none
mechanics: >
  Passed as can_use_tool (Python) / canUseTool (TypeScript) in options. Receives
  (tool_name, input, context/options) where context carries optional suggestions
  (ready-made PermissionUpdate entries) and a cancellation signal (AbortSignal in TS;
  reserved in Python). Returns PermissionResultAllow(updated_input=..., 
  updated_permissions=...) / {behavior:"allow", updatedInput, updatedPermissions} or
  PermissionResultDeny(message=...) / {behavior:"deny", message}. updated_permissions
  echoing a localSettings suggestion writes a rule to .claude/settings.local.json.
  Python requires streaming mode; a finite prompt stream needs a dummy hook or
  in-process MCP server to keep input open. AskUserQuestion delivers 1-4 multiple-
  choice questions (question/header/options/multiSelect, optional TS previewFormat);
  the callback returns {questions, answers} and optional response. AskUserQuestion is
  not available in subagents. The callback may stay pending indefinitely; a defer hook
  decision lets the process exit and resume later.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/user-input
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: canUseTool signature, allow/deny/modify/remember responses, AskUserQuestion clarifying-question flow, streaming-mode requirement, subagent limitation
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: where canUseTool sits in the evaluation flow; auto-approved tools bypass it; dontAsk skips it
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  The interactive approval decision the SDK's canUseTool callback makes is
  instead made by .engine/tools/modes.py at PreToolUse (deny/allow) plus the
  host runtime's own approval UI; persisted rules live in
  .claude/settings.local.json.
note: >
  No can_use_tool/canUseTool callback anywhere (no SDK to register it in). The
  engine's runtime write-gate (modes.py) is the hand-built analog; AskUserQuestion-
  style clarification is left to the host, not engine-implemented.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — The engine rides the host agent loop; embedding the SDK would re-home the loop without gaining a guarantee the hooks path lacks. Revisit only if the engine ever needs to own the loop.

### `claude/agent-sdk/permission-rules`

```yaml
id: claude/agent-sdk/permission-rules
provider: claude
family: agent-sdk
name: Permission rules and modes (allow/deny, permission modes)
what: Declarative controls over which tools run automatically versus need approval. allowedTools/disallowedTools add allow/deny rules; permission modes (default, dontAsk, acceptEdits, bypassPermissions, plan, auto) set global tool behavior. Evaluation order is hooks, deny rules, ask rules, permission mode, allow rules, then the canUseTool callback.
surfaces: [sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  allowed_tools/allowedTools pre-approve (bare name approves all calls; scoped like
  Bash(ls *) approves matching only). disallowed_tools bare name removes the tool from
  context; scoped rule (Bash(rm *)) denies matching calls in every mode including
  bypassPermissions. Read/Edit rules take path patterns (Edit(path) governs all
  file-writing tools; //abs vs /anchored forms). Rules also load from .claude/
  settings.json when the "project" setting source is enabled. permission_mode/
  permissionMode set at query time or changed mid-session via set_permission_mode/
  setPermissionMode. dontAsk denies anything not pre-approved without calling
  canUseTool; acceptEdits auto-approves file ops (and mkdir/rm/mv/cp/sed) within cwd;
  bypassPermissions approves all reaching the mode step (deny/ask rules and hooks
  still apply); plan routes file-edits to canUseTool; auto uses a model classifier.
  Subagents inherit the parent mode; AgentDefinition.permissionMode can override
  except under bypassPermissions/acceptEdits/auto. Unreachable canUseTool triggers a
  CLAUDE_SDK_CAN_USE_TOOL_SHADOWED process warning (TypeScript, as of v2.1.198).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/permissions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: six-step evaluation order, allow/deny rule semantics, all six permission modes, subagent inheritance, shadowed-callback warning
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  Permission control is hand-built across host settings + a PreToolUse gate:
  .claude/settings.local.json carries permissions.allow (~80-entry Bash/MCP
  allowlist) and permissions.defaultMode:"plan"; .engine/tools/modes.py
  (PreToolUse hook) enforces operating-mode gating documented in
  .engine/operations/operating-modes.md; Codex side uses per-agent sandbox_mode
  in .codex/agents/*.toml and default_tools_approval_mode="auto" in
  .codex/config.toml. Agent-level restriction via disallowedTools +
  permissions:read-only in .claude/agents/*.md.
note: >
  The engine uses the host's own settings.json allow/deny + defaultMode keys
  (which the record says the SDK also reads from .claude/settings.json under the
  "project" source) plus its own modes.py write-gate — but never invokes SDK
  allowedTools/permissionMode APIs. The declarative rules exist; the SDK code
  path that would consume them does not.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — The engine rides the host agent loop; embedding the SDK would re-home the loop without gaining a guarantee the hooks path lacks. Revisit only if the engine ever needs to own the loop.

### `claude/agent-sdk/query-api`

```yaml
id: claude/agent-sdk/query-api
provider: claude
family: agent-sdk
name: Agent SDK query API (query() and ClaudeSDKClient)
what: The SDK runs the Claude Code agent loop in your own process as a Python or TypeScript library. A single query() call takes as many agent turns as needed; a session-holding client (Python ClaudeSDKClient, TypeScript continue:true) carries context across calls. Available for Python and TypeScript only; other languages drive the same loop by running the CLI as a subprocess with -p and --output-format json.
surfaces: [sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  Python: query(prompt, options=ClaudeAgentOptions(...)) yields message objects
  (AssistantMessage, ResultMessage, SystemMessage); ClaudeSDKClient is an async
  context manager whose client.query()/receive_response() reuse one session.
  TypeScript: query({prompt, options}) returns an async iterable of SDK messages;
  pass continue:true to resume the most recent session. Single vs streaming input
  modes; several controls (set_permission_mode/setPermissionMode, mcpServerStatus,
  get_context_usage) act on the live query. A single-shot query() raises/throws
  after yielding an error result. Latest libraries this run: Python 0.2.128
  (bundles Claude CLI v2.1.220), TypeScript 0.3.220.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/overview
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: SDK positioning (library that runs the agent loop in-process), Python/TypeScript-only, CLI-subprocess fallback, capability table
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/sessions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: query() vs ClaudeSDKClient, single-turn multi-turn behavior, in-loop permission handling
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-agent-sdk-python/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Python SDK version 0.2.128 / bundled CLI v2.1.220
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-agent-sdk-typescript/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: TypeScript SDK version 0.3.220
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
overlap: none
note: >
  Engine ships no SDK embedding — no claude_agent_sdk dependency in
  /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.engine/pyproject.toml (deps:

  The engine does not run the agent loop in its own process; it rides the
```

### `claude/agent-sdk/sessions`

```yaml
id: claude/agent-sdk/sessions
provider: claude
family: agent-sdk
name: Sessions (continue, resume, fork)
what: The SDK writes each session's conversation history (prompt, tool calls, results, responses) to disk automatically, so an agent can return with full prior context. Continue picks up the most recent session in the directory; resume takes a specific session ID; fork copies a session's history into a new diverging session leaving the original unchanged. Sessions persist the conversation, not the filesystem.
surfaces: [sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Options fields: continue/continue_conversation, resume=<session_id>,
  fork_session/forkSession. Session ID read from ResultMessage.session_id (also on
  the init SystemMessage in TypeScript). Transcripts stored at
  ~/.claude/projects/<encoded-cwd>/<session-id>.jsonl (or under CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR);
  resume requires matching cwd and the file present on the host. TypeScript-only
  persistSession:false keeps a session in memory; Python always persists. Enumerate/
  mutate via listSessions/getSessionMessages/getSessionInfo/renameSession/tagSession
  (TS) and list_sessions/get_session_messages/get_session_info/rename_session/
  tag_session (Python); a SessionStore adapter mirrors transcripts to shared storage
  for cross-host resume. Subagent transcripts stored in separate files, cleaned up
  after cleanupPeriodDays (default 30). The experimental V2 session API
  (createSession) was removed in TypeScript SDK 0.3.142.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/sessions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: continue/resume/fork semantics, session-ID capture, on-disk paths, cross-host resume, listing/mutation functions, V2 API removal
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  The engine hand-builds its own conversation-persistence layer instead of
  consuming SDK continue/resume/fork: .engine/memory/ledger.ndjson (append-only
  turn-delta ledger), .engine/tools/memory/mcp_server.py (recall/pins/withhold),
  .engine/tools/memory/backup_vault.py and erasure_observer.py wired at
  SessionStart in .claude/settings.json, and .engine/tools/memory/compact.py at
  PreCompact. Session grounding is done by .engine/tools/boot.py.
note: >
  Descriptive-adjacent only: the engine reimplements durable cross-session
```

### `claude/agent-sdk/subagents`

```yaml
id: claude/agent-sdk/subagents
provider: claude
family: agent-sdk
name: Subagents (programmatic and filesystem)
what: Spawn separate agent instances that handle focused subtasks in isolated fresh contexts, optionally in parallel, each with its own system prompt and restricted tool set. Only the subagent's final message returns to the parent. Defined programmatically via the agents option, as .claude/agents/ markdown files, or as the built-in general-purpose agent.
surfaces: [sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  agents option maps name -> AgentDefinition with fields: description, prompt
  (required); tools, disallowedTools, model (alias or full ID or 'inherit'), skills,
  memory, mcpServers, initialPrompt, maxTurns, background, effort, permissionMode
  (optional). Invoked through the Agent tool (include "Agent" in allowedTools to
  auto-approve); auto-selected by description or named explicitly in the prompt.
  Programmatic agents override same-named filesystem agents. Subagent context starts
  fresh (only the Agent tool prompt passes down; project CLAUDE.md and listed skills
  load; parent history/system prompt do not). Subagents run in background by default
  (v2.1.198+) and can nest up to three layers (CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH,
  v2.1.219 default). Resumable via agentId in the Agent tool result plus resume=
  session_id. Final message is scanned for control-tag/permission/turn-marker patterns
  before the parent reads it (v2.1.210+). The Workflow tool (TypeScript SDK v0.3.149+)
  scales orchestration to many agents outside the conversation context.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-sdk/subagents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: agents option, full AgentDefinition field table, invocation, inheritance, nesting/background defaults, resume, output scanning, Workflow tool pointer
```

## Family: browser-computer-use

### `claude/browser-computer-use/browser-pane-app-preview`

```yaml
id: claude/browser-computer-use/browser-pane-app-preview
provider: claude
family: browser-computer-use
name: Desktop Browser pane — app preview and self-verification
what: >
  An in-app browser pane in the Claude Desktop Code tab that runs the project's dev server and opens
  the running app so Claude can verify its own changes — taking screenshots, inspecting the DOM,
  clicking elements, filling forms, reading server logs, and fixing issues it finds. Also opens static
  HTML, PDFs, images, and videos from the project. Works for frontend and backend servers.
surfaces: [desktop]
ownership: project
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Server setup is stored in `.claude/launch.json` at the selected folder root (JSON-with-comments),
  with fields name/runtimeExecutable/runtimeArgs/port/cwd/env/autoPort/program/args/url; multiple
  configurations can run several servers, and a `url`-only entry attaches to a server the user already
  runs. `autoPort` governs port-conflict behavior (true = pick a free port, passed via PORT env). The
  pane opens the Browser via Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+B; Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+S selects an element. `autoVerify` (on
  by default, disable per-project with `"autoVerify": false` or the server-dropdown toggle) makes
  Claude verify after every edit. From the server dropdown users start/stop servers, edit config, and
  enable "Persist sessions" to keep cookies/localStorage across restarts. Local dev servers and project
  file previews need no per-site approval, so verification runs without prompts. The Browser can be
  turned off or its saved session data cleared in Settings > Claude Code. Uses a clean browser profile
  separate from the user's personal browser (no saved logins/history).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/desktop
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Preview-your-app behavior, launch.json full field reference, autoVerify default and toggle, autoPort, Persist sessions, Browser pane shortcuts, clean-profile note, Settings toggles.
```

### `claude/browser-computer-use/browser-pane-external-browsing`

```yaml
id: claude/browser-computer-use/browser-pane-external-browsing
provider: claude
family: browser-computer-use
name: Desktop Browser pane — external site browsing
what: >
  The Desktop Browser pane doubles as a tabbed browser for opening documentation, issue trackers, or
  any external site alongside the running app, and Claude can read and act on those pages with the
  same tools it uses to verify the app. Users can sign in to sites in the pane, including popup OAuth
  flows. Even on approved sites Claude will not purchase items, create accounts, or bypass CAPTCHAs
  without user input.
surfaces: [desktop]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config

  Two extra safety checks apply beyond app preview: safety classifiers (the same ones Auto mode uses)
  review Claude's write actions — clicking, typing — on external pages in every permission mode and
  force a prompt when they flag an action; and in modes other than Auto and Bypass permissions a domain
  allowlist check runs before navigating to a new site. First action on an external site shows a
  permission card with "Allow once" / "Always allow" (saved per-site on-device, revocable in Settings)
  / "Deny"; each site including subdomains needs its own approval, while localhost dev servers and
  project files do not. Uses the same safety model as the Claude in Chrome extension and honors the
  same org site allowlist/blocklist. Org managed settings: `browserExternalPageTools: "disabled"`
  stops Claude's tools on external pages (users can still navigate manually); `disableBrowserExternalNavigation: true`
  (JSON boolean only) turns off external navigation entirely for both users and Claude while leaving
  localhost previews working. Uses a clean profile separate from the personal browser; for acting as
  the signed-in user, the docs steer to the Claude in Chrome extension instead.
```

### `claude/browser-computer-use/chrome-safety-model`

```yaml
id: claude/browser-computer-use/chrome-safety-model
provider: claude
family: browser-computer-use
name: Claude in Chrome safety model and site permissions (user side)
what: >
  The intrinsic safety layer governing what Claude may do in the browser and where. Two defensive
  layers run: one checks incoming page content for prompt-injection attempts, another checks every
  action before it runs; Anthropic reports internal attack success rates under 0.08%. Certain sites
  are blocked (adult content, known pirated-content sites); financial sites require permission before
  access. Claude will not, without user input, make purchases, create accounts, bypass CAPTCHAs,
  execute stock trading/investment transactions, input sensitive data, or scrape facial images; the
  user remains responsible for all actions Claude takes on their behalf.
surfaces: [cli, ide, desktop]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Site-level permissions are inherited from the Chrome extension and managed in the extension's
  settings, controlling which sites Claude can browse, click, and type on. High-risk actions (e.g.
  downloading a file, entering sensitive information) are screened: lower-risk actions proceed, unsafe-
  looking ones are blocked or stopped. On login pages and CAPTCHAs Claude pauses and hands control to
  the user. This is the same safety model the Desktop Browser pane uses for external sites.
sources:
  - url: https://support.claude.com/en/articles/12902428-using-claude-in-chrome-safely
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Prompt-injection dual-layer defense and <0.08% figure, blocked site categories, financial-site permission gate, prohibited-without-approval action list, user-responsibility statement.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/chrome
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Site permissions inherited from the extension; pause-and-ask on login/CAPTCHA.
```

### `claude/browser-computer-use/claude-in-chrome`

```yaml
id: claude/browser-computer-use/claude-in-chrome
provider: claude
family: browser-computer-use
name: Claude in Chrome (browser automation from Claude Code)
what: >
  A browser extension that lets Claude Code drive a real Chrome/Chromium window from the CLI or
  the VS Code extension — opening tabs, navigating, clicking, typing, filling and submitting forms,
  reading page text/DOM/console/network, taking screenshots, uploading local files to upload fields,
  and recording GIFs. Claude opens new tabs and shares the browser's existing login state, so it can
  act on any site the user is already signed into. It pauses and asks the user to handle login pages
  and CAPTCHAs manually.
surfaces: [cli, ide]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Exposed as a built-in MCP server / skill named `claude-in-chrome`; the model sees tools such as
  read_page, get_page_text, find, tabs_context_mcp, browser_batch, screenshot, plus click/type/
  navigate/tab-management and gif recording. Launched with `claude --chrome`, or set "Enabled by
  default" via the `/chrome` command (which also checks status, reconnects, and picks among multiple
  connected browsers). In VS Code, invoked with `@browser` in the prompt box; available whenever the
  extension is installed, no flag. Requires the Claude in Chrome extension v1.0.36+ and Claude Code,
  plus `/login` with a direct Anthropic plan (Pro/Max/Team/Enterprise) — API-key or setup-token auth
  keeps Chrome integration off (attempts 403'd before v2.1.216). Works with Chrome, Edge, and other
  Chromium browsers (Brave, Arc, Vivaldi, Opera); not in WSL; not via third-party providers (Bedrock/
  Vertex/Foundry). First use installs a native-messaging host config file at
  com.anthropic.claude_code_browser_extension.json under each browser's NativeMessagingHosts dir
  (Registry key on Windows). Plan-mode behavior: read-only calls (read_page, get_page_text, find,
  console/network reads, screenshot) run without a prompt; state-changing calls (clicks, typing,
  navigation, tab/window management, GIF recording) prompt for approval; as of v2.1.199 a nominally
  read-only call that sets a state-changing flag (createIfEmpty, clear, save_to_disk) also prompts,
  and a browser_batch runs unprompted only if every sub-action is read-only. File uploads (v2.1.211+):
  gated by the session's Read permission rules, capped at 10 MB total per upload, and refused for files
  with multiple hard links. Screenshot save_to_disk writes a file and returns the path as of v2.1.208.
  Org admins can suppress the whole integration by blocking the `claude-in-chrome` MCP server via the
  `deniedMcpServers` managed setting.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/chrome
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Primary reference — capabilities list, --chrome/`/chrome`, prerequisites, plan-mode tool split, file uploads, GIF/screenshot, native-messaging host, version gates.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/vs-code
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Automate browser tasks with Chrome" — @browser invocation in the VS Code extension, shared login state.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Corroborates file-upload fixes (2.1.211), save_to_disk (2.1.208), Windows setup-page and cross-account-token fixes; fetched via summarizing reader so exact version strings are secondary to the doc page's own version annotations.
```

### `claude/browser-computer-use/computer-use`

```yaml
id: claude/browser-computer-use/computer-use
provider: claude
family: browser-computer-use
name: Computer use (screen control)
what: >
  Lets Claude control the actual desktop the way a person would — open apps, move the mouse, click,
  type, scroll, and take screenshots of the screen — to handle GUI-only work that has no CLI, API,
  MCP server, or browser path (native apps, simulators, hardware/design tools). It is the broadest and
  slowest tool, tried only after connectors, Bash, and Claude in Chrome. A research preview.
surfaces: [cli, desktop]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Delivered as a built-in MCP server named `computer-use`, off by default. CLI: enable per project via
  `/mcp` (macOS only, interactive sessions only, not with `-p`, Pro or Max plan). Desktop: toggle in
  Settings > General under "Desktop app" (macOS and Windows, Pro/Max). macOS additionally requires
  Accessibility (click/type/scroll) and Screen Recording (see the screen) system permissions. Per-app
  approval each session: a prompt lists the apps Claude wants to control, extra permissions (e.g.
  clipboard), and how many apps will be hidden; choose "Allow for this session" or "Deny" (approvals
  last the session, or 30 minutes in Dispatch-spawned sessions). Control tiers are fixed by app
  category and cannot be changed: View only (browsers, trading platforms), Click only (terminals,
  IDEs), Full control (everything else); broad-reach apps (terminals/IDEs, Finder/Explorer, System
  Settings) show sentinel warnings before approval. Guardrails: a machine-wide lock allows only one
  session to control the machine at a time (held until the session exits, as of v2.1.195); other apps
  are hidden while Claude works and auto-restored on finish (Desktop has a configurable "Unhide apps"
  toggle and a "Denied apps" list; CLI has neither yet and auto-unhide is always on); the terminal
  window is excluded from screenshots; screenshots are auto-downscaled; and a global Esc (or Ctrl+C)
  aborts immediately with the keypress consumed so injection can't reuse it. Not available via third-
  party providers; not on Linux desktop yet. Each action is checked for prompt injection but it runs
  on the real desktop, not a sandbox.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/computer-use
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: CLI enablement via /mcp, macOS-only/Pro-Max/interactive constraints, tool-selection order, per-app approval, sentinel warnings, machine lock (v2.1.195), app hiding, screenshot downscaling/terminal exclusion, global Esc, CLI-vs-Desktop differences table.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/desktop
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Desktop enablement (Settings > General toggle, macOS+Windows), Accessibility/Screen Recording grants, control tiers table, Denied apps and Unhide toggle, 30-min Dispatch approvals, Linux exclusion.
```

## Family: cli

### `claude/cli/checkpointing-rewind`

```yaml
id: claude/cli/checkpointing-rewind
provider: claude
family: cli
name: Checkpointing and rewind (`/rewind`)
what: Claude Code automatically captures a code checkpoint before each user prompt, letting you rewind file edits and/or conversation to an earlier point, or summarize the conversation from a chosen point, via `/rewind` or double-Esc on an empty prompt.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: local
mechanics: >
  Every user prompt creates a checkpoint; file snapshots are kept for the 100 most recent checkpoints
  and saved with the conversation so `/rewind` works after resume; deleted with sessions after
  `cleanupPeriodDays` (30). Open the menu with `/rewind` or Esc+Esc on an empty prompt (v2.1.216 fixed
  Esc+Esc reliability); if the input has text, double-Esc clears it (saved to history) instead. Menu
  actions: Restore code and conversation, Restore conversation, Restore code, Summarize from here,
  Summarize up to here, Never mind. Code-restore options appear only when the checkpoint has tracked
  edits. Summaries can take optional guidance instructions and insert a "Summarized conversation" marker;
  summarizing is a targeted `/compact` that keeps the session. A `/clear` earlier in the same process adds
  a "previous session" entry at the top of the rewind list (v2.1.191+). Limitations: files changed by
  bash commands, subagent/background edits (except a foreground `context: fork` skill), external/
  concurrent-session changes, and symlinked/hard-linked paths are not tracked/restored. Not a replacement
  for git.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/checkpointing
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Automatic checkpoint capture, /rewind menu actions, summarize options, previous-session entry, limitations
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/interactive-mode
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Esc+Esc empty-prompt rewind vs text-clear behavior
```

No reliance on file-snapshot rewind; the engine's durability model is git (worktrees + protected-branch merge).

```yaml
family: cli
name: Context window management (`/clear`, `/compact`, `/context`)
what: In-session commands manage what occupies the context window: `/clear` starts fresh, `/compact` replaces history with a summary (optionally instruction-guided), and `/context` shows current consumption. Auto-compaction summarizes older history automatically as the conversation approaches the model's input limit.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  `/clear` empties context and starts a new conversation (previous one saved/resumable; resets `/usage`
  totals as of v2.1.211; keeps a `--name`/`/rename` name but not an AI-generated title; input history
  reset). `/compact [instructions]` sends one summarization request over the full history and replaces it
  with the summary plus recent exchanges and up to five recently read files; original messages remain in
  the transcript; `/compact` on a fresh session prints "Not enough messages to compact." Custom compaction
  guidance can be persisted via a "Compact instructions" section in CLAUDE.md. `/context` reports what
  is consuming context (including deferred MCP tool listings). Auto-compaction triggers near the model's
  maximum input size (a context/auto-compact warning is not a usage limit). Effort/thinking that inflate
  context are tunable separately (see effort selection).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sessions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: /clear, /compact [instructions], /context described under "Manage context within a session"
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/costs
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Auto-compaction behavior, /compact CLAUDE.md instructions, /clear cost/reset semantics, /context usage
```

```yaml
  `/desktop` hands the running terminal session to the Desktop app for visual diff review (macOS and x64
  Windows, requires a claude.ai subscription). Related: `--remote-control`/`--rc` and `claude
  remote-control` start a Remote Control server for driving the session from claude.ai/app. Note: the
  target web/desktop/mobile surfaces are owned by other families; this record covers only the CLI-side
  flags/commands that initiate the handoff.
```

### `claude/cli/effort-selection`

```yaml
id: claude/cli/effort-selection
provider: claude
family: cli
name: Reasoning effort selection (`/effort`, `--effort`)
what: The reasoning effort level (adaptive reasoning) is selectable per session and persistently, controlling how much the model thinks. Levels are low, medium, high, xhigh, max (model-dependent), plus a Claude Code-specific `ultracode` setting that sends xhigh and orchestrates dynamic workflows.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  `/effort` opens a slider; `/effort <level>` sets it directly; `/effort auto` resets to the model
  default. Also adjustable in `/model` (left/right arrows), via `--effort <level>` at launch,
  `CLAUDE_CODE_EFFORT_LEVEL` env var, `effortLevel` setting (low/medium/high/xhigh only), and skill/
  subagent frontmatter `effort`. Precedence: env var > configured level > model default; frontmatter
  overrides the session level but not the env var. Default is `high` on every effort-capable model except
  Opus 4.7 (`xhigh`). Unsupported levels fall back to the highest supported at/below the request. `low/
  medium/high/xhigh` persist across interactive sessions; `max` and `ultracode` are session-only.
  `ultracode` (`/effort ultracode`, `--effort ultracode` v2.1.203+, or `"ultracode": true` via
  `--settings`/SDK) adds dynamic-workflow orchestration. `ultrathink` anywhere in a prompt requests deeper
  reasoning for one turn without changing the effort setting. Option+T (Alt+T) toggles extended thinking;
  `MAX_THINKING_TOKENS` sets a fixed budget on fixed-budget models. Enterprise org effort limits cap
  per-model levels (v2.1.195+). Current level shown in session header and status-line `effort.level`.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Effort levels per model, /effort and --effort, ultracode, ultrathink, persistence rules, org effort limits, adaptive reasoning
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: --effort flag (low, medium, high, xhigh, max, ultracode)
```

### `claude/cli/fallback-model-chains`

```yaml
id: claude/cli/fallback-model-chains
provider: claude
family: cli
name: Fallback model chains (`--fallback-model`)
what: One or more fallback models can be configured so that when the primary model is overloaded, unavailable, or returns a non-retryable server error, Claude Code switches to the next model for the current turn instead of failing the request.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Set a session chain with `--fallback-model sonnet,haiku` (comma-separated) or persist with
  `fallbackModel` as an array in settings; the flag takes precedence over the setting. Chains are capped
  at three models after de-duplication; each entry is a model name/alias and `"default"` expands to the
  default model. The switch lasts only the current turn (next message retries the primary first).
  Authentication, billing, rate-limit, request-size, and transport errors never trigger a switch. Entries
  outside `availableModels` are dropped; during compaction Claude Code won't fall back to a
  smaller-context model. No startup confirmation and `/status` doesn't display the chain — the switch
  notice is the first visible sign. Distinct from content-based automatic model fallback from Fable 5 /
  Opus 5 on safety-classifier flags.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/model-config
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Fallback model chains section (flag, setting, cap, trigger conditions) and automatic content-based fallback
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: --fallback-model flag
```

### `claude/cli/headless-print-mode`

```yaml
id: claude/cli/headless-print-mode
provider: claude
family: cli
name: Print / headless mode (`-p` / `--print`)
what: Adding `-p` (or `--print`) to any `claude` command runs it non-interactively — reads stdin, runs the agent loop, prints the result, and exits with code 0 on success or non-zero on failure. It composes with all CLI options and is the primary scripting/CI entry point (delivered via the Agent SDK CLI).
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  `claude -p "<prompt>"` or `cat file | claude -p "<prompt>"`. Reads stdin (piped input capped at 10MB
  since v2.1.128). `--bare` skips auto-discovery of hooks, skills, plugins, MCP servers, auto memory,
  and CLAUDE.md for reproducible CI runs (recommended for scripted/SDK calls; will become the `-p`
  default; requires `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` since it skips OAuth/keychain). `--max-turns` limits agentic
  turns; `--max-budget-usd` caps spend; `--no-session-persistence` suppresses transcript writes for one
  run. User-invoked skills/commands work by embedding `/skill-name` in the prompt; `/model`, `/effort`,
  `/fast`, `/color`, `/rename`, `/mcp`, and `/config key=value` accept arguments in `-p` (v2.1.205+).
  Background Bash tasks are terminated ~5s after the final result; background subagents/workflows are
  waited on up to a 10-minute default cap (`CLAUDE_CODE_PRINT_BG_WAIT_CEILING_MS`). SIGTERM aborts the
  turn, kills the Bash process tree, runs SessionEnd hooks, exits 143.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/headless
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: `-p` basic usage, bare mode, stdin piping, exit codes, background-task grace periods, in-prompt command support
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: `--print`, `--max-turns`, `--max-budget-usd`, `--no-session-persistence`, `--verbose` flags
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

### `claude/cli/interactive-session`

```yaml
id: claude/cli/interactive-session
provider: claude
family: cli
name: Interactive terminal session (REPL)
what: Running `claude` (optionally with an initial prompt) starts an interactive terminal session where you converse with Claude, invoke slash commands and skills with `/`, mention files with `@`, and drive the session with a large set of readline-style and custom keyboard shortcuts. It includes command history (per working directory), reverse search (Ctrl+R), multiline input, a transcript viewer (Ctrl+O), a to-do task list (Ctrl+T), prompt suggestions, emoji shortcodes, on-demand and automatic session recap (`/recap`), and ephemeral side questions (`/btw`).
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Keyboard shortcuts include Ctrl+C (interrupt/clear), Ctrl+D (exit), Esc (interrupt turn), Esc+Esc
  (clear draft or open rewind menu), Shift+Tab (cycle permission modes), Ctrl+O (transcript viewer),
  Ctrl+R (reverse history search), Ctrl+B (background a running task), Ctrl+T (toggle to-do list),
  Ctrl+G / Ctrl+X Ctrl+E (edit prompt in $EDITOR), plus word/line editing bindings (Ctrl+A/E/K/U/W/Y,
  Alt+B/F). Multiline entry via `\`+Enter, Ctrl+J, Option+Enter, or native Shift+Enter (iTerm2, WezTerm,
  Ghostty, Kitty, Warp, Apple Terminal, Windows Terminal); `/terminal-setup` installs the Shift+Enter
  binding for VS Code, Cursor, Alacritty, Zed. Command history is stored per directory and reset by
  `/clear`; reverse search has session/project/all-projects scopes in fullscreen rendering. Prompt
  suggestions appear as grayed-out completions from git history and conversation, accepted with Tab
  (toggle via `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_PROMPT_SUGGESTION` or `/config`). Emoji shortcodes (`:name:`) require
  v2.1.217 (`emojiCompletionEnabled` setting). `/btw <question>` answers from current context with no
  tools and never enters conversation history (fork with `f`). `/recap` and automatic recaps summarize
  the session; toggled in `/config`. Keybindings customizable via `~/.claude/keybindings.json`.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/interactive-mode
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Full keyboard-shortcut tables, multiline input methods, command history, reverse search, prompt suggestions, emoji shortcodes, /btw, task list, session recap
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: `claude` and `claude "query"` entry points; `--ax-screen-reader` and other interactive flags
```

### `claude/cli/model-selection`

```yaml
id: claude/cli/model-selection
provider: claude
family: cli
name: Model selection (`/model`, `--model`, aliases)
what: The active model is selectable at launch and mid-session through several surfaces, using aliases (default, best, fable, sonnet, opus, haiku, sonnet[1m], opus[1m], opusplan) or full model names. Precedence runs `/model` picker > `--model` flag > `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` env > `model` setting, with enterprise allowlists and org defaults layered on top.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  `/model <alias|name>` switches immediately and (v2.1.153+) saves the choice as the default in user
  settings; `/model` with no argument opens a picker (Enter saves default, `s` = session only; effort
  slider adjustable with left/right arrows). `--model <alias|name>` and `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` apply to the
  launched session only; `model` in settings sets a persistent default. Aliases resolve per provider
  (e.g. `opus`→Opus 5, `sonnet`→Sonnet 5 on the Anthropic API); pin exact versions with full names or
  `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_{OPUS,SONNET,HAIKU,FABLE}_MODEL`. `opusplan` uses opus in plan mode, sonnet in
  execution. Resumed sessions keep their saved model unless retired/disallowed or overridden. Enterprise
  controls: `availableModels` allowlist + `enforceAvailableModels` (managed settings) and server-side
  organization model restrictions / organization default model (Anthropic API). Option+P (Alt+P) switches
  model without clearing the prompt. On the Anthropic API an unrecognized model string is rejected
  (v2.1.200+).
```

### `claude/cli/session-branch-fork`

```yaml
id: claude/cli/session-branch-fork
provider: claude
family: cli
name: Session branching and forking
what: `/branch` (in-session) and `--fork-session` (at launch, with `--continue`/`--resume`) create a copy of the conversation so far under a new session ID, leaving the original intact, so you can try a different approach without losing your path.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: local
mechanics: >
  `/branch [name]` copies the transcript up to that point and switches the running process to write to
  the copy; omitting the name derives it from the first prompt (past a compaction summary as of v2.1.198).
  Both branch and original session IDs are printed; the original stays in the picker. In-session
  "Allow for this session" permission grants and in-flight background subagents/Bash carry into the
  branch (their output appears in the new branch). `claude --continue --fork-session` (or
  `--resume ... --fork-session`) forks into a separate process that starts without prior permission
  grants. Branched/forked sessions get their own IDs and appear as separate picker rows, grouped when the
  picker finds multiple entries for one session. Distinct from checkpoint rewind (same-session) and from
  summarize (same-session context compression).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sessions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: /branch mechanics, --fork-session, inheritance table, picker grouping
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: --fork-session flag description
```

### `claude/cli/session-resume-persistence`

```yaml
id: claude/cli/session-resume-persistence
provider: claude
family: cli
name: Session resume and persistence
what: Every conversation is saved continuously to a local per-project transcript, so a session can be resumed after exit or `/clear`. Entry points are `--continue` (most recent), `--resume` (picker or by ID/name), `/resume` (switch inside a session), and `--from-pr`. Sessions can be named, listed in an interactive picker, exported, and located on disk.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: local
mechanics: >
  `claude --continue`/`-c` resumes the most recent session in the current directory; `claude --resume`/`-r`
  opens the picker or resumes a named session/ID directly; `/resume` switches conversations mid-session;
  `claude --from-pr <number>` filters the picker to a PR. `--session-id <uuid>` forces a specific ID;
  `--name`/`-n` and `/rename` set a display name (also `Ctrl+R` in the picker; auto-generated titles and
  default names like `my-app-3f` are not resume handles). Session ID lookup is scoped to the current
  project directory and its git worktrees. Resume restores conversation history, model (with exceptions
  for retired/disallowed/provider-ID models or a `--model`/env override), agent, permission mode (plan
  and bypassPermissions never restored), active goal, and unexpired scheduled tasks; `--mcp-config`,
  `--settings`, `--plugin-dir`, `--fallback-model`, `--add-dir` must be re-passed. On Pro/Max, resuming
  an inactive session over 100k tokens offers resume-from-summary, resume-as-is, or don't-ask-again.
  Picker shortcuts: Ctrl+A (all projects), Ctrl+W (all worktrees), Ctrl+B (current branch), Space
  (preview), Ctrl+R (rename). `/export` copies/saves a rendered transcript. Transcripts stored as JSONL
  at `~/.claude/projects/<project>/<session-id>.jsonl` (relocatable via `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`); retention
  `cleanupPeriodDays` (30 default); `CLAUDE_CODE_SKIP_PROMPT_HISTORY` suppresses writes.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sessions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Resume entry points, what-restores rules, naming, session picker, transcript storage/retention, /export
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cli-reference
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: --continue, --resume, --session-id, --name, --from-pr, --no-session-persistence flags
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: Depended on indirectly. The SessionStart hook carries a `resume` matcher (/Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.claude/settings.json) that re-runs boot.py + memory/erasure_observer.py + backup_vault.py + projects_sync + telemetry on resume. modes.clear_stance fires at every SessionStart (including resume) so a resumed session boots Explore and never inherits a prior Build signal — /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.engine/tools/boot.py (MODES STANCE CLEAR law) and /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.engine/operations/operating-modes.md (step 1).
overlap: The stance machinery's fail-safe rests on the platform re-firing SessionStart on resume; operating-modes.md step 3 also notes that a replayed build-directive on a resumed session is inert because the durable signal was cleared.
note: The engine consumes the native resume lifecycle event but manages none of the transcript storage/naming/--from-pr surface.
```

**Assessment:** action mode blocks/intercepts; enforcement deterministic where the platform honors it; the engine treats it as best-effort; failure mode open; portability provider-specific behind the engine's adapter seam.

**Disposition: `CORE`** — The resume/clear SessionStart matchers are how the stance clear and re-grounding laws hold across session lifecycle.

### `claude/cli/shell-mode`

```yaml
id: claude/cli/shell-mode
provider: claude
family: cli
name: Shell mode (`!` prefix)
what: Prefixing input with `!` runs a shell command directly inside the session without Claude interpreting or approving it, adds the command and its output to the conversation context, and (as of v2.1.186) has Claude respond to the output automatically.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  Typing `! <command>` (e.g. `! npm test`) executes immediately, streams real-time output, and appends
  it to context. Supports Ctrl+B backgrounding, history-based Tab autocomplete of prior `!` commands in
  the project, and live file-path autocomplete on a `/` token (v2.1.193). Exit shell mode with Escape,
  Backspace, or Ctrl+U on an empty prompt. Auto-response after a command lands can be disabled by
  setting `respondToBashCommands` to `false` in settings.json (before v2.1.186 output was added without
  a response). Pasting text starting with `!` enters shell mode automatically.
sources:

    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Shell mode `!` prefix behavior, autocomplete, exit keys, respondToBashCommands toggle, version notes
```

### `claude/cli/status-line`

```yaml
id: claude/cli/status-line
provider: claude
family: cli
name: Custom status line (`statusLine`)
what: A configurable bar at the bottom of the interactive session that runs a user-supplied shell script, receives rich JSON session data on stdin, and displays whatever the script prints (context usage, cost, git status, model, effort, rate limits, PR state, etc.). A parallel `subagentStatusLine` customizes per-subagent rows.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Configure `statusLine: {type: "command", command: "<script-or-inline>", padding, refreshInterval,
  hideVimModeIndicator}` in user or project settings, or run `/statusline "<natural language>"` to have
  Claude Code generate the script and wire up settings. The script reads a JSON object on stdin exposing
  `model.{id,display_name}`, `workspace.*` (current_dir, project_dir, added_dirs, git_worktree, repo),
  `cost.*`, `context_window.*` (used_percentage, sizes, current_usage), `exceeds_200k_tokens`,
  `fast_mode`, `effort.level`, `thinking.enabled`, `rate_limits.{five_hour,seven_day}`, `session_id`,
  `session_name`, `prompt_id`, `transcript_path`, `version`, `output_style.name`, `vim.mode`,
  `agent.name`, `pr.{number,url,review_state}`, `worktree.*`. Output to stdout (multiple lines, ANSI
  colors, OSC 8 links) is rendered. Runs on session start and on assistant messages, `/compact` finish,
  permission-mode change, vim-mode toggle, and optional `refreshInterval` timer; debounced 300ms;
  `COLUMNS`/`LINES` env vars give terminal size (v2.1.153+). Requires workspace trust; disabled when
  `disableAllHooks` is true. `subagentStatusLine` receives a `tasks` array and emits per-row overrides.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/statusline
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: statusLine config keys, /statusline command, full stdin JSON field list, update triggers, subagentStatusLine, trust gate
```

```yaml
note: Divergence of choice — the engine's "▸ Project status / ⚠" block is assistant-narrated from boot.py additionalContext, NOT a native `statusLine` command script. No `statusLine`/`subagentStatusLine` key exists in either settings file. Hand-rolled substitute for a native surface.
```

### `claude/cli/structured-output`

```yaml
id: claude/cli/structured-output
provider: claude
family: cli
name: Structured output (`--output-format`, `--json-schema`)
what: In print mode, `--output-format` controls the response format (text, json, stream-json) and `--json-schema` constrains the answer to a supplied JSON Schema, with the validated value returned in a `structured_output` field. Streaming emits newline-delimited JSON events including init/result metadata, cost, retries, and subagent messages.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: none
mechanics: >
  `--output-format text|json|stream-json`. `json` returns result, session_id, usage, cost (with
  `total_cost_usd` and per-model breakdown); `stream-json` (with `--verbose` and
  `--include-partial-messages`) emits per-line events, the last being a `result` message. `--json-schema
  '<schema>'` with `--output-format json` validates output; invalid schemas exit with a diagnostic
  (v2.1.205+ enforces this); `format` keyword accepted as annotation but not enforced. `--input-format
  text|stream-json` sets input encoding. Stream events include `system/init` (model, tools, mcp_servers,
  plugins, `plugin_errors`, `mcp_server_errors`, `capabilities`), `system/api_retry`,
  `system/plugin_install`, and subagent `assistant`/`user` messages keyed by `parent_tool_use_id`;
  `--forward-subagent-text` (v2.1.211) / `CLAUDE_CODE_FORWARD_SUBAGENT_TEXT` also emit subagent text and
  thinking. `--replay-user-messages`, `--include-hook-events`, `--prompt-suggestions` add further stream
  events.

    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: --output-format, --input-format, --json-schema, --include-partial-messages, --forward-subagent-text, --replay-user-messages flags
```

```yaml
id: claude/cli/vim-editor-mode

  >>/<<, J, u, `.` repeat), text objects (iw/aw, i"/a", i(/a(, etc.), and visual mode (v/V with
  motions and operators; block-wise Ctrl+V not supported). `vimInsertModeRemaps` (v2.1.208) maps a
  two-character INSERT-mode sequence such as `{"jj": "<Esc>"}` to Escape; read only from user settings,
  `--settings`, and managed settings — project settings entries are ignored. The current vim mode is
  exposed to status-line scripts via the `vim.mode` field.
```

## Family: cloud-web

### `claude/cloud-web/auto-fix-pull-requests`

```yaml
id: claude/cloud-web/auto-fix-pull-requests
provider: claude
family: cloud-web
name: Auto-fix pull requests
what: A per-PR toggle that has Claude watch a pull request and automatically respond to CI check failures and reviewer comments, pushing a fix when one is clear and asking first when a request is ambiguous or architecturally significant. Requires the Claude GitHub App installed on the repository.
surfaces: [cloud-web, cli, github-ci]
ownership: mixed
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Turned on several ways: in a web session, open the CI status bar and select Auto-fix; from the terminal, run
  `/autofix-pr` on the PR's branch (detects the open PR with `gh`, spawns a web session, enables auto-fix in one
  step); from the mobile app, ask Claude to auto-fix; or paste any PR URL into a session and ask. It is a per-PR
  toggle, cleared from the CI status bar or by telling Claude to stop. When active, Claude receives GitHub
  events (new review comments, CI check failures) and for each: makes and pushes a clear fix, asks before acting
  on ambiguous/architecturally significant requests, or notes duplicates/no-op events and moves on. GitHub does
  not emit a webhook when the base branch advances into a merge conflict, so auto-fix cannot react to conflicts
  on its own. Claude may reply to review-comment threads on GitHub using the user's GitHub account (labeled as
  Claude Code); this can trigger `issue_comment`-based automation (e.g. Atlantis, Terraform Cloud, custom
  Actions), which the docs warn to review before enabling.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Enablement paths (web bar, `/autofix-pr`, mobile, PR URL), per-PR toggle, event-response behavior, merge-conflict gap, and the on-behalf-comment / automation-trigger warning.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  Directly opposed to the engine's merge-gate discipline. There is no `/autofix-pr`, no CI-status Auto-fix
  toggle, no watching of open PRs. build-orchestration.md makes the operator's merge "the only unbypassable
  gate" (L10-13) and forbids the engine from clearing its own gate — "the engine never labels its own change to
  clear its own gate" (L48-53). Reviewers are read-only and the orchestrator is the single writer BEFORE submit
  (step 5), so nothing auto-pushes fixes to a live PR in response to CI failures or review comments.
note: >
  This is a genuine philosophical conflict, not just an unused feature: Auto-fix pushes clear fixes without asking
  and can reply to review threads on the user's behalf, whereas the engine's whole control model routes every
  change through a human-reviewed, human-merged PR and treats an unbuilt/half-built leg as a divergence to
  surface, never to silently self-heal (build-orchestration.md L377-390).
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — Head-on conflict with the merge-gate law: nothing self-heals a live PR, and the engine never clears its own gate. Rejected on principle.

### `claude/cloud-web/cloud-environments`

```yaml
id: claude/cloud-web/cloud-environments
provider: claude
family: cloud-web
name: Cloud environments
what: A saved configuration object that controls a cloud session's network access, environment variables, and setup script. The same environments apply wherever a cloud session starts (web, terminal, Claude Tag, routines, mobile, Desktop). An onboarding-created "Default" environment carries no configuration of its own beyond Trusted network access.
surfaces: [cloud-web, cli, desktop]
ownership: mixed
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Created, edited, and archived from the environment selector at claude.ai/code (the cloud icon in the row
  above the message box); there is no settings page or direct URL for the selector. The dialog holds a Name,
  Network access level, Environment variables, and Setup script. Environments a user creates are personal to
  their account. Environment variables use `.env` format (one KEY=value per line); each session copies the
  values once at startup into ordinary env vars, so edits affect only later sessions. There is no dedicated
  secrets store, and the dialog warns that anyone using the environment can read the values, so credentials
  should not be added. Environments can only be archived, not deleted; archiving blocks new sessions on any
  surface but lets running ones continue. Owners/admins on Team and Enterprise plans create organization-shared
  environments from the Cloud environments page in admin settings; these appear in every member's selector
  and are the only environments Claude Tag channel sessions use. From the CLI, `/remote-env` opens a picker
  and saves the chosen default to the `remote.defaultEnvironmentId` key in user settings (overridable by a
  higher-precedence settings layer); it only sets the default and cannot create/edit environments. Owners/admins
  set the organization default environment at claude.ai/admin-settings/claude-code.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Default environment, selector location, env-var `.env` semantics, no-secrets-store warning, archive-only lifecycle, org-shared environments, `/remote-env` and `remote.defaultEnvironmentId`.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Confirms same environments apply across surfaces and that Claude Tag channel sessions use org-shared environments only.
```

### `claude/cloud-web/cloud-sessions`

```yaml
id: claude/cloud-web/cloud-sessions
provider: claude
family: cloud-web
name: Claude Code on the web (cloud sessions)
what: Runs Claude Code tasks on Anthropic-managed cloud VMs at claude.ai/code rather than the user's machine. Sessions persist after the browser closes and can be monitored from the Claude mobile app. Currently a research preview for Pro, Max, and Team users, and for Enterprise users with premium seats or Chat + Claude Code seats.
surfaces: [cloud-web, cli, desktop]
ownership: mixed
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Each session gets a fresh VM running Ubuntu 24.04 with the repository cloned and toolchains pre-installed,
  regardless of the user's OS. Approximate resource ceilings: 4 vCPUs, 16 GB RAM, 30 GB disk; the VM may stop
  jobs needing significantly more memory. Sessions can be started from the web, the terminal (`claude --cloud`),
  Claude Tag, routines, and the mobile/Desktop apps. Sessions stop after a period of inactivity and the VM is
  reclaimed ("Environment expired"); reopening from claude.ai/code provisions a fresh VM with conversation
  history restored. Rate limits are shared with all other Claude/Claude Code usage on the account; there is no
  separate compute charge for the VM. An organization Owner can disable cloud sessions; organizations with
  Zero Data Retention cannot use cloud session features; organizations with IP allowlisting see every cloud
  session fail with an authentication error unless Anthropic-hosted services are exempted. Not available on
  Amazon Bedrock, Google Cloud's Agent Platform, or Microsoft Foundry.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Product overview, research-preview eligibility, expiry/reclaim behavior, rate limits, org disable, ZDR/IP-allowlist and provider restrictions.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: VM image (Ubuntu 24.04), fresh-clone model, and resource limits (4 vCPU / 16 GB / 30 GB).
```

### `claude/cloud-web/github-authentication`

```yaml
id: claude/cloud-web/github-authentication
provider: claude
family: cloud-web
name: GitHub authentication for cloud sessions
what: Cloud sessions reach GitHub repositories either through the Claude GitHub App (authorized during web onboarding) or by syncing the local gh CLI token via the /web-setup command. Either grants a session access to any repository the connected GitHub account can see.
surfaces: [cloud-web, cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Two methods. GitHub App: authorize the Claude GitHub App during web onboarding; this is required for Auto-fix
  (it receives PR webhooks) but is not a session-level access control. `/web-setup`: run in the terminal to sync
  the local `gh` CLI token to the Claude account; best for individuals already using `gh`. With either method a
  session can access any repository the connecting account can see, not just repos the App is installed on;
  restricting reachable repositories requires restricting access on GitHub itself. `/schedule` prompts for
  `/web-setup` if neither form of access is configured. Team/Enterprise Owners can disable `/web-setup` with the
  "Quick web setup" toggle at claude.ai/admin-settings/claude-code. Organizations with Zero Data Retention
  cannot use `/web-setup` or other cloud session features. Repository cloning and PR creation require GitHub;
  self-hosted GitHub Enterprise Server is supported for Team/Enterprise plans; GitLab/Bitbucket/other non-GitHub
  repos can only be sent as a local bundle and cannot be pushed back to.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: The two auth methods and their trade-offs, App-not-an-access-control note, `/web-setup` admin toggle, ZDR restriction, GitHub Enterprise Server support, non-GitHub limitations.
```

### `claude/cloud-web/local-bundle-upload`

```yaml
id: claude/cloud-web/local-bundle-upload
provider: claude
family: cloud-web
name: Send local repositories to a cloud session without GitHub
what: When claude --cloud runs from a repository not connected to GitHub, Claude Code bundles the local repository and uploads it directly to the cloud session instead of cloning from a remote. Sessions created from a bundle cannot push results back unless GitHub auth is also configured.
surfaces: [cli, cloud-web]
ownership: user
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  The bundle includes full repository history across all branches plus uncommitted changes to tracked files;
  untracked files are excluded (run `git add` first). The fallback activates automatically when GitHub access
  is unavailable, and can be forced with `CCR_FORCE_BUNDLE=1` even when GitHub is connected. Limits: the
  directory must be a git repository with at least one commit; the bundle must be under 100 MB, with larger
  repositories falling back to bundling only the current branch, then to a single squashed snapshot of the
  working tree, failing only if that is still too large. Sessions created from a bundle cannot push back to a
  remote unless GitHub authentication is also configured.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Bundle contents, automatic vs forced (CCR_FORCE_BUNDLE=1) activation, size/commit limits and fallbacks, no-push constraint.
```

### `claude/cloud-web/network-access-controls`

```yaml
id: claude/cloud-web/network-access-controls
provider: claude
family: cloud-web
name: Cloud environment network access levels and allowlist
what: Each cloud environment sets one outbound network access level that governs which domains its sessions can reach. Four levels exist (None, Trusted, Full, Custom); Custom takes a user-supplied allowlist. Independent security and GitHub proxies sit in front of all outbound traffic.
surfaces: [cloud-web]
ownership: mixed
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Levels: None (no outbound access through the session network), Trusted (a fixed default allowlist of package
  registries, GitHub, cloud SDKs, and other hosts), Full (any domain), Custom (a user allowlist in the Allowed
  domains field, one domain per line, `*.` matching all subdomains, with an optional "Also include default list
  of common package managers" checkbox). Set via the Network access selector in the environment dialog. There is
  no organization-level allowlist admins can push to members' environments. Claude Code's connection to the
  Anthropic API still works at None (which may allow data to exit the VM). MCP connector traffic and GitHub
  traffic bypass the environment allowlist because they route through Anthropic's servers / a dedicated GitHub
  proxy. The GitHub proxy keeps real credentials outside the VM: the in-VM git client uses a scoped credential
  the proxy swaps for the real token; `git push` works only against the session's current working branch; GitHub
  API/release-asset requests reach only repositories attached to the session (others get 403). A separate
  security proxy carries all outbound HTTP/HTTPS traffic, providing malicious-request protection, rate
  limiting/abuse prevention, content filtering, and a DNS-level audit trail of requested hostnames. Some package
  managers (e.g. Bun) have known incompatibilities with this proxy.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: The four access levels, Allowed-domains/Custom mechanics, GitHub proxy, security proxy, full default-allowlist domain list, and Bun proxy caveat.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Security-and-isolation summary: limited-by-default network, disable option, Anthropic-API reachability at None, credential proxy.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: none
note: >
  No cloud environment network levels/allowlist. Outbound-network concerns in the engine are handled instead by

  Codex sandbox_mode (`.codex/agents/*.toml`) — a different mechanism than the cloud security/GitHub proxies.
```
