### `claude/cloud-web/session-management-sharing`

```yaml
id: claude/cloud-web/session-management-sharing
provider: claude
family: cloud-web
name: Cloud session management, review, and sharing
what: Cloud sessions appear in the sidebar at claude.ai/code where they can be reviewed (diffs with inline comments), shared with teammates or publicly, archived, and permanently deleted. Cloud sessions support most built-in text-output commands, with terminal-only pickers behaving differently.
surfaces: [cloud-web]
ownership: mixed
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Each session shows a diff indicator (e.g. +42 -18) opening a diff view for inline comments sent to Claude with
  the next message. Sharing visibility depends on account type: Enterprise/Team offer Private and Team (Team =
  visible to the claude.ai organization; Claude-in-Slack sessions default to Team), with repository-access
  verification on by default; Max/Pro offer Private and Public (Public = visible to any logged-in claude.ai
  user), with repo-access verification off by default. Sharing settings (require repo access, hide name) live at
  Settings > Claude Code > Sharing settings. Archive hides a session from the default list (still viewable by
  filtering); delete permanently removes the session and its data (irreversible, with confirmation). Context
  commands: `/compact` and `/context` work; `/clear` does not (start a new session instead); auto-compaction runs
  automatically and can be tuned with `CLAUDE_AUTOCOMPACT_PCT_OVERRIDE` / `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW`.
  Terminal-only commands like `/plugin` and `/resume` are unavailable; `/model`, `/effort`, `/fast`, `/color`,
  `/rename` take a value as an argument (v2.1.205+); `/config` on the web opens the settings section instead of
  setting a value. Subagents work as locally (repo `.claude/agents/` picked up automatically); agent teams are
  off by default and enabled via `CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1`. Commits Claude creates carry a
  `Claude-Session: <url>` git trailer and PR bodies include the session URL (v2.1.179+), suppressible via
  `attribution.sessionUrl=false` (v2.1.182+); the session reads its own ID from `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE_SESSION_ID`.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Sidebar review/diff, share visibility by plan tier and repo-access verification defaults, archive/delete, command availability in cloud, session-link trailer/attribution setting.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE_SESSION_ID transcript-URL derivation and session-link usage.
```

### `claude/cloud-web/setup-scripts-and-caching`

```yaml
id: claude/cloud-web/setup-scripts-and-caching
provider: claude
family: cloud-web
name: Setup scripts and environment caching
what: A per-environment Bash setup script runs when a new cloud session starts, before Claude Code launches, to install dependencies or provision tools not pre-installed. Anthropic snapshots the resulting filesystem and reuses it as a cache so later sessions skip the script.
surfaces: [cloud-web]
ownership: mixed
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  The setup script is entered in the Setup script field of the environment dialog and runs as root on Ubuntu
  24.04. Constraints: it must exit zero (non-zero fails session start; `|| true` suggested for non-critical
  commands), should finish within roughly five minutes, and needs network access appropriate to the environment
  level for installs. After it completes, Anthropic snapshots the filesystem and reuses the snapshot for later
  sessions, which then skip the script; the cache keeps files/packages/pulled Docker images but not running
  processes. The script re-runs to rebuild the cache when the setup script or allowed network hosts change and
  when the cache expires after roughly seven days; resuming an existing session never re-runs it. Setup scripts
  are distinct from SessionStart hooks: setup scripts are cloud-only, run before Claude Code launches, and
  benefit from caching; SessionStart hooks (from repo `.claude/settings.json` or server-managed settings) run
  after launch on every session local and cloud, and can be scoped to cloud only by checking the
  `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE=true` environment variable. Sessions include pre-installed toolchains (Python, Node via
  nvm, Ruby, PHP, Java, Go, Rust, C/C++, Docker, PostgreSQL 16, Redis 7.0, plus utilities); services and
  packages outside that list are started/installed via the script or by asking Claude mid-session (mid-session
  installs do not carry to other sessions). Users get no shell into the VM; Claude runs all VM commands.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/cloud-environments
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Setup-script field/constraints, environment caching lifecycle (~5 min build, ~7 day expiry, rebuild triggers), setup-script-vs-SessionStart-hook table, installed-tools list, no-shell model.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  No cloud setup script or environment cache. The engine's session-provisioning is the LOCAL analog the record
  explicitly contrasts against: SessionStart hooks wired in
  /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.claude/settings.json (boot.py ground/brief, memory/backup_vault,
  projects_sync, telemetry) per the surface inventory §4, run after Claude Code launches on every session.
note: >
  The record's own setup-script-vs-SessionStart-hook distinction (including the CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE=true cloud
  scoping) is the relevant native feature; the engine lives entirely on the SessionStart-hook side and uses no
  cloud-only setup script or filesystem-snapshot caching.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — Cloud sessions/environments/auth/bundles/sharing are unused; the engine's off-machine story is scheduling + CI, and its isolation is local worktrees.

Preview working directory is the selected folder, so subfolders with their own servers aren't auto-detected. When autoPort reassigns, the chosen port is passed via the PORT env var."

### `claude/desktop/code-tab-parallel-sessions`

```yaml
id: claude/desktop/code-tab-parallel-sessions
provider: claude
family: desktop
name: Code tab sessions with Git worktree isolation
what: The Claude Desktop app's Code tab runs software-development sessions, each with its own chat history, project folder, and code changes, listed in a sidebar and runnable in parallel. For Git repositories each session gets an isolated copy of the project via Git worktrees so changes in one session don't affect others until committed.
surfaces: [desktop]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: "+ New session" or Cmd+N (Ctrl+N Windows); cycle with Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+Shift+Tab or Cmd+Shift+]/[; split view by Cmd/Ctrl-clicking a second sidebar session; close focused pane Cmd+\\ (Ctrl+\\). Worktrees stored at <project-root>/.claude/worktrees/ by default, changeable via Settings → Claude Code "Worktree location"; an optional branch prefix is prepended to worktree branch names. A .worktreeinclude file in the project root copies gitignored files (e.g. .env) into new worktrees. Sidebar controls filter by status/project/environment and group by project; hover-archive icon removes a worktree; "Auto-archive after PR merge or close" archives finished local sessions automatically. Requires Git installed (mandatory on Windows for the Code tab). Desktop sends an OS notification when a session finishes and it isn't the active view.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/desktop
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Manage sessions / Work in parallel with sessions; sidebar, worktree paths, settings keys, notifications"

Fixed per-category access tiers: View only (browsers, trading platforms), Click only (terminals, IDEs), Full control (everything else). Settings → General also configures 'Denied apps' and 'Unhide apps when Claude finishes'. Runs on the real desktop with prompt-injection flagging; distinct trust boundary from the sandboxed Bash tool."

### `claude/desktop/enterprise-managed-settings`

```yaml
id: claude/desktop/enterprise-managed-settings
provider: claude
family: desktop
name: Enterprise admin controls and managed settings (Desktop)
what: Team/Enterprise organizations manage desktop behavior through admin-console controls and managed settings files that override project and user settings for Desktop Claude Code sessions. Admin-console toggles include Code in the desktop, Code in the web, Remote Control, and Disable Bypass permissions mode.
surfaces: [desktop]
ownership: org
persistence: local-config
mechanics: "Managed keys include permissions.disableBypassPermissionsMode, disableAutoMode, autoMode (classifier config), browserExternalPageTools, disableBrowserExternalNavigation (JSON boolean true only), disableMobileSimulatorTools, sshConfigs, sshHostAllowlist, and managedMcpServers (3P deployments only, per-server transport + optional toolPolicy). Which managed settings reach a session depends on where it runs: local sessions read on-disk managed files (and remotely-pushed ones when authenticated with an org login/API key); cloud sessions receive server-managed settings only; SSH sessions read the remote host's managed file. Model restrictions (availableModels) are enforced as in the CLI. From v2.1.207 autoMode is read from user settings, --settings, and managed settings but not from repo-local .claude/settings.json / settings.local.json."

```yaml
id: claude/desktop/environment-configuration
provider: claude
family: desktop
name: Environment configuration and local environment editor
what: The environment picked when starting a session (Local, Cloud, SSH, WSL) determines where Claude executes. Because the desktop app does not always inherit the full shell environment, a "local environment editor" lets users store environment variables (encrypted on-device) that apply to every local session and preview server.
surfaces: [desktop]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: "On macOS, launching from Dock/Finder reads the shell profile only for PATH and a fixed set of Claude Code variables; on Windows it inherits user/system vars but not PowerShell profiles. Open the editor via the environment dropdown → hover Local → gear icon. Variables in ~/.claude.json env reach Claude sessions but not dev servers. MAX_THINKING_TOKENS=0 disables extended thinking (no effect on Fable 5; ignored on adaptive-reasoning models); CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADAPTIVE_THINKING=1 forces a fixed budget on Opus 4.6/Sonnet 4.6 only."

### `claude/desktop/permission-modes`

```yaml
id: claude/desktop/permission-modes
provider: claude
family: desktop
name: Permission modes (Code tab)
what: The Code tab exposes permission modes that govern how much autonomy Claude has (whether it asks before editing files, running commands, or both). Manual, Accept edits, Plan, Auto, and Bypass permissions are selectable from a mode selector next to the send button and can be switched mid-session.
surfaces: [desktop, cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: "Modes map to settings keys default (Manual), acceptEdits, plan, auto, bypassPermissions. Default for new local sessions set via permissions.defaultMode in settings files (same files the CLI reads); a mode picked in the selector is remembered per folder and overrides defaultMode for that folder, except Plan which is current-session only. Cmd+Shift+M opens the mode menu. Auto mode requires Opus 4.6+/Sonnet 4.6+/Fable 5 and can be disabled org-wide via disableAutoMode. Bypass permissions is gated by a Settings toggle on Pro/Max ('Allow bypass permissions mode') or org policy on Team/Enterprise; still honors explicit ask rules, requiresUserInteraction MCP tools, safety classifiers, and always-ask desktop actions. Cloud sessions support only Accept edits/Plan/Auto; the CLI-only dontAsk mode is unavailable in Desktop."
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/desktop
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Choose a permission mode table, auto-mode availability, bypass gating, CLI comparison"
```

## Family: github-ci

### `claude/github-ci/action-auth-modes`

```yaml
id: claude/github-ci/action-auth-modes
provider: claude
family: github-ci
name: Claude Code Action authentication modes
what: The GitHub Action authenticates to Claude through one of several providers, chosen independently of the workflow logic. Supported modes are Anthropic direct API (API key or workload identity federation), Claude Code OAuth token, Amazon Bedrock, Google Vertex AI, and Microsoft Foundry.
surfaces: [github-ci]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Direct API: `anthropic_api_key` (stored as the `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` repository secret) or
  `claude_code_oauth_token`. Workload identity federation (keyless, direct API) uses
  `anthropic_federation_rule_id` (fdrl_...), `anthropic_organization_id`, `anthropic_service_account_id`
  (svac_...), `anthropic_workspace_id` (wrkspc_...), and `anthropic_oidc_audience`. Third-party clouds
  use GitHub OIDC (no static keys): `use_bedrock: "true"` (with an IAM role via `AWS_ROLE_TO_ASSUME`,
  GitHub configured as an OIDC identity provider, model IDs carrying a region prefix like
  `us.anthropic.claude-sonnet-4-6`), `use_vertex: "true"` (Workload Identity Federation via
  `GCP_WORKLOAD_IDENTITY_PROVIDER` + `GCP_SERVICE_ACCOUNT`, project auto-derived), and
  `use_foundry: "true"` (Microsoft Foundry with OIDC). Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry setups typically pair with
  a custom GitHub App (APP_ID + APP_PRIVATE_KEY) rather than the default GITHUB_TOKEN. The
  `/install-github-app` quickstart is direct-API-only; Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry require manual setup.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/github-actions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Bedrock and Vertex OIDC setup steps, required secrets, model ID formats, quickstart being direct-API-only.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/main/action.yml
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Verbatim auth inputs incl. use_foundry, claude_code_oauth_token, and the anthropic_federation_* / anthropic_oidc_audience workload-identity inputs.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/main/README.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: README lists the four provider families: Anthropic direct API (key or workload identity federation), Amazon Bedrock, Google Vertex AI, Microsoft Foundry.
```

### `claude/github-ci/claude-code-action`

```yaml
id: claude/github-ci/claude-code-action
provider: claude
family: github-ci
name: Claude Code Action (anthropics/claude-code-action)
what: A GitHub Action that runs Claude Code inside GitHub Actions workflows. In interactive use it responds to @claude mentions in issue and PR comments to analyze code, answer questions, implement changes, and open pull requests; in automation use it runs immediately from a supplied prompt on any GitHub event. As of v1.0 the action auto-detects interactive vs automation behavior from the workflow context rather than a manual mode setting.
surfaces: [github-ci]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Referenced as `anthropics/claude-code-action@v1`. Core inputs (from action.yml, retrieved this run):
  `prompt` (instructions; when omitted for comment events the action responds to the trigger phrase),
  `claude_args` (arbitrary Claude Code CLI args, e.g. `--max-turns`, `--model`, `--mcp-config`,
  `--allowedTools`/`--allowed-tools`, `--disallowedTools`, `--append-system-prompt`, `--debug`),
  `github_token`, `settings` (Claude Code settings JSON string or path), `plugins` and
  `plugin_marketplaces` (newline-separated; install plugins before execution — e.g. the `code-review`
  plugin), `track_progress` (force tag mode with tracking comments), `use_sticky_comment`,
  `classify_inline_comments`, `include_fix_links`, `display_report`, `show_full_output`,
  `path_to_claude_code_executable`, `path_to_bun_executable`, `bot_id`/`bot_name`.
  Branch/PR mechanics: `base_branch`, `branch_prefix` (default `claude/`), `branch_name_template`.
  Outputs: `execution_file`, `branch_name`, `github_token`, `structured_output` (validated JSON),
  `session_id` (usable with --resume). The v1.0 upgrade removed the beta `mode:` input and renamed
  `direct_prompt`→`prompt`; `custom_instructions`/`max_turns`/`model`/`allowed_tools`/`disallowed_tools`
  are now passed via `claude_args`, and `claude_env` moved into the `settings` JSON. The action is built
  on the Claude Agent SDK. `additional_permissions` requests extra GitHub permissions (e.g. actions:read
  to view CI results). Runs on the user's GitHub-hosted runners; consumes GitHub Actions minutes plus API
  tokens.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/github-actions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Overview, @claude mention triggers, v1.0 auto-mode-detection, beta→v1 breaking-change table, claude_args passthrough, action parameter table, example workflows.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/main/action.yml
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Authoritative verbatim list of all action inputs and outputs; confirms allowed_tools/disallowed_tools are NOT inputs (use claude_args --allowedTools instead).
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code-action/main/docs/usage.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Confirms mode is auto-detected (the mode input is deprecated); event→behavior mapping.
```

## Family: hooks

### `claude/hooks/agent-hooks`

```yaml
id: claude/hooks/agent-hooks
provider: claude
family: hooks
name: Agent-based hooks (type "agent")
what: An agent hook spawns a subagent that can read files, search code, and use tools to verify a condition before returning a decision, for cases where verification needs the actual codebase state rather than the hook input alone. It is documented as experimental.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Configured as {"type":"agent","prompt":"...","timeout":N}. Uses the same "ok"/"reason" response
  format as prompt hooks but runs a multi-turn subagent with tool access — default timeout 60
  seconds and up to 50 tool-use turns. The "$ARGUMENTS" placeholder is replaced with the hook's
  JSON input. Explicitly marked experimental ("behavior and configuration may change"); the guide
  recommends command hooks for production workflows.
sources:

    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Agent-based hooks" section — experimental warning, subagent with tool access, 60s/50-turn defaults, ok/reason format, $ARGUMENTS.

    retrieved: 2026-08-02

```

### `claude/hooks/command-hooks`

```yaml
id: claude/hooks/command-hooks
provider: claude
family: hooks
name: Command hooks (type "command")
what: The default hook handler type runs a shell command or executable at the event. The command receives the event's JSON payload on stdin and communicates its result back through stdout, stderr, and its exit code.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Configured as {"type":"command","command":"..."} inside an event's hooks array. Documented
  fields include "command" (shell command or executable), optional "args" (argument list which
  switches to exec form — no shell, each arg literal — versus shell form when args is omitted,
  giving full shell interpretation of pipes/&&/globs), "timeout" in seconds (default 600;
  lowered to 30 for UserPromptSubmit and 10 for MessageDisplay per the guide), "shell"
  ("bash" or "powershell"), and async execution ("async" to run in background without blocking,
  "asyncRewake" to run in background and wake Claude on exit code 2) per the reference. The
  command reads a JSON object on stdin carrying common fields (session_id, cwd, hook_event_name)
  plus event-specific fields (e.g. tool_name, tool_input for PreToolUse; prompt for
  UserPromptSubmit; source for SessionStart). Path placeholders "$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR" /
  "${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}" (and, for plugins, "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}"/"${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}")
  resolve script locations. Scripts must be executable. Stdout is added to context for
  UserPromptSubmit/UserPromptExpansion/SessionStart; other decision behavior is via the shared
  decision-control mechanism. "CLAUDE_ENV_FILE" (written by SessionStart/CwdChanged/FileChanged
  hooks) is run as a Bash preamble; "CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP" raises the Stop-hook
  consecutive-block cap (default 8, read via stop_hook_active input field).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Read input and return output" section — stdin JSON, exit-code semantics, executable/path-placeholder guidance, CLAUDE_ENV_FILE, stop_hook_active, STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Reference command-hook fields — args/exec-vs-shell form, timeout default 600, shell, async/asyncRewake, plugin path placeholders.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  Every registered hook is type "command", shell-form (no "args" key), so it runs under sh -c /
  Git Bash. Claude form: sh "${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}/.engine/tools/hook-runner.sh" "<interp>"
  "<script>" [args] (rendered by hooks.hook_command, .engine/tools/hooks.py:153-190). The
  ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR} placeholder is used; scripts are invoked via `sh` so no executable bit is
  needed. Trailing args (hook, accept-hook, session-start, run-ambient, drain-inbox, pre-compact)
  are passed as the bare word-split tail. The stdin JSON contract is consumed by hooks.run_hook
  (.engine/tools/hooks.py:448-457) reading session_id, tool_name, tool_input, permission_mode,
  hook_event_name, and stop_hook_active (.engine/tools/hooks.py:470). The Codex variant
  (.codex/hooks.json) uses the same type "command" but resolves the project root itself via
  `cd "$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel ...)"` then codex-hook-runner.sh, because Codex has no
  project-dir token.
overlap: >
  hook-runner.sh (.engine/tools/hook-runner.sh) hand-implements a bounded wait for the venv
  interpreter and per-OS bin/python vs Scripts/python.exe resolution — a launcher wrapper the engine
  owns on top of the platform's command-hook mechanism.
note: >
  Does NOT use the documented "timeout", "shell", "async", or "asyncRewake" fields, "$CLAUDE_ENV_FILE",
  CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP override, or plugin path placeholders (${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/
  ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}). stop_hook_active is read (close/Stop path). The launcher deliberately depends
  on the documented fail-open semantics: when no venv interpreter appears it exits 1 (non-blocking) and
  never falls back to system Python (.engine/tools/hook-runner.sh, final block), and it explicitly
  avoids exit 2 because the platform reads exit-2 as a hard block.
```

**Assessment:** action mode blocks/intercepts; enforcement deterministic where the platform honors it; the engine treats it as best-effort; failure mode open; portability provider-specific behind the engine's adapter seam.

**Disposition: `CORE`** — Every engine gate and lifecycle tool is a command hook; the launcher deliberately leans on documented fail-open semantics.

### `claude/hooks/configuration-scopes`

```yaml
id: claude/hooks/configuration-scopes
provider: claude
family: hooks
name: Hook configuration scopes and precedence
what: Hooks can be declared at several scopes — user, project (committed), project-local, managed/organization policy, plugin bundles, and skill/agent frontmatter — each governing reach and shareability. Settings are watched and reloaded mid-session, and a read-only /hooks menu browses what is configured.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  The "hooks" object may live in ~/.claude/settings.json (user, all projects, not shared),
  .claude/settings.json (project, committable/team-shared), .claude/settings.local.json
  (project-local, gitignored), managed policy settings (organization-wide, admin-controlled,
  cannot be overridden), plugin hooks/hooks.json (active when the plugin is enabled), and
  skill/agent frontmatter (active while that component is). Settings precedence, highest to
  lowest: managed > command-line args > local > project > user; most keys override but permission
  rules and allowedHttpHookUrls arrays merge across scopes. Hooks-related settings keys:
  "disableAllHooks" (disables user/project/plugin hooks — managed hooks still run unless it is
  also set in managed settings), "allowManagedHooksOnly" (managed-only: load only managed, SDK,
  and force-enabled-plugin hooks), and "allowedHttpHookUrls". Files are watched and hot-reloaded
  for keys including "hooks", firing the ConfigChange event per detected change; if the watcher
  misses a change, a session restart forces reload. The read-only "/hooks" slash-command menu
  lists events with a configured-count and shows each hook's event, matcher, type, source file,
  and command; edits are made in the JSON, not the menu. A per-handler "once":true (skill
  frontmatter only) runs a hook once per session then removes it.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Configure hook location" table (six scopes + shareability), disableAllHooks behavior, /hooks menu read-only browse.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Settings precedence order, disableAllHooks / allowManagedHooksOnly / allowedHttpHookUrls keys, file-watch reload firing ConfigChange.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Reference hook-locations list, /hooks menu detail fields, and "once" (skill-frontmatter-only) field.
```

### `claude/hooks/decision-control`

```yaml
id: claude/hooks/decision-control
provider: claude
family: hooks
name: Hook decision and control output
what: The shared protocol by which a hook influences Claude Code — blocking or allowing actions, injecting context, rewriting tool input/output, and stopping the agent — via exit codes or a structured JSON object printed to stdout (or returned in an HTTP body). The available control fields vary by event.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: vendor-managed
persistence: none
mechanics: >
  Two mechanisms. Exit codes: exit 0 = no objection (stdout added to context for
  UserPromptSubmit/UserPromptExpansion/SessionStart); exit 2 = blocking error, stderr fed to
  Claude, blocks the action for blockable events (PreToolUse, PermissionRequest, UserPromptSubmit,
  UserPromptExpansion, Stop, SubagentStop, PreCompact, ConfigChange, WorktreeCreate, etc.) while
  non-blockable events (SessionStart, Setup, Notification, PostToolUse...) only surface stderr;
  any other code = non-blocking error. Structured JSON (exit 0 + stdout, do not mix with exit 2):
  universal fields "continue" (false stops Claude entirely), "stopReason", "suppressOutput",
  "systemMessage", "additionalContext", "terminalSequence". Event-specific decision fields:
  top-level "decision":"block" + "reason" for PostToolUse/Stop/SubagentStop/PostToolBatch/
  ConfigChange/PreCompact/etc.; PreToolUse uses hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecision of
  "allow"|"deny"|"ask"|"defer" (with permissionDecisionReason), plus "updatedInput" to rewrite
  tool arguments and "updatedToolOutput" (PostToolUse) to rewrite results; PermissionRequest uses
  hookSpecificOutput.decision.behavior "allow"|"deny" and may carry "updatedPermissions" entries
  such as {"type":"setMode","mode":"acceptEdits","destination":"session"}; UserPromptSubmit uses
  hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext to inject context. "allow" skips the interactive prompt but
  never overrides deny/ask permission rules (including managed deny lists); a hook "deny" blocks
  even in bypassPermissions mode. When several hooks match, the most restrictive PreToolUse answer
  wins in order deny > defer > ask > allow, and additionalContext from all hooks is concatenated.
  The "defer" value is specific to non-interactive (-p) mode for Agent SDK resumption. Stop hooks
  are overridden after 8 consecutive blocks (raise via CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Exit-code semantics, structured-JSON output, PreToolUse permissionDecision allow/deny/ask/defer, PermissionRequest decision.behavior, updatedPermissions/setMode, most-restrictive merge order, deny-precedence, 8-block cap.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Reference JSON-output schema (continue/stopReason/suppressOutput/systemMessage/additionalContext/terminalSequence, decision:block, hookSpecificOutput incl. updatedInput/updatedToolOutput) and exit-code-2-by-event table.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: continue:false halt fix (2.1.212), PermissionDenied {retry:true} retry signal, hook-matcher exact-match fixes (2.1.195, 2.1.186).
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  The engine uses a narrow slice of the control protocol, centralized in hooks._translate
  (.engine/tools/hooks.py:495-531). It uses: (a) PreToolUse hookSpecificOutput.permissionDecision of
  "allow"/"deny"/"ask" via hooks.decide (.engine/tools/hooks.py:295-298, PERMISSION_DECISIONS at
  :112) with permissionDecisionReason — this is how modes.py denies a build action in Explore
  (modes.handler -> hooks.decide("deny", reason), .engine/tools/modes.py:380/392); (b)
  hookSpecificOutput.additionalContext injection via hooks.inject (.engine/tools/hooks.py:234-238,
  :510-515) on SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, PreToolUse, and notably PostToolUse (modes'
  plan-acceptance Build-entry directive, .engine/tools/modes.py:443-453); (c) exit-2 stderr blocking
  — used ONLY by the Stop hook (close.py) via hooks.block (.engine/tools/hooks.py:228-231, :501-508).
overlap: >
  The engine wraps the raw protocol in its own decision vocabulary (proceed/block/inject/decide) and
  enforces a self-declared block budget (only PreToolUse/Stop may exit 2) at .engine/tools/hooks.py:501-508.
note: >
  Depends heavily on documented fail-open behavior: any non-2 exit lets the action proceed, and the
  engine's entire safety posture is stated to rest on this ("a crashing gate lets the action
  through", .engine/tools/modes.py:42-46; run_hook catches Exception AND SystemExit and downgrades to
  non-blocking, .engine/tools/hooks.py:474-487). Deliberately does NOT use: "updatedInput"
  (tool-arg rewriting), "updatedToolOutput" (PostToolUse result rewriting), the "defer" permission
  value (explicitly declined, .engine/tools/hooks.py:110-112), PermissionRequest decision.behavior,
  "updatedPermissions"/setMode, top-level "decision":"block" for PostToolUse, and the universal
  fields continue/stopReason/suppressOutput/systemMessage/terminalSequence. The engine depends on the
  (recently corrected) platform behavior that PostToolUse additionalContext reaches the model
  (.engine/tools/modes.py:405-409) — an inductive/version-sensitive dependence it flags itself.
  Prefers structured permissionDecision:deny over exit-2 for PreToolUse specifically because exit-2
  is read as a crash across built-in AND GitHub-MCP tools (.engine/tools/modes.py:39-46).
```

**Assessment:** action mode blocks/intercepts; enforcement deterministic where the platform honors it; the engine treats it as best-effort; failure mode open; portability provider-specific behind the engine's adapter seam.

**Disposition: `CORE`** — The deny/inject/block slice is the engine's write-gate mechanism; the unused control fields (updatedInput, defer, PermissionRequest) stay rejected for determinism.

### `claude/hooks/http-hooks`

```yaml
id: claude/hooks/http-hooks
provider: claude
family: hooks
name: HTTP hooks (type "http")
what: An HTTP hook POSTs the event JSON to a configured URL instead of running a local command, and reads the decision back from the HTTP response body using the same output format as command hooks. Useful for delegating hook logic to a web server or shared service.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Configured as {"type":"http","url":"...","headers":{...},"allowedEnvVars":[...],"timeout":N}.
  The endpoint receives the same JSON a command hook would get on stdin and returns results in
  the response body: a 2xx with JSON is parsed as decision output, 2xx with plain text is added
  as context, 2xx empty is success, and non-2xx / connection failure is a non-blocking error;
  HTTP status codes alone cannot block an action — blocking requires the appropriate
  hookSpecificOutput fields in the body. Header values support environment-variable interpolation
  with "$VAR"/"${VAR}", but only variables named in the "allowedEnvVars" array are resolved
  (others stay empty). A settings-level "allowedHttpHookUrls" allowlist (wildcard "*" supported,
  arrays merge across settings sources) governs which URLs HTTP hooks may target.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "HTTP hooks" section — POST semantics, response-body decision format, headers/allowedEnvVars interpolation.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Reference HTTP-hook response handling (2xx/non-2xx cases) and field list.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: allowedHttpHookUrls allowlist key and its cross-source array merge.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  none — all engine hooks are local type "command"; work the docs would route to a shared web service
  (e.g. telemetry -> GitHub Issues) is done inside the command hooks via telemetry.emit_finding
  (.engine/tools/hooks.py:345-359, lazy-imported off the hot path), not via an HTTP hook.
note: >
  No settings carry allowedHttpHookUrls or allowedEnvVars. The type "http" handler and its
  response-body decision protocol are entirely unexercised.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — All engine hooks are local commands; delegating gate logic to a web service adds a network dependency the control plane must not have.

### `claude/hooks/lifecycle-events`

```yaml
id: claude/hooks/lifecycle-events
provider: claude
family: hooks
name: Hook lifecycle events (and matchers)
what: Claude Code fires named events at fixed points in its lifecycle, and a hook registered on an event runs when that event occurs. Each event exposes an event-specific matcher field that narrows which occurrences trigger the hook. The event set is defined by Claude Code; users cannot add new events.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: vendor-managed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Hooks are keyed by event name under the top-level "hooks" object; each event maps to an
  array of {matcher, hooks:[...]} groups. Documented events include tool-lifecycle
  (PreToolUse, PermissionRequest, PermissionDenied, PostToolUse, PostToolUseFailure,
  PostToolBatch), prompt-lifecycle (UserPromptSubmit, UserPromptExpansion), session-lifecycle
  (SessionStart, Setup, SessionEnd, SubagentStart, SubagentStop, Stop, StopFailure,
  TeammateIdle, TaskCreated, TaskCompleted), context/config (PreCompact, PostCompact,
  ConfigChange, InstructionsLoaded, CwdChanged, DirectoryAdded, FileChanged, WorktreeCreate,
  WorktreeRemove), MCP (Elicitation, ElicitationResult), and UI (Notification, MessageDisplay).
  Matchers filter per event on an event-specific field: tool name for tool events (e.g. "Bash",
  "Edit|Write"), how the session started for SessionStart ("startup","resume","clear","compact",
  "fork"), notification type for Notification ("permission_prompt","idle_prompt","auth_success",
  "elicitation_dialog","agent_needs_input","agent_completed", etc.), reason for SessionEnd,
  compaction trigger ("manual","auto") for PreCompact/PostCompact, and so on; some events
  (UserPromptSubmit, Stop, PostToolBatch, CwdChanged, MessageDisplay) take no matcher and always
  fire. Matcher strings are exact-match for plain identifiers (letters/digits/_/-/space/comma/pipe)
  and treated as unanchored JavaScript regex otherwise; "*"/""/omitted matches all. A per-handler
  "if" field (permission-rule syntax, e.g. "Bash(git *)", "Edit(*.ts)") further filters tool
  events by tool name plus arguments and is documented as best-effort/fails-open. When multiple
  hooks match one event they run in parallel and results are merged.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Reference page — full event catalog, matcher-support column, matcher-pattern evaluation rules.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Guide "How hooks work" table (event/when-fires), matcher-filter table per event, and the "if" field rules.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Recent event additions — DirectoryAdded (2.1.219), Notification agent_needs_input/agent_completed (2.1.203), SessionStart "fork" source (2.1.203).
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  Uses 6 of the platform's events, wired identically in .claude/settings.json (Claude) and
  .codex/hooks.json (Codex): SessionStart, PreToolUse, PostToolUse, Stop, PreCompact, UserPromptSubmit.
  The closed event inventory is asserted in code at .engine/tools/hooks.py:68-77 (EVENT_INVENTORY;
  also declares SessionEnd as hooks-owned but NO SessionEnd registration exists in either settings
  file). Matchers used: SessionStart uses the string matchers "startup"/"resume"/"clear"
  (.claude/settings.json:4-... , with "clear" running a reduced subset: boot + erasure_observer +
  backup_vault only). PreToolUse/PostToolUse/Stop/PreCompact/UserPromptSubmit all use the empty
  matcher "" (Claude) or omit the matcher key entirely (Codex, e.g. .codex/hooks.json PreToolUse
  block has no "matcher"). The engine does NOT use the per-event tool-name matcher for PreToolUse
  (e.g. "Bash", "Edit|Write") — it registers one broad handler (modes.py etc.) and classifies the
  tool in-code (modes.is_building_action, .engine/tools/modes.py:259-272). The per-handler "if"
  permission-rule field is not used anywhere.
overlap: >
  The engine maintains its own event catalog + block-budget as data (hooks.EVENT_INVENTORY,
  BLOCK_ELIGIBLE_EVENTS at .engine/tools/hooks.py:68-82) and a static coherence leg
  (validate.block_budget_findings) that re-enforces "only PreToolUse/Stop may block" — duplicating a
  platform-side constraint in engine code.
note: >
  Many documented events are deliberately declined and unused: PermissionRequest, PermissionDenied,
  PostToolBatch, PostToolUseFailure, SubagentStart/Stop, Setup, Stop/StopFailure, TeammateIdle,
  TaskCreated/Completed, PostCompact, ConfigChange, InstructionsLoaded, CwdChanged, DirectoryAdded,
  FileChanged, WorktreeCreate/Remove, Elicitation/ElicitationResult, Notification, MessageDisplay,
  UserPromptExpansion. hooks.py:52 acknowledges the platform set is larger. No use of the
  Notification matcher, SessionStart "fork" source, or SessionEnd (declared owner but unwired).
```

**Assessment:** action mode blocks/intercepts; enforcement deterministic where the platform honors it; the engine treats it as best-effort; failure mode open; portability provider-specific behind the engine's adapter seam.

**Disposition: `CORE`** — The engine's control plane rides these events (6 of the documented set, both runtimes); the event vocabulary is a vendor-neutral contract the engine already treats as one (hooks.py EVENT_INVENTORY).

### `claude/hooks/mcp-tool-hooks`

```yaml
id: claude/hooks/mcp-tool-hooks
provider: claude
family: hooks
name: MCP tool hooks (type "mcp_tool")
what: An MCP tool hook invokes a tool on an already-connected MCP server as the hook action, rather than running a shell command or calling a URL.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Configured as {"type":"mcp_tool","server":"...","tool":"...","input":{...}}. "server" names a
  configured MCP server (or a plugin-scoped name of the form plugin:plugin-name:server-name),
  "tool" is the tool on that server, and "input" supplies the tool arguments with "${path}"
  substitution drawn from the hook's input JSON (e.g. "${tool_input.file_path}"). Shares the
  standard 10-minute (600s) default timeout with command and HTTP hooks. This is distinct from
  matching MCP tools as hook targets (the mcp__<server>__<tool> matcher convention), which is
  part of the event/matcher mechanism.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Reference MCP-tool-hook fields — server/tool/input and ${path} substitution.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Guide lists type "mcp_tool" as one of the handler types (calls a tool on an already-connected MCP server).
```

note: >

### `claude/hooks/prompt-hooks`

```yaml
id: claude/hooks/prompt-hooks
provider: claude
family: hooks
name: Prompt-based hooks (type "prompt")
what: A prompt hook sends the hook input plus a natural-language prompt to a Claude model for a single-turn yes/no judgment instead of running deterministic code. It is intended for decisions that require judgment rather than fixed rules.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Configured as {"type":"prompt","prompt":"...","model":"...","timeout":N}. The model (Haiku by
  default, overridable via "model") returns a JSON decision: {"ok":true} lets the action proceed;
  {"ok":false,"reason":"..."} takes an event-specific effect — for Stop/SubagentStop the reason
  is fed back so Claude keeps working; for PreToolUse the tool call is denied (by default the
  turn ends with the reason as a warning line, or "continueOnBlock":true returns the reason to
  Claude as the tool error so it continues); for PostToolUse similar with continueOnBlock; for
  PostToolBatch/UserPromptSubmit/UserPromptExpansion the turn ends with the reason shown. The
  "$ARGUMENTS" placeholder in the prompt is replaced with the hook's JSON input. Default timeout
  30 seconds. Output handling differs from command hooks (ok/reason rather than exit codes /
  hookSpecificOutput).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks-guide
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Prompt-based hooks" section — ok/reason JSON, default Haiku, model override, per-event effect of ok:false, continueOnBlock.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Reference prompt-hook fields — prompt/$ARGUMENTS placeholder, model, 30s timeout.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  none — the engine deliberately uses deterministic command hooks for gating (modes.py write-gate).
note: >
  hooks.py's fail-open harness is built for exit-code / structured-stdout command hooks only; the
  ok/reason JSON protocol of prompt hooks is not implemented anywhere.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — Judgment-based gating would weaken the engine's deterministic, fail-safe-by-policy gate; deterministic command hooks own this ground.


## Family: ide

### `claude/ide/built-in-ide-mcp-server`

```yaml
id: claude/ide/built-in-ide-mcp-server
provider: claude
family: ide
name: Built-in IDE MCP server
what: When the VS Code extension or JetBrains plugin is active it runs a local MCP server (named ide) that the CLI connects to automatically, used to open diffs in the IDE's native viewer, read the current selection for @-mentions, pull diagnostics, and (VS Code) execute notebook cells. It is hidden from /mcp because there is nothing to configure.
surfaces: [ide]
ownership: vendor-managed
persistence: session
mechanics: "Model-visible tools: mcp__ide__getDiagnostics (read-only, language-server/inspection errors, optionally one file) in both IDEs; mcp__ide__executeCode (VS Code only, runs Python in the active Jupyter kernel, always prompts via a native Quick Pick, refuses without an active notebook / ms-toolsai.jupyter / a Python kernel). Remaining hosted tools are internal RPC filtered out before reaching the model. Transport is unencrypted loopback ws://: VS Code binds 127.0.0.1 on a random port 10000–65535; JetBrains uses an OS-assigned ephemeral port. Each activation writes a fresh auth token to ~/.claude/ide/<port>.lock (0600 in a 0700 dir; $CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/ide/ if set); the CLI presents it as the X-Claude-Code-Ide-Authorization header. JetBrains adds a 'Accept connections from all network interfaces' setting (Networking Advanced) that, when enabled, exposes the port on the local network in cleartext."
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/vs-code
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "The built-in IDE MCP server section (VS Code): tools, transport/auth, Jupyter flow"
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/jetbrains
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "The built-in IDE MCP server section (JetBrains): getDiagnostics only, no executeCode, networking setting"
```

id: claude/ide/vscode-at-mentions
provider: claude

## Family: instructions-memory

### `claude/instructions-memory/agents-md-interop`

```yaml
id: claude/instructions-memory/agents-md-interop
provider: claude
family: instructions-memory
name: AGENTS.md interoperability
what: Claude Code reads CLAUDE.md and not AGENTS.md directly, but a repository using the AGENTS.md standard for other agents can have Claude read the same instructions by importing or symlinking it.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: project
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Recommended pattern is a CLAUDE.md containing `@AGENTS.md` (loaded at session start), after
  which Claude-specific instructions can be appended below the import. A symlink
  (`ln -s AGENTS.md CLAUDE.md`) also works when no Claude-specific content is needed; on Windows
  symlinks require Administrator/Developer Mode, so the import is preferred. /init (with
  CLAUDE_CODE_NEW_INIT=1) additionally reads AGENTS.md when generating CLAUDE.md.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Claude reads CLAUDE.md not AGENTS.md; @AGENTS.md import and symlink patterns; Windows caveat; new /init reads AGENTS.md.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  Not used as documented. The engine does NOT use the recommended interop patterns (a CLAUDE.md
  containing "@AGENTS.md", or a CLAUDE.md→AGENTS.md symlink). CLAUDE.md and AGENTS.md are two
  separate regular files (ls shows both as regular files, neither a symlink), each carrying its
  own independently-worded floor managed block. instantiator.py declares them as two distinct
  halves: _ROOT_CLAUDE_REL = "CLAUDE.md" (L1282) and _ROOT_AGENTS_REL = "AGENTS.md" (L1283,
  "the Codex floor — arrival's second instruction-floor half").
overlap: >
  The two files are kept coherent by hand/tooling rather than by import/symlink. Their bodies are
  deliberately divergent per runtime (CLAUDE.md L13 "Claude Code's built-in memory"; AGENTS.md L18
  "Codex's (or ChatGPT's) built-in memory"; AGENTS.md adds Codex-only sections on /hooks approval,
  $engine-start, .codex/config.toml protection).
note: >
  Divergence from the doc's own recommendation: the platform advises a single source (import or
  symlink) so the two agents read identical instructions; the engine instead maintains two parallel
  managed-block files because it wants runtime-specific wording. This is a capability the docs offer
  (interop dedup) that the engine reimplements by hand as two separately-installed floors.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — Single-source dedup (import/symlink) is deliberately declined: the two floors carry runtime-specific wording by design; coherence is tooling-enforced.

### `claude/instructions-memory/auto-memory`

```yaml
id: claude/instructions-memory/auto-memory
provider: claude
family: instructions-memory
name: Auto memory
what: A system in which Claude writes notes to itself across sessions (build commands, debugging insights, architecture notes, preferences) based on the user's corrections, without the user authoring anything. On by default.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Stored per project at ~/.claude/projects/<project>/memory/ (project path derived from the git
  repo, so all worktrees/subdirs of one repo share it; outside a repo the project root is used).
  Machine-local, not shared across machines or cloud environments. Contains a MEMORY.md index plus
  optional topic files (e.g. debugging.md). Only the first 200 lines or 25KB of MEMORY.md
  (whichever comes first) load at session start; topic files load on demand. Frontmatter and
  block-level HTML comments are stripped before measuring the limit (v2.1.211); a write that leaves
  the index over-limit returns an error telling Claude to rewrite it (v2.1.210). Files beginning
  with YAML frontmatter get a `modified` ISO-8601 timestamp on write (v2.1.214). Toggle via the
  /memory auto-memory toggle (saves autoMemoryEnabled to ~/.claude/settings.json), per-project
  `autoMemoryEnabled:false`, or env var CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY=1. Storage location
  overridable via `autoMemoryDirectory` (absolute or ~/ path; from project/local settings only after
  the workspace trust dialog). Natural-language requests like "remember that ..." are saved here.
  Subagents can have their own separate auto-memory directory; the main conversation's auto memory
  is not loaded into subagents except a fork.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Storage path/derivation, machine-local scope, MEMORY.md + topic files, 200-line/25KB load limit, frontmatter/comment stripping, over-limit error, modified timestamp, toggles, remember verb, subagent memory.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: autoMemoryEnabled default true, autoMemoryDirectory accepted formats and workspace-trust gating.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  Unused as a project record, and actively disavowed. The engine ships a complete hand-built
  memory substrate that replaces native auto-memory: .engine/memory/ (ledger.ndjson,
  index.sqlite3, vectors.sqlite3, backup-vault + capture/consolidation state) driven by
  ~20 modules under .engine/tools/memory/ (capture.py, recall.py, compact.py, erase.py, pins.py,
  ledger.py, index.py, mcp_server.py, semantic/) and surfaced through the engine-memory MCP
  server. CLAUDE.md L13-14 states Claude Code's built-in memory "is **not** this project's record
  and must never be cited as fact about the project"; conduct/defaults.md L84 repeats it.
overlap: >
  Direct, and it goes further than disuse — the engine's write-gate actively recognizes and
  guards the native notebook. .engine/tools/modes.py is_memory_target/_is_memory_path (L327-361)
  detects writes to "the harness auto-memory notebook (the ~/.claude/.../memory/ default shape)"
  by path SHAPE (a `memory` dir nested under a `.claude` dir) and routes a "remember this" request
  to the engine's own pin verb with a memory-specific denial relay (_MEMORY_DENIAL, L323-324)
  rather than letting the write land in the native store. Native compaction re-injection is
  likewise shadowed by the engine's own PreCompact hook (.engine/tools/memory/compact.py
  pre-compact, settings.json L133-142).
note: >
  This is the single largest native-vs-hand-built overlap in the group: an entire committed
  memory system (recall-by-meaning, recall-window, search, pins, withhold, erasure, backup vault)
  reimplements — and is declared authoritative over — the platform's on-by-default auto-memory.
  The engine does not toggle autoMemoryEnabled off; it leaves the native store in place but
  denies writes to it and instructs every runtime never to trust it.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — The engine ships its own authoritative memory substrate and actively fences the native notebook (modes.py denies writes; CLAUDE.md declares it non-authoritative). Recommended to stand as-is (ratification rides the migration decision record).

### `claude/instructions-memory/claude-md-excludes`

```yaml
id: claude/instructions-memory/claude-md-excludes
provider: claude
family: instructions-memory
name: claudeMdExcludes (memory-file exclusion)
what: A setting that skips specific CLAUDE.md files by path or glob when loading memory, used chiefly in large monorepos to ignore unrelated ancestor CLAUDE.md files.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  `claudeMdExcludes` takes glob patterns or absolute paths matched against absolute file paths.
  Configurable at any settings layer (user, project, local, managed); arrays merge across layers.
  Commonly placed in .claude/settings.local.json to keep the exclusion machine-local. Applies only to
  user/project/local memory — managed policy CLAUDE.md files cannot be excluded. Separately, the
  --add-dir flag does not load additional directories' memory by default; setting
  CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1 loads CLAUDE.md, .claude/CLAUDE.md, .claude/rules/*.md,
  and CLAUDE.local.md from those directories.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: claudeMdExcludes purpose, glob/absolute matching, settings-layer merge, managed exemption, additional-directories env var.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: claudeMdExcludes description and scope restriction to user/project/local memory.
```

### `claude/instructions-memory/claude-md-imports`

```yaml
id: claude/instructions-memory/claude-md-imports
provider: claude
family: instructions-memory
name: CLAUDE.md @-imports
what: An import syntax that pulls additional files into a CLAUDE.md so they are expanded and loaded into context at launch alongside the referencing file.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Uses `@path/to/import` syntax anywhere in a CLAUDE.md. Both relative (resolved relative to
  the containing file, not the cwd) and absolute paths are allowed, including home-directory
  paths like @~/.claude/my-project-instructions.md. Imports recurse with a maximum depth of
  four hops. Import parsing skips Markdown code spans and fenced code blocks, so `` `@README` ``
  in backticks stays literal while `@README` imports. Imported files still load into context at
  launch (organization, not context savings). An import in a project-level file that resolves
  outside the working directory is "external" and triggers a one-time approval dialog listing
  the files the first time it is encountered; declining disables them permanently and suppresses
  the dialog. User-scope imports load without the dialog.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: @-import syntax, relative/absolute/home paths, max depth four, code-span skipping, external-import approval dialog.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  Used. CLAUDE.md L86-87 uses two @-imports — "@.engine/conduct/defaults.md" and
  "@.engine/conduct/operator.md" — to pull the codes of conduct into context at launch. Both are
  relative paths resolving inside the working directory, so they are NOT "external" imports and
  raise no approval dialog. Import depth is 1 (the two conduct files contain no further @-imports;
  grep confirmed).
overlap: >
  This is the load mechanism for .engine/conduct/*, which is otherwise the engine's own
  conduct surface (shaped by the engine-conduct skill).
note: >
  Asymmetry worth flagging: the Claude side uses the native @-import, but the Codex twin AGENTS.md
  cannot and instead hand-reimplements the same effect as a prose instruction — AGENTS.md L9-12
  ("Required reading — before anything else ... this file cannot pull them in automatically here,
  so reading them is my first act"). Same two conduct files, native import on one runtime, manual
  "go read them" on the other.
```

### `claude/instructions-memory/init-command`

```yaml
id: claude/instructions-memory/init-command
provider: claude
family: instructions-memory
name: /init command
what: A slash command that generates a starting CLAUDE.md by analyzing the codebase for build commands, test instructions, and conventions; when a CLAUDE.md already exists it suggests improvements rather than overwriting.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: none
mechanics: >
  Run /init in a session. It reads Cursor rules (.cursor/rules/ or .cursorrules) and Copilot rules
  (.github/copilot-instructions.md) and folds relevant parts into the generated CLAUDE.md. Setting
  CLAUDE_CODE_NEW_INIT=1 enables an interactive multi-phase flow that asks which artifacts to set up
  (CLAUDE.md, skills, hooks), explores the codebase with a subagent, asks follow-ups, and presents a
  reviewable proposal before writing; in this mode it also reads AGENTS.md, .devin/rules/,
  .windsurf/rules/ or .windsurfrules, and .clinerules, and its "personal" option creates a gitignored
  CLAUDE.local.md.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: /init behavior, cross-agent rule ingestion, CLAUDE_CODE_NEW_INIT multi-phase flow and additional sources.
```

overlap: >

  .engine/provisioning/ seeds (first-run-assets.json, module-catalog.json, module-surfaces.json,

  (.claude/skills/engine-setup/SKILL.md, allowed-tools: Bash(python3 .engine/tools/instantiator.py *)).
note: >
  Where /init would analyze a codebase and propose a CLAUDE.md, the engine ships a pre-authored
  floor and provisions deterministically. It does not read the cross-agent rule sources /init
  ingests (.cursor/rules, copilot-instructions.md, etc.) — none are present or consulted.
```

### `claude/instructions-memory/local-claude-md`

```yaml
id: claude/instructions-memory/local-claude-md
provider: claude
family: instructions-memory
name: Local project CLAUDE.md (CLAUDE.local.md)
what: A private, per-project instruction file for personal preferences (sandbox URLs, preferred test data) that is not checked into version control.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Stored at ./CLAUDE.local.md at the project root; intended to be added to .gitignore.
  Loads alongside CLAUDE.md and is treated the same way; within each directory it is
  appended after CLAUDE.md so personal notes are read last at that level. Because a
  gitignored CLAUDE.local.md exists only in the worktree where it was created, importing a
  file from the home directory is the documented way to share personal instructions across
  worktrees. With CLAUDE_CODE_NEW_INIT=1, /init's "personal" option creates and gitignores it.

### `claude/instructions-memory/project-rules`

```yaml
id: claude/instructions-memory/project-rules
provider: claude
family: instructions-memory
name: Project rules (.claude/rules/)
what: A directory of modular, topic-specific markdown instruction files that can be loaded unconditionally or scoped to specific file paths, keeping instructions organized and reducing context noise.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Place .md files in .claude/rules/; all .md files are discovered recursively (subdirectories
  allowed). Rules without `paths` frontmatter load at launch with the same priority as
  .claude/CLAUDE.md. Path-specific rules use YAML frontmatter `paths:` with glob patterns
  (e.g. "src/api/**/*.ts", brace expansion like "src/**/*.{ts,tsx}") and load only when Claude
  reads a matching file (matching also works through symlinked paths as of v2.1.198). A rule's
  whole paths list shares a budget of 1,000 expanded patterns / 4 MiB; over-budget patterns are
  used unexpanded. Personal user-level rules live in ~/.claude/rules/ and load before project
  rules (project rules take priority). The directory supports symlinks (circular symlinks handled).
  Project rules are skipped when `project` is excluded from --setting-sources (nested/path-scoped
  rules were incorrectly loaded before v2.1.217).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/memory
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: .claude/rules layout, recursive discovery, paths frontmatter/glob mechanics, pattern budget, user-level ~/.claude/rules precedence, symlink support, setting-sources behavior.
```

### `claude/mcp/claude-ai-connectors`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/claude-ai-connectors
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: claude.ai connectors in Claude Code
what: MCP servers (connectors) added in a claude.ai account are automatically available in Claude Code when signed in with that account. Organizations can apply per-tool controls, and connectors can be disabled client-side.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide, cloud-web]
ownership: mixed
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Connectors are configured at claude.ai/customize/connectors (Team/Enterprise: admins only) and
  appear in /mcp labeled as from claude.ai. They load only when the active authentication method
  is a claude.ai subscription login — not under ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN,
  apiKeyHelper, third-party providers (Bedrock, GCP), or a CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN from
  setup-token. Never-used connectors collapse behind "Show unused connectors" (v2.1.161). A locally
  added server pointing at the same URL takes precedence and hides the connector. Some Anthropic-
  hosted connectors (M365, Gmail, Google Calendar) can only be authenticated from claude.ai.
  Organization per-tool controls: a tool set to "ask" prompts on every call (even in
  acceptEdits/auto/bypassPermissions; denied in dontAsk; not skipped by allow rules); "blocked"
  filters the tool out before Claude sees it (enforcement requires v2.1.129+). Disable all
  connectors via disableClaudeAiConnectors:true (any-source-true; managed can't be overridden by
  project false) or ENABLE_CLAUDEAI_MCP_SERVERS=false; block individual connectors via
  deniedMcpServers by display name or URL. In Claude Code on the web, connectors arrive as
  --mcp-config entries so disableClaudeAiConnectors does not apply there.

    retrieved: 2026-08-02

### `claude/mcp/dynamic-headers`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/dynamic-headers
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: Dynamic authentication headers (headersHelper)
what: For MCP servers using auth other than OAuth (Kerberos, short-lived tokens, internal SSO), a headersHelper command generates request headers at connection time. Claude Code runs it and merges the JSON output into the connection headers.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide]
ownership: mixed
persistence: mixed
mechanics: >
  headersHelper is a field on an http/sse/ws server entry; the command (script path or inline)
  must write a JSON object of string key/value pairs to stdout, runs in a shell with a 10-second
  timeout from the session's cwd, and runs fresh on each connect/reconnect (no caching). Dynamic
  headers override static headers of the same name. On a 401/403 tool-call response the helper is
  re-run and the call retried once (v2.1.193). Claude Code sets CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_SERVER_NAME,
  CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_SERVER_URL, and (plugin servers only) CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT in the helper's env.
  Because it executes arbitrary shell commands, at project/local scope it runs only after the
  workspace trust dialog is accepted. Plugin-provided headersHelper cannot reference
  ${user_config.*} (shell-parsed) and is reported as misconfigured (v2.1.207).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: headersHelper requirements, env vars, re-run on 401/403, trust-dialog gate, plugin user_config restriction
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.207 rejection of ${user_config.*} in shell-form headersHelper
```

### `claude/mcp/env-var-expansion`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/env-var-expansion
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: Environment variable expansion in .mcp.json
what: Claude Code expands ${VAR} and ${VAR:-default} references inside MCP server configuration, letting teams share a committed .mcp.json while keeping machine-specific paths and secrets out of it.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide, cloud-web]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Syntax: ${VAR} (value of VAR) and ${VAR:-default} (VAR or fallback). Expanded in command,
  args, env, url, and headers. A referenced-but-unset variable with no default does not fail the
  load: Claude Code warns in claude mcp list output and uses the literal ${VAR} text. Plugin
  configs also substitute ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}, ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}, and ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}
  (the latter directly, without needing a default). Expansion reads Claude Code's own process
  environment, which is also why managed allowlist/denylist ${VAR} entries expand to
  user-controlled values (see managed-config record).
sources:

### `claude/mcp/lifecycle-and-reliability`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/lifecycle-and-reliability
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: MCP server lifecycle, dynamic updates, and reconnection
what: Claude Code refreshes a server's tools/prompts/resources on list_changed notifications, retries connection and capability-discovery requests on transient errors, and reports connection failures to Claude.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api, cloud-web]
ownership: vendor-managed
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  On an MCP list_changed notification Claude Code refreshes that server's tools/prompts/resources
  without reconnect; a failed refresh keeps the previously discovered set (v2.1.214). HTTP/SSE
  servers auto-reconnect with exponential backoff (up to 5 attempts, 1s doubling); initial
  connection retries up to 3 times on transient errors (v2.1.121); stdio servers are not
  auto-reconnected. Post-connect discovery requests (tools/list, prompts/list, resources/list)
  retry transient errors up to 3 times (v2.1.191); auth/4xx/timeout errors are not retried. With
  tool search enabled (default), Claude Code tells Claude which server failed and its error,
  including in ToolSearch results; without tool search, and on Bedrock/GCP Agent Platform/Microsoft
  Foundry, failed connections are not reported to Claude (v2.1.205). A server with an empty url
  shows as "not configured" and is not connected (v2.1.208). claude mcp list shows per-server
  health (Connected / Needs authentication / Failed), including HTTP status and error text
  (v2.1.219).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: list_changed refresh, reconnection backoff, capability-discovery retries, failure reporting to Claude, empty-url handling, health statuses
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.196 discovery retries, v2.1.207 empty-url not-configured, v2.1.219 HTTP status in list/get, v2.1.214 refresh-failure retention
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused (platform reporting); reimplemented by hand
overlap: >
  Both engine servers expose a content-free `health` tool as an availability probe, and answer through a
  degrade-to-git-native path (knowledge_query.with_degrade attaches a `degraded` note; memory falls back
  when the store is unreadable).
note: >
  Native feature reimplemented by hand: rather than relying on the platform reporting failed server
  connections to Claude, the engine builds its own availability/degradation signalling — the `health`
  probe tool, boot.MCP_AVAILABILITY_CHECK (a live-helper check the model runs, because "boot reads
  committed files only and cannot detect MCP routing"), and ledger_health.detect_recall_offline for the
  read-only "memory offline" notice. This is a deliberate hand-rolled analog of list_changed-refresh /
  connection-health reporting, driven by the constraint that boot cannot see MCP routing.
```

**Assessment:** action mode observes; enforcement none — signal only; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `OBSERVATION ONLY`** — The engine hand-rolls availability signalling (health probes, boot MCP check, degrade-to-git) because boot cannot see MCP routing; platform reporting is a bonus, not a dependency.

### `claude/mcp/managed-configuration`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/managed-configuration
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: Managed (enterprise) MCP configuration
what: Administrators centrally control which MCP servers run in an organization — deploying a fixed set with managed-mcp.json (exclusive control), disabling MCP entirely, or filtering configured servers with allowedMcpServers / deniedMcpServers allow/deny lists.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide]
ownership: org
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  managed-mcp.json (same format as project .mcp.json) is read from a system path: macOS
  /Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode/managed-mcp.json, Linux/WSL /etc/claude-code/
  managed-mcp.json, Windows C:\Program Files\ClaudeCode\managed-mcp.json. It is a standalone file
  (not deliverable via server-managed settings), deployed by MDM/GPO/fleet tooling. When present,
  only its servers load — users cannot add/modify/use others (including plugin servers), and
  claude mcp add fails with "enterprise MCP configuration is active and has exclusive control...".
  An empty {"mcpServers":{}} disables MCP everywhere. claude.ai connectors are suppressed unless
  allowAllClaudeAiMcps:true is set in a managed settings source (v2.1.149+; managed tiers only).
  allowedMcpServers / deniedMcpServers are lists of {serverUrl|serverCommand|serverName} entries.
  Evaluation order: merge lists (allowManagedMcpServersOnly:true keeps only the managed allowlist;
  denylist always merges from all sources) → check denylist (any match blocks, nothing overrides)
  → check allowlist (unset = all pass; remote servers must match a serverUrl entry, stdio must
  match serverCommand, with serverName counting only when no stronger entry of that type exists).
  Commands match exactly (all args in order); serverUrl/serverCommand expand ${VAR} before matching
  and URLs support * wildcards (case-insensitive host); serverName matches literally and is not a
  security control. Unset vs empty-array [] differ (empty allowlist = no servers). Blocked servers
  either error at claude mcp add or silently disappear from /mcp and claude mcp list.
  strictPluginOnlyCustomization with "mcp" restricts servers to plugins only.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/managed-mcp
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: managed-mcp.json paths/format/exclusive control, allow/deny list keys and evaluation order, wildcard/exact-match rules, allowManagedMcpServersOnly, allowAllClaudeAiMcps, user-facing errors
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.219 managed allowlist/denylist ${VAR} resolution from startup/managed-settings env
```

```

### `claude/mcp/oauth-authentication`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/oauth-authentication
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: OAuth 2.0 authentication for remote MCP servers
what: Claude Code authenticates to remote HTTP/SSE MCP servers with OAuth 2.0. A server returning 401/403 is flagged as needing sign-in; users complete the browser flow from the /mcp panel or the CLI. Supports fixed callback ports, pre-configured credentials, scope restriction, and metadata-discovery override.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Trigger sign-in via /mcp (in-session) or claude mcp login <name> / logout <name> (from
  v2.1.186; --no-browser prints the URL for SSH/headless). Tokens are stored in the system
  keychain (macOS) or a credentials file, refreshed automatically; a 401 on an already-signed-in
  server triggers one refresh+retry (v2.1.206). Discovery follows RFC 9728 protected-resource
  metadata then RFC 8414 auth-server metadata; also supports Client ID Metadata Document (CIMD).
  Config knobs live in the server's "oauth" object in .mcp.json: authServerMetadataUrl (override
  discovery, https only), scopes (space-separated string pinning requested scopes, takes
  precedence over discovery; offline_access appended automatically if advertised), clientId,
  callbackPort. CLI flags: --callback-port PORT (registers http://localhost:PORT/callback),
  --client-id, --client-secret (masked prompt; MCP_CLIENT_SECRET env var for CI). A configured
  headers.Authorization that the server rejects fails the connection rather than falling back to
  OAuth. In non-interactive mode Claude Code reports the server as needing auth rather than
  hiding its tools (v2.1.196, requires tool search). Some Anthropic-hosted connectors (M365,
  Gmail, Google Calendar) only support OAuth from claude.ai, not local Claude Code.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: OAuth flow, /mcp, login/logout, callback-port, pre-configured credentials, oauth.authServerMetadataUrl/scopes, discovery chain
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.186 login/logout, v2.1.196 scope-request fix, v2.1.203/2.1.206/2.1.216 token-refresh reliability
```

### `claude/mcp/output-limits`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/output-limits
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: MCP tool output limits and warnings
what: Claude Code caps and warns on large MCP tool output to protect the conversation context, with per-tool overrides that server authors can declare.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api, cloud-web]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  A warning fires when any MCP tool output exceeds 10,000 tokens (threshold fixed); the maximum
  defaults to 25,000 tokens and is raised via MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS. Server authors can raise a
  single tool's persist-to-disk threshold by setting _meta["anthropic/maxResultSizeChars"] in its
  tools/list entry (up to a 500,000-char ceiling), which applies to text content independently of
  MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS. Results over the threshold without the annotation are persisted to disk
  and replaced with a file reference. Image-returning tools remain subject to MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS.
  Tools declaring input schemas with a root-level anyOf/oneOf/allOf combinator are flattened so the
  Claude API accepts them (v2.1.195), with the branch semantics described in the tool description.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Output warning/limit thresholds, MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS, maxResultSizeChars annotation, root-level combinator handling
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused (platform-level); reimplemented by hand at the application level
overlap: >
  The engine sets no MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS and no _meta["anthropic/maxResultSizeChars"] annotation.
note: >
  Native feature reimplemented by hand: engine-memory's search tool imposes its OWN result bound —
  _DEFAULT_LIMIT = 10 in .engine/tools/memory/mcp_server.py (_recall), explicitly to stop a common-word
  query flooding the caller's context. This is an application-level cap standing in for what the
  platform's token-based output cap/persist-to-disk mechanism would otherwise handle.
```

**Assessment:** action mode observes; enforcement none — signal only; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `OBSERVATION ONLY`** — Platform caps apply; the engine adds its own app-level result bound (_DEFAULT_LIMIT=10) as the real control.

id: claude/mcp/plugin-provided-servers

  requires /reload-plugins (unchanged servers keep live connections, v2.1.210+). In web sessions an
  MCP call to a not-yet-connected plugin server starts it on demand (v2.1.211+). Path placeholders:
  ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT} (install dir), ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA} (persistent state), ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}
  (project root, substituted directly) — applied to stdio command/args/env and to http/sse/ws
  url/headers/headersHelper (headersHelper substitution from v2.1.195). Supports stdio, SSE, HTTP,
  WS. Plugin servers can be toggled off in /mcp without uninstalling. They can be suppressed
  entirely by managed-mcp.json exclusive control.

### `claude/mcp/server-config-and-scopes`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/server-config-and-scopes
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: MCP server configuration and installation scopes
what: Claude Code connects to external MCP servers configured at three scopes — local (default, private to you in the current project), project (shared via a checked-in .mcp.json), and user (private, across all your projects). Servers are added with claude mcp add / add-json / add-from-claude-desktop and managed with list, get, remove, reset-project-choices.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide, cloud-web, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: mixed
mechanics: >
  Local- and user-scope servers are stored in ~/.claude.json (local under the project's
  path entry, user under a top-level mcpServers key); project scope is stored in .mcp.json
  at the project root using {"mcpServers": {<name>: {...}}}. On Windows ~/.claude.json is
  %USERPROFILE%\.claude.json, or inside CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR if set. Scope precedence when a name
  is defined more than once: local > project > user > plugin-provided > claude.ai connectors;
  the entire winning entry is used (no field merge). The three scopes match duplicates by name;
  plugins/connectors match by endpoint (URL or command). Project-scoped servers require an
  approval prompt before use, recorded via enableAllProjectMcpServers / enabledMcpjsonServers /
  disabledMcpjsonServers in settings; a cloned repo cannot self-approve in an untrusted folder
  (as of v2.1.196/2.1.197). Server names added via claude mcp are restricted to letters, numbers,
  hyphens, underscores. Reserved names (workspace, claude-in-chrome, computer-use, Claude Preview,
  Claude Browser) are rejected/skipped. Disable-without-removing is per-project via
  disabledMcpServers / enabledMcpServers in ~/.claude.json.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Scopes table, storage files, scope precedence list, add/list/get/remove commands, reserved names, disable lists
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp-quickstart
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Scope-to-file table, changing scope by remove+re-add, editing .mcp.json by hand, connect-from-other-surfaces list
```
