### `claude/mcp/timeouts-and-backgrounding`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/timeouts-and-backgrounding
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: MCP timeouts and automatic backgrounding
what: Claude Code bounds how long MCP server startup and tool calls may run via configurable startup, per-call wall-clock, per-request, and idle timeouts, and automatically moves long main-conversation tool calls to a background task.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api, cloud-web]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Startup: MCP_TIMEOUT env var (ms; default ~30s). Per-tool-call wall clock: per-server "timeout"
  field (ms; overrides MCP_TOOL_TIMEOUT for that server; values <1000 ignored; default ~28h when
  MCP_TOOL_TIMEOUT unset). A second per-request timer (HTTP/SSE/claude.ai connectors) covers first
  response byte, default 60s, raised by timeout/MCP_TOOL_TIMEOUT >=60s. Idle timeout (v2.1.187+):
  aborts a call sending no response/progress within the idle window — default 5 min for HTTP/SSE/
  WS/connector, 30 min for stdio (v2.1.203); set via CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_TOOL_IDLE_TIMEOUT (ms; 0
  disables). A per-server timeout >=1000 acts as a floor on the idle timeout (v2.1.203). Auto-
  backgrounding (v2.1.212+): a main-conversation call still running after 2 minutes moves to a
  /tasks background task; threshold set by CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_AUTO_BACKGROUND_MS (0 disables;
  CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1 also disables). Not backgrounded: subagent calls, IDE
  server calls, non-interactive mode (unless CLAUDE_AUTO_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1), or calls awaiting an
  open elicitation dialog. Output token controls: warning at 10,000 tokens, default cap 25,000,
  raised by MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: MCP_TIMEOUT, per-server timeout, per-request/idle timers, auto-backgrounding rules and env vars
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.186 CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_AUTO_BACKGROUND_MS, v2.1.203 request_timeout_ms fix, v2.1.212 2-minute auto-background
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: none
note: >
  No MCP_TIMEOUT / MCP_TOOL_TIMEOUT / per-server timeout / CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_AUTO_BACKGROUND_MS /
  MAX_MCP_OUTPUT_TOKENS set in-repo; the engine servers declare no timeout field. All timeout/
  auto-backgrounding behavior is left at platform defaults.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — Remote/interactive MCP features (OAuth, dynamic headers, tool-search tuning, resources, prompts, elicitation, channels, managed config, connectors, plugin servers, timeouts tuning) are not applicable to two local stdio servers; each stays available to operators as host configuration.

### `claude/mcp/tool-permissions-and-naming`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/tool-permissions-and-naming
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: MCP tool naming and permission integration
what: MCP tools carry a structured callable name used in permission rules, allowed-tools lists, subagent tools fields, and hook matchers. Server authors can mark a tool as always requiring human approval on every call.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api, cloud-web]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  A user-configured server's tool is named mcp__<server>__<tool>. A plugin-bundled server's tool
  is mcp__plugin_<plugin-name>_<server-name>__<tool-name> (non [A-Za-z0-9_-] chars replaced with
  _); the server itself registers under plugin:<plugin-name>:<server-name>. A hook matcher written
  against the bare server key never fires for a plugin server. Server authors set
  _meta["anthropic/requiresUserInteraction"]=true (JSON boolean, v2.1.199+) to force the permission
  prompt on every call — it shows even in acceptEdits/auto/bypassPermissions modes, offers no
  "don't ask again", is not skipped by allow rules, and is denied in dontAsk mode. Under
  --permission-prompt-tool an allow for such a tool is converted to a deny; the Agent SDK
  canUseTool callback does receive and can approve it. Remote Control / one-tap surfaces withhold
  one-tap approval for these tools (v2.1.214).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Tool naming (user and plugin forms), scoped server name, requiresUserInteraction annotation and permission behavior
```

### `claude/mcp/tool-search`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/tool-search
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: MCP tool search (deferred tool loading)
what: Tool search keeps MCP tools out of the context window until Claude needs them — only tool names and server instructions load at session start, and a search tool discovers relevant tools on demand. Enabled by default; configurable per environment.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api, cloud-web]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Controlled by the ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH env var (or settings.json env): unset = all tools deferred
  (falls back to upfront loading on Google Cloud Agent Platform, non-first-party ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL,
  or Microsoft Foundry on Azure); true = force defer; auto = load upfront if within 10% of context,
  else defer; auto:N = custom threshold percent; false = load all upfront. Requires a model
  supporting tool_reference blocks (Sonnet 4.5, Haiku 4.5, Opus 4.5+). CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_
  EXPERIMENTAL_BETAS keeps it off and overrides ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH. The ToolSearch tool can be
  disabled via permissions.deny ["ToolSearch"]. Per-server alwaysLoad:true (all server types,
  v2.1.121+) or per-tool _meta "anthropic/alwaysLoad":true exempts tools from deferral and loads
  them upfront (alwaysLoad also blocks startup until connect, capped at the 5s connect timeout).
  Without tool search, Claude Code uses a WaitForMcpServers tool and does not report failed server
  connections to Claude. Descriptions and server instructions are truncated at 2KB each.

  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: ENABLE_TOOL_SEARCH value table, model compatibility, alwaysLoad, ToolSearch deny, WaitForMcpServers fallback, 2KB truncation
```


### `claude/mcp/transports`

```yaml
id: claude/mcp/transports
provider: claude
family: mcp
name: MCP transports (stdio, HTTP, SSE, WebSocket)
what: Claude Code connects to MCP servers over four transports. Local stdio servers run as subprocesses; remote HTTP (streamable-http) is the recommended transport; SSE is deprecated but still supported; WebSocket holds a persistent bidirectional connection.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide, cloud-web]
ownership: mixed
persistence: mixed
mechanics: >
  Added with claude mcp add --transport <http|sse|stdio> <name> <url|-- command args>;
  --transport/-t and --header/-H have short forms. In JSON (.mcp.json, ~/.claude.json,
  add-json) the type field selects the transport; "streamable-http" is an accepted alias for
  "http". A JSON entry with a url but no type is treated as stdio and skipped with an error
  (message changed at v2.1.202). WebSocket uses "type":"ws" with url/headers/headersHelper/
  timeout/alwaysLoad; --transport does not accept ws (add via add-json or .mcp.json), and ws
  auth is header-only. Stdio servers receive CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR in their environment (stable
  project root) and can implement MCP roots/list, which Claude Code answers with the launch
  directory plus any additional working directories; notifications/roots/list_changed is sent
  when that set changes (v2.1.203/2.1.204). The -- separator divides Claude's own flags from the
  server command for stdio. Automatic reconnection with exponential backoff (up to 5 attempts)
  for HTTP/SSE; stdio processes are not auto-reconnected.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/mcp
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Four transport options, streamable-http alias, url-without-type error, ws fields, CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR and roots/list, reconnection backoff
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.204 notifications/roots/list_changed
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  Only stdio is used. Both .mcp.json servers launch via `uv run --directory ... python tools/...`
  (subprocess/stdio); server code calls FastMCP server.run() with "stdio transport by default"
  (.engine/tools/knowledge_mcp_server.py, .engine/tools/memory/mcp_server.py). HTTP (streamable-http),
  SSE, and WebSocket are entirely unused.
overlap: none
note: >
  Docs recommend streamable-http as the transport for remote servers; the engine's servers are local
  in-repo processes, so stdio is the fitting choice — but it means none of the reconnection/backoff
  behavior (HTTP/SSE only) applies; stdio servers are not auto-reconnected.
```

**Assessment:** action mode advises; enforcement advisory; failure mode open; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `CORE`** — stdio fits local in-repo servers; remote transports have no current subject.


## Family: permissions-sandboxing

### `claude/permissions-sandboxing/auto-mode`

```yaml
id: claude/permissions-sandboxing/auto-mode
provider: claude
family: permissions-sandboxing
name: Auto mode (classifier-gated permission mode)
what: A permission mode in which a separate classifier model reviews each non-trivial action before it runs and blocks anything that escalates beyond the request, targets untrusted infrastructure, or appears driven by hostile content, instead of prompting the user. Distinct from the sandbox's auto-allow mode and can be combined with it.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide, cloud-web, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Availability gated by plan (all), provider, and model (e.g. Anthropic API/Claude Platform on
  AWS: Opus 4.6+/Sonnet 4.6+/Fable 5; Bedrock/Agent Platform/Foundry/gateway: Sonnet 5, Opus 4.7+,
  Fable 5). Set via `permissions.defaultMode: "auto"` (user/managed only; ignored in project/local)
  or `--permission-mode auto`; appears in Shift+Tab cycle when available. Decision order: allow/ask/deny
  rules resolve first (protected-path writes and org-`ask`/requiresUserInteraction tools route away
  from auto-approval), then read-only actions and working-dir edits auto-approve, then everything
  else goes to the classifier. On entering, broad code-execution allow rules (`Bash(*)`,
  `Bash(python*)`, package-run, `Agent`) are dropped and restored on exit. Classifier sees user
  messages, tool calls, and CLAUDE.md but not tool results (a server-side probe scans results
  separately); runs on Sonnet 5 by default. Ships large default block lists (curl|bash, external
  data egress, prod deploys/migrations, force push, IaC destroy, secret/credential exfil, unapproved
  PR merges, etc.) and allow lists (local file ops, lockfile installs, reads sending creds to matching
  API, read-only HTTP, pushes to any branch of the working repo). Conversation-stated boundaries act as
  block signals but are re-read from transcript each check (lost on compaction). Sandbox network requests
  route through the classifier with per-host verdict caching. Fallback to prompting after 3 consecutive
  or 20 total blocks; `-p` non-interactive aborts on repeated blocks. Org disable via
  `permissions.disableAutoMode: "disable"` in managed settings; trusted infrastructure tuned via
  `autoMode.environment` (see auto-mode-config, not fetched). Subagents are classifier-checked at spawn,
  per-action, and on return; subagent frontmatter `permissionMode` is ignored.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permission-modes
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Availability requirements, classifier decision order, default block/allow lists, fallback thresholds, subagent handling, cost/latency
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: autoMode.environment/allow/soft_deny/hard_deny and disableAutoMode keys (paraphrased; exact shapes not confirmed against canonical auto-mode-config page)
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.207 auto mode on by default on Bedrock/Vertex; v2.1.206 transcript-tamper block; v2.1.205/2.1.198/2.1.183 destructive-git and rm-rf-on-variable blocks
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  The engine's classifier-free, hand-enumerated Explore write-gate (modes.py) occupies the same
  conceptual space (deciding per-action instead of prompting) but is deterministic code, not the
  classifier-model auto mode.
note: >
  No defaultMode:"auto", no autoMode.* config, no disableAutoMode anywhere. The engine's gate has NO
  default-deny (an unclassified action resolves to allow, modes.py:259-272) — the exact opposite of
  auto mode's classifier-reviews-everything posture.
```

**Assessment:** action mode observes; enforcement none — signal only; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `OBSERVATION ONLY`** — A classifier-gated mode the engine neither requires nor forbids; its own gate has no default-deny, so auto mode is a host-side complement the engine cannot depend on.

### `claude/permissions-sandboxing/managed-policy-settings`

```yaml
id: claude/permissions-sandboxing/managed-policy-settings
provider: claude
family: permissions-sandboxing
name: Managed (enterprise) policy settings
what: Administrator-deployed settings that take highest precedence and cannot be overridden by user, project, local, or command-line settings, used to enforce permission and sandbox policy organization-wide. A set of managed-only keys are read only from managed settings.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide, sdk-api]
ownership: org
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Delivered via MDM/OS policy files, a managed-settings drop-in directory, server-managed settings
  (claude.ai), or a self-hosted Claude apps gateway. File paths: macOS
  `/Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode/managed-settings.json`; Linux/WSL
  `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json`; Windows `C:\Program Files\ClaudeCode\managed-settings.json`;
  plus `managed-settings.d/*.json` merged alphabetically. Precedence: managed > CLI args > local >
  project > user; a deny at any level cannot be re-allowed by a lower level. For boolean keys managed
  wins outright; for array keys entries merge across scopes (developers can append). Managed-only keys
  relevant here include `allowManagedPermissionRulesOnly` (only managed allow/ask/deny rules apply),
  `sandbox.filesystem.allowManagedReadPathsOnly`, `sandbox.network.allowManagedDomainsOnly`,
  `allowManagedHooksOnly`, `allowManagedMcpServersOnly`, `disableSideloadFlags`, `forceRemoteSettingsRefresh`,
  and `strictPluginOnlyCustomization`. `disableBypassPermissionsMode` / `disableAutoMode` are typically
  managed but work from any scope. Enforce sandboxing org-wide with `sandbox.enabled`,
  `sandbox.failIfUnavailable`, `sandbox.allowUnsandboxedCommands: false`; when managed settings configure
  `sandbox.filesystem` or any `sandbox.credentials.files`, only managed settings may set
  `filesystem.disabled`. `mask` credential entries, `network.tlsTerminate`, `strictAllowlist`,
  `allowAppleEvents`, and `filesystem.disabled` are honored only from user/managed/`--settings` sources,
  not repository settings. Login can be pinned org-wide with managed `forceLoginMethod`/`forceLoginOrgUUID`.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Managed settings section, managed-only settings table, settings precedence, allowManagedPermissionRulesOnly
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sandboxing
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Enforce sandboxing with managed settings; managed-only locks on filesystem.disabled, allowManagedReadPathsOnly, allowManagedDomainsOnly
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Managed settings file paths and precedence order
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/iam
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: URL redirects to the Authentication page; org managed policy for login pinning (forceLoginMethod/forceLoginOrgUUID) documented there
```

### `claude/permissions-sandboxing/permission-modes`

```yaml
id: claude/permissions-sandboxing/permission-modes
provider: claude
family: permissions-sandboxing
name: Permission modes (default/Manual, acceptEdits, plan, dontAsk, bypassPermissions)
what: Session-level modes that set the baseline for how often Claude Code pauses to ask before acting. Modes set the baseline and permission rules layer on top; deny/ask rules, org-`ask` connector tools, and requiresUserInteraction markers still apply in every mode.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide, cloud-web, github-ci, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Config value set via `permissions.defaultMode` in settings, `--permission-mode <mode>` at
  startup, or interactively (CLI Shift+Tab cycles default→acceptEdits→plan; VS Code/Desktop mode
  selectors; web/mobile dropdown). `default` (labeled "Manual", alias `manual`, v2.1.200+):
  prompts on first use of each tool, reads-only run free. `acceptEdits`: auto-approves file
  edits plus common filesystem Bash commands (`mkdir touch rm rmdir mv cp sed`, PowerShell
  Set/Add/Clear-Content, Remove-Item) for in-scope paths (working dir + additionalDirectories);
  out-of-scope and protected-path writes still prompt. `plan`: reads and read-only shell only,
  edits blocked until plan approval (approval switches the session to another mode). `dontAsk`:
  auto-denies anything not matching an allow rule / read-only set / PreToolUse-hook allow; also
  denies AskUserQuestion and org-`ask` connector tools. `bypassPermissions` (equivalent to
  `--dangerously-skip-permissions`): skips prompts and safety checks including protected-path
  writes, but explicit ask rules, org-`ask` connector tools, requiresUserInteraction MCP tools,
  and root/home removal circuit-breaker (`rm -rf /`, `rm -rf ~`, including via command/process
  substitution, v2.1.208+) still prompt; refuses to start as root/sudo; one-time acceptance
  dialog saved to user settings; cannot be entered from a session not started with it.
  `permissions.disableBypassPermissionsMode` / `permissions.disableAutoMode` set to `"disable"`
  block those modes (any scope; typically managed). Cloud-web sessions ignore checked-in
  `defaultMode: "bypassPermissions"` and `"dontAsk"`. `defaultMode: "auto"` is ignored from
  project/local settings (must be user/managed).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permission-modes
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Per-mode behavior, how each mode is set per surface, plan-approval flow, protected-path table, cloud-web exceptions
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: defaultMode settings table, disableBypassPermissionsMode / disableAutoMode locks
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.200 default→Manual relabel; v2.1.212 plan-mode file-modifying-Bash prompt fix
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  permissions.defaultMode: "plan" is set in .claude/settings.local.json. The engine also reads the
  live permission_mode from the hook payload to detect Claude's native plan mode
  (modes.is_plan_artifact keys on permission_mode == "plan", .engine/tools/modes.py:287-302) so its
  Explore write-gate exempts the plan-file write.
overlap: >
  The engine runs a wholly separate, hand-built "stance" system — explore/build/routine — in modes.py
  (.engine/tools/modes.py:75-129), stored as an OS-temp session-keyed marker, entered by typed verbs
  or plan-acceptance (accept_handler, :443-453). This is a parallel concept to Claude permission
  modes and is not the platform's mode machinery; it coexists with defaultMode:"plan".
note: >
  Does not use acceptEdits, dontAsk, bypassPermissions, or auto as configured modes. The engine's
  "routine" write-stance is guarded by its own worktree-isolation check (checkout_health.is_isolated_
  worktree, modes.py:622-632), not by any platform mode. Potential confusion surface: the engine's
  "explore" stance and Claude's "plan" defaultMode both gate writes but are independent mechanisms —
  the engine flips its stance to build on ExitPlanMode regardless of what platform mode the plan
  approval selects (modes.py:400-403).
```

**Assessment:** action mode blocks/intercepts; enforcement deterministic where the platform honors it; the engine treats it as best-effort; failure mode open; portability provider-specific behind the engine's adapter seam.

**Disposition: `HOST CONFIGURATION`** — defaultMode:plan is the one mode the engine sets; the stance system (explore/build/routine) is engine machinery layered above host modes.

### `claude/permissions-sandboxing/permission-rules`

```yaml
id: claude/permissions-sandboxing/permission-rules
provider: claude
family: permissions-sandboxing
name: Permission rules (allow / ask / deny)
what: Fine-grained rules that control which tools Claude Code may use and which files, domains, commands, or parameters each tool may act on. Rules are Claude-Code-enforced (not model-enforced) and evaluated in the fixed order deny, then ask, then allow, with the first match winning regardless of specificity.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide, cloud-web, github-ci, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Rule format is `Tool` or `Tool(specifier)`. A bare tool name (or the equivalent `Bash(*)`)
  matches all uses; as a deny rule a bare name removes the tool from Claude's context entirely
  (every tool except EndConversation, which a deny/ask rule can't remove while other tools
  remain). Tool-scoped specifiers: Bash/PowerShell support `*` glob at any position with a
  trailing `:*` equivalent to ` *`, shell-operator-aware (separators `&& || ; | |& & newline`
  each matched independently), strips a fixed wrapper set (`timeout time nice nohup stdbuf
  command builtin noglob`, bare `xargs`) and leading known-safe env assignments; a built-in
  read-only command set (`ls cat echo pwd head tail grep find wc which diff stat du cd`,
  read-only git) runs with no prompt in every mode and is not configurable. Read/Edit use
  gitignore-style path patterns with anchors `//abs`, `~/home`, `/settings-source-relative`,
  `path`/`./path` cwd-relative; Edit rules cover all file-editing tools and a Read deny also
  blocks Edit on that path; only `Edit(path)` and `Read(path)` are consulted for file checks
  (Write/NotebookEdit/Glob/MultiEdit path rules warn at startup). WebFetch uses `domain:` host
  matching. MCP uses `mcp__server` / `mcp__server__tool` (allow globs require a literal server
  prefix). Also Agent(name), Cd(path), and generic deny/ask parameter matching `Tool(param:value)`
  with `*` wildcard (primary content fields like `command`/`file_path`/`url` are not matchable).
  Rules merge across settings scopes; a deny at any scope beats an allow at any scope.
  "Yes, don't ask again" persists Bash approvals to `.claude/settings.local.json` at the git
  repo root (v2.1.211+). PreToolUse hooks can further gate calls but cannot bypass deny/ask rules.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Full rule syntax, precedence, per-tool specifiers, wrappers, read-only set, compound commands, symlink handling
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: permissions.allow/deny/ask keys and cross-scope merge behavior (key names; paraphrased examples treated with caution)
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.214 fixes to single-segment allow-rule matching and fail-closed Bash checks; v2.1.213 docker daemon-flag prompts; v2.1.210 startup warning for non-consulted path rules
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  Only permissions.allow is used, in .claude/settings.local.json (project-local, gitignored-shape) —
  a large (102-entry) allowlist of Bash(...) specifiers (gh pr/run/api/issue, git fetch/ls-remote/
  grep/rg/awk/python3 one-liners, uv run *) and bare MCP rules (mcp__engine-memory__search,
  mcp__engine-memory__recall-by-meaning/recall-window, mcp__engine-knowledge-graph__get-entity/
  neighbors). Read(...) rules for //Users/shanekidd/** paths also appear. These are accumulated
  "Yes, don't ask again" grants plus MCP approvals. No deny or ask rules exist in any settings file;
  .claude/settings.json carries hooks only (no permissions block).
overlap: >
  The engine's actual write-blocking is NOT done with permission deny/ask rules — it is a hand-built
  PreToolUse hook, modes.py (.engine/tools/modes.py:366-393), returning permissionDecision:deny for an
  enumerated build set (_MUTATING_TOOLS, _BASH_BUILD_PATTERNS, _MCP_PR_TOOL at :232-256). This
  reimplements, in engine code, the tool/parameter matching that native deny rules provide.
note: >
  The engine DELIBERATELY refuses to allow-list a gated tool because an allow rule outranks its hook
  deny (stated at .engine/tools/modes.py:44-46) — so its own gate depends on NOT using the native
  precedence (deny>ask>allow) that would otherwise make its job trivial and unbypassable. The docs
  note a PreToolUse hook cannot bypass deny/ask rules; the engine has no such rules, so its
  best-effort shell-string classification (evadable by alias/eval/substitution, admitted at
  modes.py:239-245) is the only pre-merge write barrier, with the protected-branch merge as the sole
  hard backstop.
```

**Assessment:** action mode blocks/intercepts; enforcement deterministic where the platform honors it; the engine treats it as best-effort; failure mode open; portability provider-specific behind the engine's adapter seam.

**Disposition: `HOST CONFIGURATION`** — The allowlist is accumulated operator grants (settings.local.json); the engine deliberately builds its own deny gate instead of native deny rules (an allow rule would outrank its hook) — native rules remain the operator's surface, not the engine's.

### `claude/permissions-sandboxing/protected-paths`

```yaml
id: claude/permissions-sandboxing/protected-paths
provider: claude
family: permissions-sandboxing
name: Protected paths
what: A fixed set of repository-state and Claude-configuration paths whose writes are never auto-approved, guarding against accidental corruption. The protection is independent of and runs before permissions.allow rules, so an allow rule cannot pre-approve these writes.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide, cloud-web, sdk-api]
ownership: vendor-managed
persistence: none
mechanics: >
  Per-mode outcome: default/acceptEdits prompt; plan prompts (allowed only when bypass permissions
  available, else routed to classifier when auto mode available during planning); auto routes to
  classifier; dontAsk denies; bypassPermissions allows (before v2.1.126 it prompted). The safety
  check runs before settings allow rules are evaluated, so `Edit(.claude/**)` in settings does not
  change the outcome; in prompting modes a `.claude/` write offers a "Yes, and allow Claude to edit
  its own settings for this session" option. Protected directories include `.git`, `.config/git`,
  `.vscode`, `.idea`, `.husky`, `.cargo`, `.devcontainer`, `.yarn`, `.mvn`, and `.claude` (except
  `.claude/worktrees`). Protected files include `.gitconfig`, `.gitmodules`, shell rc/profile files,
  package-manager configs (`.npmrc`, `.yarnrc*`, `.pnp.*`, `bunfig.toml`, etc.), Bazel configs,
  pre-commit/lefthook configs, wrapper property files, `.devcontainer.json`, `.ripgreprc`,
  `pyrightconfig.json`, `.mcp.json`, and `.claude.json`. The sandbox separately denies writes to
  Claude Code's settings.json files at every scope and to the managed-settings directory (with
  symlink-target resolution, v2.1.210+).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permission-modes
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Protected-path per-mode table and the full protected directory/file lists
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sandboxing
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Sandbox-side deny of settings files and managed-settings directory
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  The engine's own gate independently keeps .claude/ writes denied in Explore (every ~/.claude/ write
  carries no plan-marker so stays denied, modes.py:283-286 / describe_explore_scope), overlapping the
  vendor protected-path guard on .claude specifically.
note: >
  Nothing in the repo configures or toggles protected paths (they are vendor-managed and always-on).
  The engine writes into .claude/ (settings.json, hooks) only through provisioning/wiring, not runtime
  hook actions, so it does not lean on the protected-path prompt as a runtime control.
```

### `claude/permissions-sandboxing/sandbox-credentials`

```yaml
id: claude/permissions-sandboxing/sandbox-credentials
provider: claude
family: permissions-sandboxing
name: Sandbox credential protection (sandbox.credentials)
what: An opt-in sandbox setting that declares credential files and environment variables to protect from sandboxed Bash commands, since the sandbox's default read policy still exposes credential files and inherited env vars. Files can be denied and env vars can be denied or masked.
surfaces: [cli, ide, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  `sandbox.credentials.files` entries (`{ "path", "mode": "deny" }`) block reads inside the sandbox
  (same as filesystem.denyRead; ignored if filesystem isolation is disabled). `sandbox.credentials.envVars`
  entries support `"mode": "deny"` (variable unset before each sandboxed command; still enforced even with
  filesystem isolation off) and `"mode": "mask"` (v2.1.199+): the command sees a per-session sentinel and
  the sandbox proxy substitutes the real value only on requests to the credential's `injectHosts` (each of
  which must be within `network.allowedDomains`), requiring `network.tlsTerminate` so the proxy can rewrite
  request contents. There is no built-in credential deny list — only listed entries are protected; `deny`
  entries narrow access and merge from every scope, while `mask` (and tlsTerminate/allowPlaintextInject)
  are honored only from user/managed/`--settings` sources, not repository settings. `deny` beats `mask` if
  both are set. Requires Claude Code v2.1.187+ (files/deny), v2.1.199+ (mask). Broader stripping of
  Anthropic/cloud credentials from all subprocesses is a separate mechanism (`CLAUDE_CODE_SUBPROCESS_ENV_SCRUB`).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sandboxing
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Protect credentials" and "Mask environment variables" sections — files/envVars modes, injectHosts, tlsTerminate requirement, scope honoring
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.187 sandbox.credentials introduced
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: none
note: >
  No sandbox.credentials.files or .envVars entries (the sandbox is not enabled at all). Credential
  care in the engine is prose/discipline (conduct/defaults.md "Handle secrets and irreversible
  actions with care") and repo secret-scanning (secret-scan.yml), not the native sandbox credential
  deny/mask feature. CLAUDE_CODE_SUBPROCESS_ENV_SCRUB is not used.
```

**Assessment:** action mode blocks/intercepts; enforcement deterministic where the platform honors it; the engine treats it as best-effort; failure mode open; portability provider-specific behind the engine's adapter seam.

**Disposition: `HOST CONFIGURATION`** — Unused; recommended alongside sandboxed Bash for credential isolation (M5).

### `claude/permissions-sandboxing/sandboxed-bash`

```yaml
id: claude/permissions-sandboxing/sandboxed-bash
provider: claude
family: permissions-sandboxing
name: Sandboxed Bash tool (filesystem and network isolation)
what: An OS-level sandbox that confines every Bash command and its child processes to a defined filesystem and network boundary, letting most commands run without per-command prompts. It has two independent layers (filesystem and network), is enforced by the OS regardless of what the model chose to run, and complements (does not replace) permission rules.
surfaces: [cli, ide, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Built into Claude Code on macOS (Seatbelt), Linux and WSL2 (bubblewrap + socat, optional seccomp
  filter via `@anthropic-ai/sandbox-runtime`); native Windows unsupported. Enable per-project via
  `/sandbox` (writes mode to `.claude/settings.local.json`) or globally with `sandbox.enabled: true`.
  Two sandbox modes: auto-allow (sandboxed commands run without prompting; commands that can't be
  sandboxed fall back to the regular permission flow) and regular permissions (all Bash goes through
  permission flow); `sandbox.autoAllowBashIfSandboxed` (default true) controls auto-approval and a
  bare `Bash` ask rule is skipped for sandboxed commands (except in plan mode). Filesystem layer:
  default write = working dir + session temp ($TMPDIR); default read = whole machine except denied
  paths; configure via `sandbox.filesystem.allowWrite/denyWrite/denyRead/allowRead` (more-specific path
  wins; arrays merge across scopes; path prefixes `/`=absolute, `~/`=home, `./`/bare=source-relative);
  linked git worktrees get write to the main repo's shared `.git` (hooks/ and config denied);
  `sandbox.filesystem.disabled: true` (v2.1.216+, user/managed/CLI only) drops the filesystem layer
  while keeping network. Network layer: no domains pre-allowed by default (first use prompts, then
  host allowed for the session, v2.1.191+); pre-allow with `sandbox.network.allowedDomains` (WebFetch
  domain allow rules also pre-allow) and block with `deniedDomains`; `strictAllowlist: true` (v2.1.219+)
  denies non-allowlisted hosts instead of prompting; `allowManagedDomainsOnly` locks to managed domains;
  proxy runs outside the sandbox (`httpProxyPort`/`socksProxyPort` for a custom proxy; default proxy
  does not terminate TLS unless `network.tlsTerminate` set). Escape hatch: a failed sandboxed command may
  retry with `dangerouslyDisableSandbox` (runs unsandboxed via regular permission flow) unless
  `allowUnsandboxedCommands: false` (Strict sandbox mode); `excludedCommands` runs listed commands outside
  the sandbox. `failIfUnavailable: true` makes a missing sandbox a hard startup failure. Other toggles:
  `allowAppleEvents`, `enableWeakerNetworkIsolation`, `enableWeakerNestedSandbox`, `allowUnixSockets`.
  Subagents share the parent's sandbox config; built-in Read/Edit/Write and Computer-use run outside the
  sandbox under the permission system.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sandboxing
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Full sandbox mechanics — modes, two layers, filesystem/network config keys, OS primitives, escape hatch, disable-filesystem, troubleshooting, limitations
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "How permissions interact with sandboxing" — merge of filesystem/network settings with Read/Edit/WebFetch rules, autoAllowBashIfSandboxed, plan-mode substitution
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.220 strictAllowlist, v2.1.216 filesystem.disabled, v2.1.181 allowAppleEvents
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  The engine substitutes git-worktree isolation (.claude/worktrees/) + the modes.py write-gate + the
  protected-branch merge for OS-level Bash sandboxing. hook-runner.sh has per-OS logic but only to
  locate the venv interpreter, not to sandbox commands.
note: >
  No sandbox.enabled, no sandbox.filesystem/network config, no /sandbox mode written to
  .claude/settings.local.json, no autoAllowBashIfSandboxed, allowUnsandboxedCommands, failIfUnavailable,
  strictAllowlist, or excludedCommands. The dangerouslyDisableSandbox escape hatch is not referenced.
  The engine therefore does not benefit from OS-enforced Bash confinement; its Bash safety rests
  entirely on the (admittedly fallible, fail-open) permission-allowlist + hook-gate + merge model.
```

**Assessment:** action mode blocks/intercepts; enforcement deterministic where the platform honors it; the engine treats it as best-effort; failure mode open; portability provider-specific behind the engine's adapter seam.

**Disposition: `HOST CONFIGURATION`** — Unused today; RECOMMENDED host hardening — OS-enforced confinement would back the engine's fail-open gate with a real wall (migration M5 documents this).

### `claude/permissions-sandboxing/working-directories`

```yaml
id: claude/permissions-sandboxing/working-directories
provider: claude
family: permissions-sandboxing
name: Working directories and additional directories
what: The set of directories Claude Code may read and edit without per-path prompts. By default this is the launch directory; it can be extended at startup, mid-session, or via persistent settings. Additional directories govern the scope of read access and acceptEdits/auto file-edit approvals.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide, sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Extend access three ways: `--add-dir <path>` at startup, `/add-dir` in-session, or
  `permissions.additionalDirectories` in settings. Files in additional directories follow the same
  permission rules as the primary working directory — readable without prompts, edits gated by the
  current mode. Directories added via `--add-dir`/`/add-dir` also load a limited set of configuration
  (skills, subagents, and only `enabledPlugins`/`extraKnownMarketplaces` from settings; CLAUDE.md only
  when `CLAUDE_CODE_ADDITIONAL_DIRECTORIES_CLAUDE_MD=1`), whereas `permissions.additionalDirectories`
  entries grant file access only and load no configuration. `/cd` (v2.1.169+) relocates the session's
  primary working directory (loads that dir's CLAUDE.md) and is itself gated by `Cd(path)` permission
  rules. On macOS, background sessions request access to protected folders (~/Desktop, ~/Documents,
  ~/Downloads) separately.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Working directories" section — --add-dir/-/add-dir/additionalDirectories, config-loading exceptions, /cd relocation
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  The engine achieves working-scope isolation with git worktrees under .claude/worktrees/ (11 live
  per inventory) and a PR-only path to main, plus a Cd-independent worktree-isolation check
  (checkout_health.is_isolated_worktree used by set-routine, modes.py:622-632) — an isolation model
  parallel to, but not built on, additionalDirectories.
note: >
  No permissions.additionalDirectories, no --add-dir/-add-dir usage, no /cd or Cd(path) rules in
  committed settings. (Read(//Users/shanekidd/**) allow rules in settings.local.json widen read
  reach but are permission rules, not additionalDirectories.)
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — The engine's isolation model is git worktrees + PR-only merge, not additionalDirectories reach-widening.

### `claude/permissions-sandboxing/workspace-trust`

```yaml
id: claude/permissions-sandboxing/workspace-trust
provider: claude
family: permissions-sandboxing
name: Workspace trust prompt
what: A trust dialog that must be accepted before a project's capability-granting settings (permissions.allow rules and permissions.additionalDirectories from a committed .claude/settings.json) take effect. Restrictive deny/ask rules are unaffected and apply without trust.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, ide]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Until the folder is trusted, Claude Code reads a project's allow rules and additional directories but
  does not apply them; the dialog lists what the folder would grant for review. Trust is saved per
  workspace, keyed on the git repo root (or the start directory outside a repo); starting in the home
  directory holds trust for the session only, not written to disk. Trusting a parent does not apply a
  nested project's allow rules. `.claude/settings.local.json` is normally the user's own file and skips
  the check, but goes through the trust check when a repository could have supplied it (committed to git,
  or `.claude` is a symlink); two exceptions apply the file's allow rules without trust — the start
  directory is not inside a git repo, or the session runs in the user's own config home
  (home dir or a `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` directory). Dialog offers "Yes, I trust this folder" or
  "No, continue without these permissions". In `-p` non-interactive mode no dialog appears and the rules
  stay ignored. Behavior described was restored/changed across v2.1.195–2.1.207.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Project allow rules and workspace trust" section — trust keying, settings.local.json handling, non-interactive behavior
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: none
note: >
  No engine machinery for the trust dialog (it is an interactive user feature). Relevant only
  implicitly: the committed .claude/settings.local.json permissions.allow list would, per the doc,
  be subject to the trust check when the repo could have supplied it (it is committed in the template).
  The engine neither configures nor depends on trust behavior.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `HOST CONFIGURATION`** — Interactive host feature; the engine's committed allowlist is subject to it but the engine neither configures nor depends on it.


## Family: review-handoff-merge

### `claude/review-handoff-merge/code-review-local`

```yaml
id: claude/review-handoff-merge/code-review-local
provider: claude
family: review-handoff-merge
name: /code-review (local diff review command)
what: A Claude Code slash command that reviews a diff in the local terminal without installing the GitHub App. It reports correctness bugs plus reuse, simplification, and efficiency cleanups, defaulting to the branch's commits ahead of upstream plus uncommitted changes.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  `/code-review` reviews the branch's commits ahead of upstream plus working-tree changes; accepts a
  target (file path, PR number, branch name, or ref range like `main...my-feature`). Flags: `--fix`
  (applies findings to the working tree — background edits land outside checkpoints so `/rewind` won't
  undo them, use git), `--comment` (posts findings as inline PR comments). An effort level (low…max)
  trades coverage for confidence. Since v2.1.218 it runs as a background subagent with its own context
  window (foreground when: rerun while one is in progress, non-interactive `-p`/Agent SDK,
  or CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1). Reads `CLAUDE.md` but NOT `REVIEW.md`. When the host requests
  it (v2.1.218+), findings are reported via the `ReportFindings` tool and rendered as a findings list
  (file location, one-sentence summary, category tag like `correctness`); later fixes are re-reported and
  marked fixed/skipped/no-change. Marked `disable-model-invocation` (v2.1.215+: Claude won't run it on its
  own; can't be used as a scheduled-task prompt). Formerly `/simplify` (renamed at v2.1.147).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/code-review
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Review a diff locally" section — targets, --fix/--comment, background subagent, effort levels, ReportFindings, CLAUDE.md-not-REVIEW.md, disable-model-invocation, /simplify history.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.218 (background subagent), v2.1.215 (no longer self-invoked), v2.1.212 (merged cleanup finders, ~25% token cut).
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  The engine does not invoke Claude Code's built-in `/code-review` (or `--fix`/`--comment`). There is no
  .claude/commands/ directory (skills are the only invocation vector). The engine's build-time equivalent is the
  cold pre-submission pass in build-orchestration.md step 6 plus the qa-review personas, which report findings
  rather than apply `--fix` edits to the working tree.
note: >
  The engine's own commitment to "build each slice to its full capability" and flag under-builds as divergences
  (build-orchestration.md L377-383) overlaps the local review's correctness+cleanup remit, but the engine keeps
  finding and fixing separated (read-only reviewers; the orchestrator is the single writer, step 5) — the
  opposite of `/code-review --fix`'s in-place background edits.
```

**Assessment:** action mode advises; enforcement advisory; failure mode open; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `OPTIONAL INTEGRATION`** — Same pairing logic as the managed service, for local diffs.

### `claude/review-handoff-merge/code-review-managed`

```yaml
id: claude/review-handoff-merge/code-review-managed
provider: claude
family: review-handoff-merge
name: Code Review (managed GitHub PR review service)
what: A managed service (research preview) in which a fleet of specialized agents review GitHub pull requests on Anthropic infrastructure and post findings as inline comments on the offending lines, plus a summary and a check run. Findings are tagged by severity and never approve or block the PR, leaving existing review workflows intact.
surfaces: [github-ci, cloud-web]
ownership: org
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Enabled once per organization by an Owner at claude.ai/admin-settings/claude-code via the Claude GitHub
  App (Contents r/w, Issues r/w, Pull requests r/w); repositories selected per-org. Per-repo Review
  Behavior dropdown: "Once after PR creation", "After every push" (auto-resolves threads when fixes land),
  or "Manual". Multiple agents analyze the diff plus surrounding codebase in parallel; a verification step
  checks candidates against real code behavior to filter false positives; results are deduplicated and
  ranked. Severity markers: 🔴 Important, 🟡 Nit, 🟣 Pre-existing; each finding has collapsible extended
  reasoning. Findings appear as inline comments (with 👍/👎 pre-attached for one-click rating), as a
  "Claude Code Review" check run (always neutral conclusion, machine-readable severity JSON in the Details
  text parseable via gh/jq — key `bughunter-severity`), and as Files-changed annotations. Manual triggers
  (top-level PR comment): `@claude review` (single review, no subscription), `@claude review always`
  (review + subscribe to push-triggered reviews), `@claude review once` (same as bare); a July 2026 update
  changed the bare command from subscribing to not subscribing. Customization: repo `CLAUDE.md`
  (violations flagged as nits, bidirectional doc-staleness) and repo-root `REVIEW.md` (review-only,
  injected verbatim as highest-priority system-prompt block per agent; no @-import expansion). Averages
  ~20 min per review, ~$15-25 billed as usage credits (separate from plan usage); org spend cap at
  claude.ai/admin-settings/usage; analytics at claude.ai/analytics/code-review. Not available for
  Zero-Data-Retention orgs; requires Team/Enterprise (research preview).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/code-review
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Full managed-service description: setup, review behaviors, severity levels, check run, @claude review commands, CLAUDE.md/REVIEW.md customization, pricing, analytics, troubleshooting.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  This is the audit's central reimplementation. The engine hand-rolls a multi-persona PR review entirely in-repo
  instead of enabling Anthropic's managed Code Review service. The reviewer fleet is
  /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.claude/agents/engine-qa-review-{spec-conformance,divergence-hunter,
  usability,technical-integrity,security-governance}.md (post-build) and engine-design-review-{architecture,
  feasibility,product-intent,risk-governance}.md (pre-build), driven by
  /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.engine/operations/build-orchestration.md (plan-review gate step 3,
  pre-submission gate step 6, consumed-review-lenses block L187-192). Findings are dispositioned via
  .engine/policies/finding-disposition.md; the operator's merge is the only unbypassable gate.
note: >
  Precise divergence in coverage. What the native managed service offers that the engine personas do NOT:
  (a) inline PR-line comments + a "Claude Code Review" check run with machine-readable severity JSON — the engine
  instead relays findings in chat and as prose in the PR "Review" section (build-orchestration Notes L157-170),
  with only a presence-gate that the section exists (L24-25, L154-155); (b) cloud-hosted parallel fleet with an
  independent verification step to filter false positives; (c) automatic post-push re-review that auto-resolves
  threads; (d) CLAUDE.md/REVIEW.md violation flagging (no REVIEW.md in repo). What the engine personas cover that
  the native service does NOT: (a) spec-referent-driven acceptance checking — each obligation re-derived from a
  `locked` docs/spec/ span via .engine/tools/spec_referent.py, criteria handed verbatim to the passes
  (build-orchestration L241-254; agent mandate engine-qa-review-spec-conformance.md L20); (b) PLAN-stage cold
  review before any code exists (the 4 design-review personas) — native review only sees built diffs;
  (c) guardrail-ack coupling — a guardrail-weakening change leaves the gate red for the operator (build-orch
  L48-53), enforced by .github/workflows/engine-guard.yml; native findings "never approve or block";
  (d) structural read-only enforcement (every persona `permissions: read-only` + `disallowedTools`), where the
  native service is review-only by design but its siblings (/code-review --fix, auto-fix) do write;
  (e) coupled decorrelated spec-conformance + divergence-hunter pair against a locked requirement (build-orch
  L182-185); (f) disclosed no-op instead of silent green when nothing is installed/locked.
```

**Assessment:** action mode advises; enforcement advisory; failure mode open; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `OPTIONAL INTEGRATION`** — Complementary to the persona gates (native: inline comments, severity check-runs, cloud verification, post-push re-review; engine-only: spec-referent acceptance, plan-stage review, guardrail-ack, structural read-only). An operator may enable alongside; never a replacement for the merge gate.

### `claude/review-handoff-merge/remote-control`

```yaml
id: claude/review-handoff-merge/remote-control
provider: claude
family: review-handoff-merge
name: Remote Control
what: Connects claude.ai/code or the Claude mobile app to a Claude Code session running on the user's own machine, so a task started at the desk can be steered from a phone, tablet, or another browser. Claude keeps running locally, so execution and filesystem access stay on the machine; the web/mobile interfaces are a window into the local session.
surfaces: [cli, ide, cloud-web, desktop]
ownership: mixed
persistence: mixed
mechanics: >
  Started from the CLI three ways — `claude remote-control` (server mode; stays running awaiting connections,
  shows a session URL and spacebar QR code), `claude --remote-control`/`--rc` (interactive session also
  controllable remotely), or `/remote-control`/`/rc` inside a running session — or via `/remote-control` in the
  VS Code extension. Server mode flags include `--name`, `--remote-control-session-name-prefix`, `-c/--continue`
  (v2.1.200+), `--session-id` (v2.1.200+), `--spawn same-dir|worktree|session`, `--capacity <N>` (default 32),
  `--[no-]create-session-in-dir`, `--verbose`, and `--sandbox`/`--no-sandbox` (off by default). Connect from
  another device via the session URL, QR scan, or the session list at claude.ai/code / the app (sessions show a
  computer icon with a green dot when online). Conversation and subagent/workflow progress stay in sync across
  all connected devices; attachments sent from phone/browser are downloaded to the machine and passed as `@`
  file references; sessions reconnect automatically after sleep/network drop. Auto-connect for every interactive
  session is set via `/config` "Enable Remote Control for all sessions" (true/false/default), also in Desktop
  and VS Code settings. Requirements: Pro/Max/Team/Enterprise (API keys unsupported); on Team/Enterprise an
  Owner must enable the Remote Control toggle at claude.ai/admin-settings/claude-code; claude.ai full-scope OAuth
  login; api.anthropic.com endpoint only (not Bedrock/GCP/Foundry, and disabled when ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL points
  elsewhere, v2.1.196+); feature-flag evaluation not disabled (DISABLE_TELEMETRY, DO_NOT_TRACK,
  CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_NONESSENTIAL_TRAFFIC, DISABLE_GROWTHBOOK each break it); and accepted workspace-trust.
  Connection is outbound HTTPS only (no inbound ports), registering with the Anthropic API and polling for work
  over TLS with multiple short-lived scoped credentials. While connected, the transcript is stored on Anthropic
  servers to sync devices and enable reconnect (execution/files stay local); turn off entirely with the
  `disableRemoteControl` setting; ZDR/compliance organizations cannot enable it. Limits: one remote session per
  interactive process (use server mode for concurrency); the local process must keep running; a >~10-minute
  network outage times the session out; starting an ultraplan session disconnects Remote Control.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/remote-control
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
```

```yaml
  session-URL reminders (v2.1.208+, "Still working"/"Check in from your phone" and "Approve tool calls from your
  phone") also surface the session link during long turns or after repeated permission prompts and cannot be
  configured off.
```

### `claude/review-handoff-merge/terminal-to-web-cloud`

```yaml
id: claude/review-handoff-merge/terminal-to-web-cloud
provider: claude
family: review-handoff-merge
name: Terminal-to-web session start (--cloud)
what: The claude --cloud flag creates a new cloud session for the current repository from the terminal, cloning the current directory's GitHub remote at the current branch so work runs in the cloud while the developer keeps working locally. The Desktop app additionally offers a "Continue in" menu that can send a local session to the web.
surfaces: [cli, cloud-web, desktop]
ownership: user
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  `claude --cloud "<task>"` creates a new cloud session on claude.ai; the VM clones from GitHub (not the local
  machine), so local commits must be pushed first. Works with a single repository at a time and can be run
  multiple times to launch parallel independent cloud sessions. The older `--remote` spelling remains as a
  deprecated alias. As of v2.1.195 the CLI shows a live checklist of setup steps (clone, setup script) while the
  container provisions, and messages typed during provisioning are queued. Progress is monitored with `/tasks`
  in the CLI or by opening the session on claude.ai / mobile. Handoff from the CLI is one-way: you can pull
  cloud sessions into the terminal but cannot push an existing terminal session to the web via the CLI; the
  Desktop "Continue in" menu is the path that sends a local session to the web. `--cloud` requires claude.ai
  subscription sign-in (API-key auth fails with "Unable to get organization UUID"); it is not available on
  Amazon Bedrock, Google Cloud's Agent Platform, or Microsoft Foundry. `--cloud` (creates cloud sessions) is
  distinct from `--remote-control` (exposes a local session for monitoring).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/claude-code-on-the-web
    retrieved: 2026-08-02

```

### `claude/review-handoff-merge/trusted-devices`

```yaml
id: claude/review-handoff-merge/trusted-devices
provider: claude
family: review-handoff-merge
name: Trusted Devices (Remote Control)
what: An organization-wide beta setting (Team and Enterprise) that requires members to verify their device before viewing or steering Remote Control sessions from claude.ai, the mobile apps, or Claude Desktop. It ties Remote Control access to an enrolled device plus a recent authentication.
surfaces: [cloud-web, desktop, cli]
ownership: org
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Off by default until an admin enables the "Require trusted devices" toggle under the Remote Control setting at
  claude.ai/admin-settings/claude-code; it applies to every member and only to Remote Control sessions started
  after enablement (already-running sessions are not retroactively protected). Interacting with a Remote Control
  session then requires both an enrolled device (each browser/phone/desktop app enrolls its own credential, only
  offered shortly after a full sign-in) and a sign-in no more than 18 hours old, refreshed via Face ID, Touch ID,
  Windows Hello, or a passkey. Biometric checks run on-device through the OS/browser (same mechanism as passkey
  sign-in); Anthropic stores only the device public key and metadata (display name, platform, enrollment time),
  never biometric data. The terminal machine receives its own credential automatically at CLI sign-in with no
  separate enrollment. Members manage/revoke their devices at claude.ai/settings/account (Trusted devices
  section); credentials also expire if unused; admins can "Sign out everywhere" to revoke a member's sessions
  and devices. The setting applies only to Remote Control; regular chat, terminal Claude Code, and API usage are
  unaffected.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/remote-control
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Trusted Devices beta scope, admin enablement, dual enrolled-device + 18-hour sign-in requirement, biometric/on-device model, device management/revocation, and scope limited to Remote Control.
```

### `claude/review-handoff-merge/ultrareview`

```yaml
id: claude/review-handoff-merge/ultrareview
provider: claude
family: review-handoff-merge
name: Ultrareview (/code-review ultra, cloud multi-agent review)
what: A deep, multi-agent code review (research preview) that runs on Claude Code on the web infrastructure. `/code-review ultra` launches a fleet of reviewer agents in a remote sandbox to find and independently verify bugs in a branch or pull request before merge, keeping the local terminal free.
surfaces: [cli, cloud-web, github-ci]
ownership: user
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Invoked with `/code-review ultra` (alias `/ultrareview` when available); Claude never starts it on its
  own. Default scope: current branch vs the default branch including uncommitted and staged changes
  (branch mode bundles and uploads local repo state). `/code-review ultra <branch>` reviews against a
  different base (fetched from origin if not local). `/code-review ultra <pr#>` (also `#1234`, `PR 1234`,
  pasted URL) uses PR mode, which clones the PR host-side and uploads nothing local; works on github.com
  and Owner-connected GitHub Enterprise Server. Plain-words note (v2.1.218+): multi-word non-branch/PR text
  is kept as a note related to the findings. Diff limits: up to 500 changed files / 8,000 changed lines by
  default for branch review; empty-diff and no-merge-base fallbacks (whole-repo review offered). A launch
  dialog shows scope, remaining free runs, and estimated cost before confirming; the review runs as a
  background task (~5-10 min), tracked via `/tasks` (stop archives the session, no partial findings).
  Non-interactive: `claude ultrareview [pr#|base]` blocks until done, prints findings to stdout, exit 0/1
  (130 on Ctrl-C); flags `--json` (raw bugs.json) and `--timeout <minutes>` (default 30); progress/session
  URL to stderr. On v2.1.218+, `claude -p '/code-review ultra'` launches without waiting (prints tracking
  link), but stops before launching when the run would bill usage credits (needs interactive billing
  confirmation). Pricing: Pro/Max get 3 one-time free runs, Team/Enterprise none; thereafter ~$5-25 as
  usage credits; billing confirmed once per conversation. Requires claude.ai-account auth; not available on
  Bedrock, Vertex, Microsoft Foundry, or Zero-Data-Retention orgs — when unavailable, `/code-review ultra`
  falls back to a local review.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/ultrareview
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Full ultrareview page — invocation, branch/PR/plain-words modes, diff limits, launch dialog, /tasks tracking, claude ultrareview subcommand and flags, pricing/free runs, availability constraints, comparison table.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/code-review
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: "Escalate to ultrareview" section — /code-review ultra --fix scope, claude -p launch behavior, Bedrock/Vertex/Foundry/ZDR exclusions.
```

### `claude/review-handoff-merge/web-to-terminal-teleport`

```yaml
id: claude/review-handoff-merge/web-to-terminal-teleport
provider: claude
family: review-handoff-merge
name: Web-to-terminal teleport (--teleport / /teleport)
what: Teleport pulls a cloud session and its branch into the local terminal to continue locally, loading the full conversation history. The terminal gets its own copy of the session; new local work stays local and does not appear back in the cloud session.
surfaces: [cli, cloud-web]
ownership: user
persistence: mixed

  Entry points: `claude --teleport` (interactive session picker) or `claude --teleport <session-id>`; `/teleport`
  or `/tp` inside an existing CLI session; `/tasks` then press `t`; or the web "Open in CLI" button that copies a
  paste-able command. On teleport, Claude verifies the correct repository, fetches and checks out the cloud
  session's branch, and loads history locally. Requirements: clean git state (prompts to stash), a checkout of
  the same repository (not a fork; v2.1.199+ accepts unparseable remotes like SSH host aliases after a
  confirmation matching owner/repo), the session branch pushed to the remote, and the same claude.ai account.
  `--teleport` is distinct from `--resume` (which reopens local history and lists no cloud sessions). Requires
  claude.ai subscription auth (API-key auth prompts `/login`); unavailable on Bedrock/GCP Agent Platform/Foundry
  and when the organization has disabled cloud sessions. It connects through the same Remote Control session
  infrastructure, so auth/expiry failures surface with Remote Control wording ("Remote Control session
  expired", "Access denied"). To keep steering from a phone after teleporting, start `/remote-control` in the
  local session.
```

## Family: scheduling-background

### `claude/scheduling-background/background-sessions`

```yaml
id: claude/scheduling-background/background-sessions
provider: claude
family: scheduling-background
name: Background sessions (background tasks / agent view)
what: Sessions can run detached in the background via a per-user supervisor process, freeing the terminal while work continues; they persist across terminal close, sleep, and Claude Code restarts. Managed through the agent view and shell subcommands.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Start with `claude --bg "<prompt>"` (flags `--name`, `--model`, `--permission-mode`,
  `--effort`, `--agent`, `--settings`, `--add-dir`), move a live session with `/background`/`/bg`,
  or press `←` on an empty prompt. `claude agents` opens a full-screen TUI grouping sessions by
  state (peek with `Space`, attach with `Enter`/`→`, dispatch new sessions, `Ctrl+X` stop/delete,
  `Ctrl+T` pin); `claude agents --json [--all] [--cwd]` lists non-interactively. Shell
  subcommands: `claude attach <id>`, `claude logs <id>`, `claude stop|kill <id>`, `claude rm <id>`,
  `claude respawn <id>|--all`, `claude daemon status|stop [--any] [--keep-workers]`.
  `claude --bg --exec '<cmd>'` runs a background shell job (no model, output kept ~5 min).
  `/fork` copies a session to an independent background session (2.1.212+). A per-user supervisor
  (`~/.claude/daemon`, state in `roster.json` and `~/.claude/jobs/<id>/`) hosts sessions, maintains
  a pre-warmed worker pool, respawns on binary update, and stops idle non-pinned sessions after
  ~1 hr. In-flight work (background shells, subagents, `/loop` tasks) carries over on background;
  disable via `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_ADOPT=1` / `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BG_EXIT_HANDOFF=1`. Detaching
  never stops a session (`/stop` inside, or `Ctrl+X`/`claude stop` do). Notifications fire on
  needs-input/finish/fail via `preferredNotifChannel` and the `Notification` hook; scheduled
  (`/loop`) sessions notify only on needs-input. `--dangerously-skip-permissions` is not carried
  into background sessions. Disable the whole feature with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AGENT_VIEW=1` /
  `disableAgentView`.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-view
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents claude --bg/--exec, /background, /fork, the agent-view TUI and shortcuts, shell subcommands, the supervisor daemon and its lifecycle, work carry-over env vars, attach/detach/stop semantics, and notifications.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Records background-session fixes — daemon token/attach (2.1.203/2.1.208), sleep/wake stops (2.1.200), rename persistence (2.1.202), memory-pressure reaping (2.1.193), and Windows /background spawn (2.1.212).
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: none
note: No `claude --bg`, agent-view TUI, `/background`, `/fork`, or daemon reliance. Unattended work runs as a fresh scheduled session (routine-entry.md), not a detached background session.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

### `claude/scheduling-background/desktop-scheduled-tasks`

```yaml
id: claude/scheduling-background/desktop-scheduled-tasks
provider: claude
family: scheduling-background
name: Desktop local scheduled tasks
what: Local scheduled tasks in the Claude Desktop app start a new session automatically on a schedule, running on your machine with direct access to local files and tools. They fire only while the app is open and the computer is awake.
surfaces: [desktop]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Created via Routines > New routine > **Local**, or by asking Claude in any Desktop session.
  Fields: Name (lowercased to kebab-case, used as on-disk folder, must be unique), Description,
  Instructions (with permission-mode and model pickers, working-folder selection, and an
  isolated-worktree toggle), Schedule. Schedule presets: Manual, Hourly, Daily, Weekdays,
  Weekly; other cadences (e.g. every 15 min, monthly, one-off) are set by asking Claude in
  natural language. Minimum interval 1 minute. Desktop checks the schedule every minute while
  open, starts a fresh session at a deterministic few-minute stagger, and fires a desktop
  notification with the session appearing under a **Scheduled** sidebar section. Sleeping
  through a time skips the run; on wake/start Desktop runs one catch-up for the most recently
  missed time within seven days (older discarded). Each task has its own permission mode plus
  `~/.claude/settings.json` allow rules; Manual-mode tasks stall on unpermitted tools until
  approved. Task prompt lives at `~/.claude/scheduled-tasks/<task-name>/SKILL.md` (YAML
  frontmatter `name`/`description`, prompt as body; schedule/folder/model/enabled not in file).
  A running task can reschedule itself via the `update_scheduled_task` MCP tool. Manage
  (Run now, Active/Paused, Edit, history, revoke approvals, Delete with optional on-disk file
  removal) from the detail page or by asking Claude. Distinct from cloud routines: local files
  access, requires machine on and app open, no API/GitHub triggers.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/desktop-scheduled-tasks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents creation fields, schedule presets and 1-minute minimum, per-minute checking/stagger/notifications, missed-run catch-up, per-task permissions, SKILL.md on-disk format, update_scheduled_task self-rescheduling, and management actions.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: This is the Claude-side host for the engine's unattended build routine. /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.claude/skills/engine-routine/SKILL.md is explicitly "a command you put inside a scheduled automation — a Claude Desktop routine or a Codex Automation." The entry procedure is /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.engine/operations/routine-entry.md; the write-stance gate is `set-routine` in /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.engine/tools/modes.py (line 616), which grants the Routine stance ONLY on positive proof of worktree isolation (checkout_health.is_isolated_worktree). The Routine stance is one of the three stances in /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.engine/operations/operating-modes.md (step 4).
overlap: engine-routine skill + routine-entry.md + modes.py set-routine + operating-modes.md Routine stance are a full engine layer on top of the native local scheduled task.
note: The engine consumes the task's per-task permission mode + isolated-worktree toggle but sets neither ("operator-side settings in the scheduling app," routine-entry.md Notes). It relies on the task firing SessionStart (so its hooks-ran safety check works) and on the single-flight the Desktop task provides (routine-entry.md line 87). ### `claude/scheduling-background/in-session-scheduled-tasks`
```

```yaml
id: claude/scheduling-background/in-session-scheduled-tasks
provider: claude
family: scheduling-background
name: In-session scheduled tasks (/loop and cron tools)
what: Session-scoped scheduling that re-runs a prompt on an interval, at a self-paced cadence, or once at a future time, within an open Claude Code session. Tasks live in the current conversation and stop when a new one starts, but are restored on --resume/--continue if unexpired.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  `/loop` (a bundled skill) runs a prompt on repeat while the session stays open:
  `/loop 5m <prompt>` runs on a fixed interval (units `s`/`m`/`h`/`d`; seconds round up to a
  minute; off-grid intervals like `7m`/`90m` round to the nearest cron step); `/loop <prompt>`
  lets Claude choose each interval (1 min–1 hr) via the `ScheduleWakeup` tool, ending with
  `stop: true`; bare `/loop` runs a built-in maintenance prompt (or a `.claude/loop.md`
  [project, precedence] / `~/.claude/loop.md` [user] override, truncated past 25,000 bytes).
  A skill passed as the prompt re-runs each iteration, but only self-invocable skills execute
  (built-ins, `disable-model-invocation` skills, denied/withheld skills, MCP prompts reach
  Claude as plain text). Stop a waiting fixed loop with `Esc`. Natural-language one-time
  reminders (e.g. "remind me at 3pm...") schedule a single-fire self-deleting task. Underlying
  tools: `CronCreate` (5-field cron `minute hour day-of-month month day-of-week`, wildcards/
  values/steps/ranges/lists; no `L`/`W`/`?`/name-aliases; either-match when DoM and DoW both
  set), `CronList`, `CronDelete` (8-char IDs; up to 50 tasks/session). The scheduler checks
  every second, fires between turns at low priority, interprets all times local, and adds
  deterministic jitter (recurring up to 30 min or half the interval; one-shot at :00/:30 up to
  90 s early). Recurring tasks expire 7 days after creation (fire once more, then delete); no
  catch-up for missed fires. Disable entirely with `CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_CRON=1`. On Bedrock/
  AWS/Google/Foundry, a no-interval `/loop` runs on a fixed 10-minute schedule and loop.md/
  maintenance prompt are unavailable.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/scheduled-tasks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents /loop modes and interval syntax, loop.md precedence/size, self-invocable-skill restriction, one-time reminders, CronCreate/List/Delete and cron reference, jitter, 7-day expiry, session scoping/resume, and CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_CRON.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/tools-reference
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Verbatim ScheduleWakeup description (reschedules self-paced /loop, stop:true cancels the pending wakeup, requires 2.1.202+, appears in Stop hook session_crons).
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
note: The native /loop, CronCreate/List/Delete, and ScheduleWakeup machinery is entirely unused — no `.claude/loop.md` or `~/.claude/loop.md` override, no CronCreate calls, no CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_CRON. Recurrence is deliberately pushed to CI cron + operator-scheduled tasks instead of the session-scoped scheduler.
```

### `claude/scheduling-background/monitor-tool`

```yaml
id: claude/scheduling-background/monitor-tool
provider: claude
family: scheduling-background
name: Monitor tool
what: A built-in tool that runs a command in the background and feeds each output line back to Claude so it can react to logs, file changes, or polled status mid-conversation, without pausing the session. It can also watch a WebSocket and treat each message as an event.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  Claude may use Monitor directly (often for dynamic `/loop` requests, as it streams output
  instead of polling and is more token-efficient). It uses the same permission rules as `Bash`,
  so Bash allow/deny patterns apply; the WebSocket source (a `ws` input replacing `command`,
  with its own two fields) has a separate approval prompt and cannot be combined with a command
  in one call. Stop a monitor by asking Claude to cancel it or by ending the session. Plugins
  can declare monitors that start automatically when the plugin is active.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/tools-reference
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Verbatim Monitor description, command vs ws inputs, Bash-permission reuse, WebSocket approval prompt, and plugin-declared monitors.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/scheduled-tasks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents Claude using Monitor for dynamic /loop schedules as a polling-free alternative.
```

### `claude/scheduling-background/routine-api-trigger`

```yaml
id: claude/scheduling-background/routine-api-trigger
provider: claude
family: scheduling-background
name: Routine API trigger (/fire endpoint)
what: An API trigger gives a routine a dedicated HTTP endpoint; POSTing to it with the routine's bearer token starts a new cloud session and returns a session URL. Used to wire routines into alerting, deploy pipelines, or internal tools.
surfaces: [cloud-web, sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Added to an existing routine from the web only (CLI cannot create/revoke tokens). POST to
  `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/claude_code/routines/<routine_id>/fire` with
  `Authorization: Bearer <token>`, `anthropic-beta: experimental-cc-routine-2026-04-01`,
  `anthropic-version: 2023-06-01`. Each routine has its own single-use-view token
  (Generate/Regenerate/Revoke); a bad token returns 401. Optional freeform `text` body field
  passes run-specific context; it is not parsed and arrives wrapped in a
  `<routine-fire-payload>` block labeled untrusted, so the routine's saved prompt must opt in to
  acting on it. Success returns JSON with `claude_code_session_id` and `claude_code_session_url`.
  Ships under a dated beta header; the two most recent prior header versions keep working.
  Full reference at platform.claude.com; the endpoint is claude.ai-users-only and not part of
  the Claude Platform API surface.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/routines
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents the /fire endpoint URL, required headers/beta version, token lifecycle, the text field and its untrusted-payload wrapping, and the response shape.
```

### `claude/scheduling-background/routine-github-trigger`

```yaml
id: claude/scheduling-background/routine-github-trigger
provider: claude
family: scheduling-background
name: Routine GitHub event trigger
what: A GitHub trigger starts a new cloud session automatically when a matching event (pull request or release) occurs on a connected repository, with optional filters. Each matching event starts its own session.
surfaces: [cloud-web]
ownership: user
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Configured from the web UI only; requires installing the Claude GitHub App on the repo
  (running `/web-setup` grants clone access but does not install the app or enable webhooks).
  Supported event categories are Pull request (opened/closed/assigned/labeled/synchronized/etc.)
  and Release (created/published/edited/deleted); you can pick a specific action or all actions
  in a category. Pull-request filters (Author, Title, Body, Base branch, Head branch, Labels,
  Is draft, Is merged) each pair a field with an operator (equals, contains, starts with, is one
  of, is not one of, matches regex); all conditions must match. `matches regex` tests the whole
  field value. Session reuse across events is not available — each event maps to an independent
  session. During research preview, per-routine and per-account hourly webhook caps drop excess
  events until the window resets.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/routines
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents GitHub App requirement, supported events, PR filter fields/operators, one-session-per-event mapping, and preview hourly caps.
```
