### `claude/scheduling-background/routines-cloud`

```yaml
id: claude/scheduling-background/routines-cloud
provider: claude
family: scheduling-background
name: Routines (cloud) with scheduled and one-off triggers
what: A routine is a saved Claude Code configuration (prompt, repositories, environment, connectors) that runs autonomously on Anthropic-managed cloud infrastructure, so it keeps working when the machine is off. Currently in research preview. Schedule triggers run on a recurring cadence or once at a future time.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, cloud-web]
ownership: user
persistence: cloud
mechanics: >
  Created and managed at claude.ai/code/routines, in the Desktop Routines page (choose
  **Cloud**), or from the CLI with `/schedule` (alias `/routines`); all surfaces write to the
  same cloud account. `/schedule` accepts natural language (e.g. `/schedule daily PR review at
  9am`, or one-off `/schedule in 2 weeks, open a cleanup PR`) and supports `list`, `update`,
  `run`. Schedule presets are hourly/daily/weekdays/weekly; custom cron is set via
  `/schedule update`, minimum interval 1 hour (faster expressions rejected). Times are entered
  local and converted to wall-clock; runs stagger a few minutes with a per-routine consistent
  offset. Each run clones selected repos from the default branch, pushes to `claude/`-prefixed
  branches, runs autonomously with no permission prompts, and uses the selected model and a
  chosen cloud environment (network access, env vars, setup script). One-off runs auto-disable
  after firing (marked **Ran**) and are exempt from the daily routine-run cap but draw down
  subscription usage. Available on Pro/Max/Team/Enterprise with Claude Code on the web enabled;
  Team/Enterprise Owners can disable via the Routines admin toggle. `/schedule` requires a
  claude.ai login (not Console API key or cloud-provider auth) and feature-flag fetching enabled.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/routines
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents the routine concept, creation surfaces, /schedule command and subcommands, schedule presets/custom cron/one-off, autonomous execution, repos/branches, environments, usage caps, and plan/admin gating.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/scheduled-tasks
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Comparison table placing Cloud routines against Desktop and /loop (runs on Anthropic cloud, no machine/session required, 1-hour minimum interval).
```

## Family: skills-commands-plugins

### `claude/skills-commands-plugins/builtin-commands`

```yaml
id: claude/skills-commands-plugins/builtin-commands
provider: claude
family: skills-commands-plugins
name: Built-in slash commands
what: Built-in commands are slash commands whose behavior is coded into the Claude Code CLI and executed directly (e.g. /help, /compact, /config, /clear, /status, /context, /permissions, /plugin, /skills, /add-dir). They are distinct from bundled skills, which are prompts handed to Claude that Claude can also invoke autonomously. Custom author-defined commands are the separate merged-into-skills surface.
surfaces: [cli, desktop]
ownership: vendor-managed
persistence: none
mechanics: >
  Invoked with /<name>; execute fixed CLI logic rather than prompting the model. The commands reference
  distinguishes "built-in commands" (behavior coded into the CLI) from bundled skills (marked "Skill" in
  the Purpose column). /config opens the Settings interface (theme, model, output style, other
  preferences). A few built-in commands (/init, /review, /security-review) are additionally reachable
  through the Skill tool; most (e.g. /compact) are not. To add your own commands, the reference directs
  users to skills.
sources:

    retrieved: 2026-08-02

  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Confirms /init, /review, /security-review are exposed via the Skill tool while other built-in commands like /compact are not.
```

```yaml
  the engine's hooks); AGENTS.md L50/L90 and README L236/L282 point to /config for settings/output
```

```yaml
id: claude/skills-commands-plugins/bundled-skills

Unlike built-in commands, which execute fixed CLI logic, bundled skills hand Claude detailed instructions it orchestrates with its tools; Claude auto-invokes some, while others (/verify, /code-review as of v2.1.215) run only on explicit invocation.

  hidden via DISABLE_DOCTOR_COMMAND env var or a skillOverrides "doctor":"off" entry. Listed alongside
  built-in commands in the commands reference, marked "Skill" in the Purpose column. The /run, /verify,
  /run-skill-generator trio (v2.1.145+) can record per-project recipes into .claude/skills/run-<name>/
  or .claude/skills/verify/SKILL.md. A few built-in commands (/init, /review, /security-review) are
  also reachable through the Skill tool; others like /compact are not.
```

```yaml
  Plugins carry these as a flat commands/ directory at the plugin root (documented as the legacy
  layout; skills/ recommended for new plugins). The docs explicitly frame this as a merge, not a
  removal: "Custom commands have been merged into skills ... Your existing .claude/commands/ files
  keep working." Skills add features commands lack: a supporting-files directory, invocation-control
  frontmatter, and automatic model-invoked loading.
```

### `claude/skills-commands-plugins/output-styles`

```yaml
id: claude/skills-commands-plugins/output-styles
provider: claude
family: skills-commands-plugins
name: Output styles
what: Output styles change how Claude Code responds (role, tone, output format) by directly modifying the system prompt, without changing what Claude knows. Three built-in styles beyond Default exist — Proactive, Explanatory, Learning — and users can author custom Markdown output styles. This surface is live, NOT retired or merged; only the standalone /output-style command was deprecated (v2.1.73) and removed (v2.1.91) in favor of /config and the outputStyle setting.
surfaces: [cli, desktop]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Built-ins: Default (existing SWE system prompt), Proactive (execute-immediately autonomy), Explanatory
  (adds educational "Insights"), Learning (learn-by-doing, inserts TODO(human) markers). Selected via
  /config -> Output style; saved to the outputStyle field in .claude/settings.local.json (project-local).
  Set directly with {"outputStyle":"Explanatory"}. Takes effect after /clear or a new session (system
  prompt is read once at session start). Custom output style = a Markdown file (frontmatter + instructions
  appended to the system prompt) at one of three levels: user ~/.claude/output-styles, project
  .claude/output-styles (loaded from every such dir between cwd and repo root; nearest wins on name clash
  as of v2.1.178), or managed-policy .claude/output-styles in the managed settings dir. Frontmatter fields:
  name, description, keep-coding-instructions (default false — when false, built-in SWE instructions are
  omitted), force-for-plugin (plugin styles only; auto-applies whenever the plugin is enabled, overriding
  the user's outputStyle). Applies to the main conversation only; subagents run their own system prompt,
  except a fork inherits the parent's. Plugins can ship styles in an output-styles/ directory (reloaded
  via /reload-plugins, not live change detection).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/output-styles
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Full output-styles reference — built-in styles, /config selection, outputStyle setting, three custom-style levels and file paths, frontmatter table (name/description/keep-coding-instructions/force-for-plugin), /output-style deprecation (v2.1.73) and removal (v2.1.91), system-prompt mechanics, plugin output-styles/ directory.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Corroborates that plugin output-styles/ changes need /reload-plugins (not live change detection).
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

### `claude/skills-commands-plugins/plugin-marketplaces`

```yaml
id: claude/skills-commands-plugins/plugin-marketplaces
provider: claude
family: skills-commands-plugins
name: Plugin marketplaces
what: A marketplace is a catalog (a .claude-plugin/marketplace.json file) that lists plugins and where to fetch them, providing discovery, version tracking, and updates. Marketplaces are hosted on git (GitHub/GitLab), local paths, or other sources, and users add them and install individual plugins from them.
surfaces: [cli, desktop]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  marketplace.json lives at .claude-plugin/marketplace.json in the repo root. Required fields: name
  (kebab-case public identifier; one registration per name per user — re-adding a name replaces the
  prior one), owner (object with name/email), plugins (array). Each plugin entry needs at least name
  and source; source may be a relative path ("./plugins/x"), or an object such as
  {"source":"github","repo":"org/repo"}, plus optional description/version/author. A set of names is
  reserved for official Anthropic use (e.g. claude-plugins-official, claude-community, anthropic-*,
  agent-skills) and re-checked on every load (v2.1.205+). Users run /plugin marketplace add <path-or-repo>,
  then /plugin install <plugin>@<marketplace>, then /reload-plugins; install prompts for an installation
  scope. Refresh with /plugin marketplace update. Installation copies the plugin directory to a cache
  location, so plugins cannot reference files outside their own directory (use symlinks to share). Team
  distribution via a repo's config; private repos supported for internal marketplaces. Anthropic's
  claude-plugins-official registers automatically on first interactive launch (else
  claude plugin marketplace add anthropics/claude-plugins-official).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugin-marketplaces
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Marketplace guide — marketplace.json location and required-fields schema, source types (path/github object), add/install/update commands, install-caching behavior, reserved marketplace names.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Corroborates official (claude-plugins-official) vs community (claude-community) marketplaces, /plugin marketplace add usage, and community submission/validation flow.
```

### `claude/skills-commands-plugins/plugins`

```yaml
id: claude/skills-commands-plugins/plugins
provider: claude
family: skills-commands-plugins
name: Plugins
what: A plugin is a self-contained directory that bundles skills, agents, hooks, MCP servers, LSP servers, background monitors, and default settings for sharing across projects and teams. It is identified by a .claude-plugin/plugin.json manifest (optional when components use default locations); plugin skills are namespaced /plugin-name:skill-name to avoid conflicts. Standalone .claude/ config is the alternative for personal/single-project use.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, cloud-web]
ownership: mixed
persistence: mixed
mechanics: >
  Manifest .claude-plugin/plugin.json fields: name (unique id + skill namespace), description,
  version (optional — if omitted for git-distributed plugins the commit SHA is the version), author,
  and more (homepage/repository/license in the full schema). All component dirs sit at the plugin ROOT,
  never inside .claude-plugin/: skills/ (<name>/SKILL.md), commands/ (legacy flat .md), agents/,
  hooks/ (hooks.json), .mcp.json, .lsp.json, monitors/ (monitors.json), bin/ (added to Bash PATH while
  enabled), settings.json (only agent and subagentStatusLine keys honored). A one-skill plugin may put
  SKILL.md at the plugin root. Install/enable via /plugin (install, marketplace add/update), or test
  locally with claude --plugin-dir <dir> (accepts a .zip since v2.1.128) or --plugin-url <zip-url>
  (session-only). claude plugin init scaffolds a skills-dir plugin under ~/.claude/skills/<name>/ that
  auto-loads as <name>@skills-dir. /reload-plugins reloads plugins, skills, agents, hooks, plugin MCP
  and LSP servers mid-session. Project-scoped plugins can be declared in a repo's .claude/settings.json;
  those install at cloud-session start, while user-settings-only plugins don't transfer. Project .claude/
  agents override same-named plugin agents; plugin skills coexist with same-named local skills via
  namespacing. Trust: project skills-dir plugins and project skill allowed-tools require accepting the
  workspace-trust dialog. claude plugin validate checks a plugin; two Anthropic marketplaces exist
  (claude-plugins-official, curated; claude-community, reviewed third-party submissions).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/plugins
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Full plugin-creation guide — manifest schema, plugin-structure directory table, standalone-vs-plugin comparison, --plugin-dir/--plugin-url, skills-dir plugins, settings.json (agent/subagentStatusLine), component types, migration, marketplaces, validation/submission.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Plugin fixes/features — install-consent on project-settings loader paths (2.1.195/2.1.206), worktree loading (2.1.200), auto-rename via marketplace renames map (2.1.211), plugin-skill name-prefix fix (2.1.210), Skills section in /plugin Installed tab (2.1.186).
```

```yaml
overlap: >
  the engine's own packaging machinery holds this ground — module_manager.py, instantiator.py,

  The engine ships as standalone .claude/ + .codex/ + .agents/ config — the documented alternative
  to plugin packaging for a single project. Its cross-component bundle (skills + agents + hooks +
  2 MCP servers + settings) is exactly what a plugin would carry, but assembled directly in the

note: >
```

### `claude/skills-commands-plugins/skills`

```yaml
id: claude/skills-commands-plugins/skills
provider: claude
family: skills-commands-plugins
name: Skills (Agent Skills / SKILL.md)
what: A skill is a SKILL.md file (Markdown body plus optional YAML frontmatter) placed in a named directory that extends what Claude Code can do; Claude loads it automatically when relevant or the user invokes it directly as /skill-name. Claude Code follows the agentskills.io open standard and adds extensions (invocation control, subagent execution, dynamic context injection). A skill folder may include supporting files (templates, reference docs, scripts) loaded only when referenced.
surfaces: [cli, desktop, cloud-web, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: mixed
mechanics: >
  Skill lives at <name>/SKILL.md; the directory name becomes the /command by default. Scope by
  location: enterprise (managed settings dir), personal (~/.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md), project
  (.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md), plugin (<plugin>/skills/<name>/SKILL.md). Name-clash precedence:
  enterprise > personal > project, and any level overrides a bundled skill of the same name; plugin
  skills are namespaced plugin-name:skill-name so they never conflict. Nested .claude/skills/ below
  the cwd load lazily when Claude touches a file in that subtree; on a clash the nested skill appears
  as <dir>:<name> (v2.1.203+/2.1.178+). --add-dir / /add-dir load .claude/skills/ from added dirs
  (an exception to file-access-only). Frontmatter fields (all optional; only description recommended):
  name, description, when_to_use, argument-hint, arguments, disable-model-invocation, user-invocable,
  allowed-tools, disallowed-tools, model, effort, context (fork), agent, background, hooks, paths,
  shell (bash|powershell). Boolean fields accept yes/no/on/off/1/0 as well as true/false (v2.1.218+).
  Invocation control: disable-model-invocation:true = user-only; user-invocable:false = Claude-only.
  Arguments via $ARGUMENTS, $ARGUMENTS[N], $N, named $name; substitutions include ${CLAUDE_SESSION_ID},
  ${CLAUDE_EFFORT}, ${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}, ${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}. context:fork runs the skill body as a
  subagent prompt (background by default; background:false to block; agent field picks agent type,
  default general-purpose). allowed-tools pre-approves tools for the invoking turn only (grant clears
  on next user message); disallowed-tools removes tools for the active skill. Content lifecycle: rendered
  SKILL.md enters the conversation once and persists for the session; re-invocation appends a fresh copy
  only if rendered content differs (v2.1.202+). skillOverrides setting (.claude/settings.local.json)
  controls visibility per skill: on / name-only / user-invocable-only / off. Skill-listing budget scales
  at 1% of context window (skillListingBudgetFraction / SLASH_COMMAND_TOOL_CHAR_BUDGET); combined
  description+when_to_use capped at 1,536 chars (skillListingMaxDescChars). Live change detection picks
  up SKILL.md edits mid-session without restart. Adding .claude-plugin/plugin.json to a skill folder
  makes it a <name>@skills-dir plugin. Permission control via Skill / Skill(name) / Skill(name *) rules.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/skills
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Full skills reference — SKILL.md format, complete frontmatter table, scopes/precedence, substitutions, invocation control, lifecycle, skillOverrides, listing budget.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Version history for nested-skill loading (2.1.178), stacked skill invocation (2.1.199), frontmatter kebab/snake/camelCase (2.1.181), malformed-YAML handling (2.1.181), duplicate-context fix (2.1.217).
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  Used heavily as the engine's primary command surface. 13 project-scope skills under
  .claude/skills/*/SKILL.md (engine-board-setup, engine-conduct, engine-design, engine-help,
  engine-parts, engine-recall, engine-routine, engine-setup, engine-start, engine-status,
  engine-tune, engine-upgrade). Real platform frontmatter fields in use: `name`, `description`,
  `disable-model-invocation: true` (all operator-typed skills), and `allowed-tools` scoped to
  narrow Bash globs (e.g. engine-routine "Bash(uv run *)", engine-board-setup "Bash(gh *),
  Bash(uv run *)"). engine-recall omits disable-model-invocation so it stays model-invocable.
overlap: >
  Skills are the engine's chosen command mechanism in place of .claude/commands/ (see
  custom-commands record). Skill bodies are thin entrypoints that defer to .engine/operations/*
  runbooks.
note: >
  The engine adds a NON-STANDARD frontmatter field `invocation: operator-typed | model-auto` to
  every skill (e.g. engine-routine L5, engine-recall L3). The platform does not define
  `invocation`; the actual enforcement is the documented `disable-model-invocation: true`. So the
  engine carries a hand-authored annotation alongside the real control — and codex_gen.py reads
  that pair to drive the Codex twin's allow_implicit_invocation (see codex/skills record). Low
  risk (the platform ignores the extra key) but it is redundant metadata layered on a documented
  field.
```

**Assessment:** action mode advises; enforcement advisory; failure mode open; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `CORE`** — The engine's primary command surface (13 skills, correct use of disable-model-invocation/allowed-tools); the extra engine-private `invocation:` key feeds the Codex twin generator.


## Family: subagents

### `claude/subagents/agent-teams`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/agent-teams
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Agent teams
what: Multiple Claude Code instances coordinating as a team — a lead session plus teammates that each run in their own context, share a task list, and message each other directly. Unlike subagents, teammates communicate peer-to-peer and can be addressed individually. Experimental, disabled by default.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Enable with CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS=1 (shell or settings.json env). A team forms when the
  first teammate spawns (v2.1.178+ no setup step; TeamCreate/TeamDelete tools removed and the Agent tool's
  team_name input is now ignored). You request teammates in natural language or Claude proposes them (always
  with your confirmation). Architecture: team lead (agent type team-lead) + teammates + shared task list +
  mailbox. Storage under session-derived name (session-<first 8 chars of session id>): team config at
  ~/.claude/teams/{team}/config.json (holds runtime state, removed at session end), mailboxes at
  ~/.claude/teams/{team}/inboxes/{agent}.json, task list at ~/.claude/tasks/{team}/ (persists, never
  uploaded, retention via cleanupPeriodDays). No project-level team config is recognized. Display modes:
  in-process (default; agent panel below prompt, ↑/↓ select, Enter view/message, Esc interrupt, x stop) or
  split-panes (requires tmux or iTerm2 with it2); set via teammateMode setting or --teammate-mode.
  Teammates don't inherit the lead's /model by default (set Default teammate model in /config) but do inherit
  the lead's effort. Task list has pending/in-progress/completed states with dependencies and file-locked
  claiming. Optional plan-approval gating (teammate stays read-only until lead approves). Quality-gate hooks:
  TeammateIdle, TaskCreated, TaskCompleted (exit 2 to block/feedback). Teammates start with the lead's
  permissions; a relayed approval from another agent is untrusted, never consent. A subagent definition can
  back a teammate (its `tools` and `model` apply, body appended to system prompt; SendMessage and task tools
  always available; `skills` and `mcpServers` frontmatter NOT applied). Limits: one team per session, no
  nested teams, lead fixed for life, no in-process-teammate session resumption, no background subagents from
  in-process teammates. Uses significantly more tokens than a single session; 3-5 teammates recommended.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-teams
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Full agent-teams page — enablement, architecture/storage paths, display modes, task list, permissions, subagent-definition teammates, limitations.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Cross-reference that subagent definitions from any scope are reusable as teammates; extra task/cron tools teammates keep.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.178 TeamCreate/TeamDelete removal + implicit team; v2.1.196 failed-teammate reporting.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  CLAUDE_CODE_EXPERIMENTAL_AGENT_TEAMS is never set; no team config. The engine's multi-agent design is
  a lead session delegating to independent read-only review personas (subagents that report and return),
  never peer-to-peer teammates sharing a task list/mailbox.
note: >
  The record notes teammate-path caveats (skills/mcpServers frontmatter not applied) that are moot here
  since the engine uses neither field and never runs a persona as a teammate.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — Experimental peer-to-peer teams; the engine's delegation is flat one-shot personas.

### `claude/subagents/agent-view`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/agent-view
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Agent view (background sessions)
what: A terminal screen (claude agents) for dispatching and managing many independent background Claude Code sessions from one place — distinct from subagents (which live inside one session) and agent teams (which message each other). Research preview.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Sessions are hosted by a per-user supervisor process that starts automatically on first `claude --bg` or
  `claude agents`, keeps a pre-warmed worker pool, and keeps sessions running with no terminal attached.
  Launch: `claude agents` (optionally --cwd/--model/--effort/--permission-mode/--agent/--json); shell
  `claude --bg "<task>"` (with --name, --model, --exec for shell jobs); in-session /background sends the
  current conversation to background and /fork copies it (v2.1.212+). Dispatch input: <prompt> for a new
  session, @<agent> or code-reviewer <prompt> to run a named subagent as the main agent, ! <cmd> for a shell
  job, filters a:/s:/#PR/URL. States: working, needs input, idle, completed, failed, stopped. Idle sessions
  stop after ~1h (state persists to disk and restarts on reattach); detaching never stops a session
  (`claude stop <id>` or Ctrl+X does). Management CLI: claude stop/attach/logs/rm/respawn <id>,
  claude daemon status|stop. State under ~/.claude/: daemon/roster.json, jobs/<id>/state.json, jobs/<id>/tmp/.
  Config: disableAgentView:true or CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AGENT_VIEW=1 turns it off. The default agent for an
  unnamed dispatch is the built-in `claude`. (Worktree-based isolation of background edits is owned by a
  separate surface family; noted here only as a seam.)
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-view
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Full agent-view page — supervisor lifecycle, dispatch/shortcuts, state storage paths, management CLI, disable config.
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: `claude` built-in as the default unnamed-dispatch background agent; Note distinguishing subagents from background agents and teams.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
overlap: >

  (.claude/skills/engine-routine/SKILL.md, disable-model-invocation:true, allowed-tools: Bash(uv run *))
  driving .engine/operations/routine-entry.md, explicitly placed inside a Claude Desktop routine or a
  Codex Automation — not the `claude agents` / `claude --bg` background-session view.
note: >
```

### `claude/subagents/built-in-agents`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/built-in-agents
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Built-in subagents
what: Claude Code ships subagents Claude uses automatically. Named ones are Explore (fast read-only codebase search), Plan (research during plan mode), and general-purpose (multi-step work with full tool access). Helper agents include statusline-setup, claude-code-guide, and claude (the default background/dispatch agent).
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: vendor-managed
persistence: none
mechanics: >
  Registered by default in interactive sessions; each inherits parent permissions plus tool restrictions.
  Explore: read-only tools (Write/Edit denied), invoked with a thoroughness level (quick/medium/very
  thorough); inherits the main conversation's model capped at Opus on the Claude API (v2.1.198+ — previously
  always Haiku). Plan: read-only, inherits model. general-purpose: all subagent-available tools, inherits
  model. Explore and Plan alone skip CLAUDE.md and git status to stay cheap, and are one-shot (return no
  agent ID, cannot be resumed). Helper table: statusline-setup (Sonnet, via /statusline), claude-code-guide
  (Haiku, feature Q&A), claude (inherits, default background-session/dispatch agent). A user/project subagent
  named `Explore` overrides the built-in and keeps its own `model`. Restriction: add
  `Agent(<name>)` to permissions.deny to block one; deny the `Agent` tool to block all delegation;
  CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_EXPLORE_PLAN_AGENTS=1 removes Explore+Plan (v2.1.198+);
  CLAUDE_AGENT_SDK_DISABLE_BUILTIN_AGENTS=1 removes all built-ins in headless/SDK.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Built-in subagents tab set (Explore/Plan/general-purpose/Other), model/tool profiles, disable mechanisms.

    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.198 Explore model-inheritance change.
```

### `claude/subagents/custom-definitions`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/custom-definitions
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Custom subagents
what: Specialized AI assistants defined as Markdown files with YAML frontmatter, each running in its own context window with a custom system prompt, tool access, and independent permissions. Claude delegates a matching task to the subagent, which works and returns only a summary. Defined once and reused across sessions and projects.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Files live at .claude/agents/<name>.md (project), ~/.claude/agents/<name>.md (user), a plugin's
  agents/ directory, managed-settings .claude/agents/ (org), or passed as JSON via the --agents CLI flag
  (session-only, same fields keyed by name with `prompt` for the body). Precedence when names collide,
  highest to lowest: managed settings > --agents flag > project > user > plugin. Project agents are
  discovered by walking up from cwd; nested definitions closest to cwd win (v2.1.178+). Directories are
  scanned recursively; identity comes only from the `name` field, not path. Frontmatter fields: `name`
  and `description` (required); optional `tools`, `disallowedTools`, `model`, `permissionMode`, `maxTurns`,
  `skills`, `mcpServers`, `hooks`, `memory`, `background`, `effort`, `isolation`, `color`, `initialPrompt`.
  The Markdown body is the system prompt (subagents get only this plus environment details, not the full
  Claude Code system prompt). Files under ~/.claude/agents and .claude/agents are hot-watched (change picked
  up within seconds, no restart) except for a scope's first file in a newly created agents directory.
  Plugin subagents ignore `hooks`, `mcpServers`, and `permissionMode` for security. `isolation: worktree`
  runs the subagent in a temporary git worktree (worktree mechanics owned by a separate surface family;
  noted here only as a seam). `name` cannot contain `:` (reserved for plugin scoping) as of v2.1.218.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: File format, scopes/precedence table, full supported-frontmatter-fields table, discovery/watch rules, --agents flag, managed/plugin subagents.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.218 name-colon restriction; v2.1.212 Task tool `mode` param deprecation.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  Used. 10 project-scope subagents at /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.claude/agents/*.md
  (engine-audit, 4 engine-design-review-*, 5 engine-qa-review-*). Each is a Markdown file with YAML
  frontmatter + prose body-as-system-prompt, exactly the documented file format. Of the documented
  frontmatter field set the engine uses only: name, description, model, effort, disallowedTools.
  It uses NONE of: tools (allowlist), permissionMode, maxTurns, skills, mcpServers, hooks, memory,
  background, isolation, color, initialPrompt. Only the project scope is used — no user-scope

  Agent frontmatter shape is schema-enforced by .engine/schemas/agent.v1.json.
note: >
  The engine adds five NON-platform frontmatter fields Claude Code does not read — role, lens, model-tier,
  permissions, output-contract — carried purely for engine machinery (bindings/routing/output shape).
```

### `claude/subagents/foreground-background-execution`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/foreground-background-execution
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Foreground and background subagent execution
what: A subagent runs either in the foreground (blocking the main conversation) or the background (concurrent while you keep working). Background is the default as of v2.1.198; the `background` frontmatter field can force it always.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  Foreground subagents block and pass permission prompts through as they arise. Background subagents run
  concurrently; a background permission prompt surfaces in the main session naming the subagent, approvable
  or Esc-deniable per tool call (v2.1.186+; before that background prompts auto-denied). As of v2.1.198
  subagents default to background; Claude runs one in the foreground only when it needs the result before
  continuing. Background subagents run with the reduced built-in tool set (see tool-access-control record).
  A background subagent's results reach Claude as a completion notification in a later turn; Claude reports
  "still running" if asked before it arrives (v2.1.211+). Steering: ask Claude to run fore/background, or
  press Ctrl+B to background a running task. `background: true` frontmatter forces background even when the
  result is needed immediately. Completed background subagents stay in /tasks marked done (v2.1.208+).
  CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS=1 disables all background task functionality (takes precedence over
  fork mode). Not available for an in-process agent-team teammate's own subagents (they run foreground).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Run-subagents-in-foreground-or-background section, background-by-default, permission surfacing, Ctrl+B, disable env var.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.198 background-by-default; v2.1.186 permission surfacing; v2.1.199 API-error reporting.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
  The `background` frontmatter field is set on no agent; no CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS or
  CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT in-repo. Foreground/background is left to platform default.
note: none
```

### `claude/subagents/fork-conversation`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/fork-conversation
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Fork the current conversation
what: A fork is a subagent that inherits the entire conversation so far — same system prompt, tools, model, and message history — instead of starting fresh, letting you hand off a side task without re-explaining context while keeping the fork's tool calls out of the main window.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  Started with /subtask <task> (v2.1.212+; named /fork on v2.1.161-2.1.211). When agent view is off,
  /subtask is unavailable and /fork starts the forked subagent; otherwise /fork copies the whole session into
  a new background session. Forks skip both subagent tool filters and receive the parent's exact tool pool
  (Agent stays listed but errors at the depth limit). Claude can spawn a fork by requesting the `fork`
  subagent type. CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT=1 forces fork mode on (in interactive, headless, and SDK): every
  subagent then runs in the background and the frontmatter `background` field has no effect (fork mode removes
  the run_in_background parameter); =0 disables fork mode everywhere. CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_BACKGROUND_TASKS
  takes precedence and keeps subagents synchronous. A fork reuses the parent's prompt cache (cheaper than a
  fresh subagent) and can be spawned with isolation:"worktree". A fork cannot spawn further forks. Fork's own
  output style is shared with main (unlike named subagents). Panel keys: ↑/↓ move, Enter open transcript,
  x dismiss/stop, Esc return to prompt.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Fork-the-current-conversation section, /subtask vs /fork history, fork-vs-named-subagent table, CLAUDE_CODE_FORK_SUBAGENT behavior.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: none
note: none
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — No engine use for conversation forks.

### `claude/subagents/lifecycle-hooks`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/lifecycle-hooks
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Subagent hooks
what: Subagents can carry lifecycle hooks that fire during their run, defined either in the subagent's own frontmatter (scoped to that subagent) or in settings.json responding to subagent start/stop events in the main session. Lets teams enforce quality gates such as validate-before-Bash or lint-after-edit.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Frontmatter `hooks` supports all hook events; common ones are PreToolUse (matcher=tool name), PostToolUse
  (matcher=tool name), and Stop (auto-converted to SubagentStop when running as a subagent). Each hook entry
  is type=command with a command string; input arrives as JSON on stdin, exit code 2 blocks. Frontmatter
  hooks also fire when the agent runs as the main session via --agent. Session-wide events in settings.json:
  SubagentStart (matcher=agent type name, fires when a subagent begins) and SubagentStop (fires when it
  completes); matchers are the frontmatter `name` (or plugin-scoped id, an unanchored regex needing ^…$).
  Exact hyphenated matching for SubagentStart/Stop requires v2.1.195+. Settings, managed-policy, and plugin
  hooks all fire inside subagents. Project-level frontmatter hooks require accepting the workspace-trust
  dialog for the folder (v2.1.218+); user-level and --agents hooks don't. Plugin subagents ignore the
  frontmatter `hooks` field.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Define-hooks-for-subagents section, frontmatter vs settings.json events, SubagentStart/Stop, workspace-trust requirement.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  The engine uses hooks heavily, but only the settings.json event-family form, never the subagent
  frontmatter `hooks` field: .claude/settings.json wires SessionStart/PreToolUse/PostToolUse/Stop/
  UserPromptSubmit/PreCompact via .engine/tools/hook-runner.sh, mirrored on Codex in .codex/hooks.json
  via codex-hook-runner.sh.
note: >
  The engine also does not use the session-level SubagentStart / SubagentStop events — no per-subagent
  lifecycle gating exists in settings.json. Subagent quality gating is achieved by the personas' own
  prose mandates, not hooks.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — Per-subagent frontmatter hooks and SubagentStart/Stop events unused; persona conduct lives in mandates, gates in session hooks.

### `claude/subagents/model-effort-selection`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/model-effort-selection
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Subagent model and effort selection
what: A subagent can run on a chosen model and reasoning-effort level distinct from the main conversation, via the `model` and `effort` frontmatter fields, letting teams route cheap work to Haiku and hard work to Opus.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  `model` accepts an alias (sonnet, opus, haiku, fable), a full model ID (e.g. claude-opus-5), or `inherit`;
  default is `inherit` (main conversation's model). Resolution order: (1) CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL env var
  if set to an alias/ID; (2) the per-invocation `model` parameter Claude passes when spawning; (3) the
  definition's `model` frontmatter; (4) the main conversation's model. As of v2.1.196 setting
  CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL=inherit is the same as unset (falls through to steps 2-3); earlier it forced the
  main model. All resolved values are checked against the org `availableModels` allowlist; an excluded value
  is skipped and the inherited model used. A per-invocation model also sticks across resume/follow-up
  (v2.1.211+). `effort` sets the level while the subagent is active (low, medium, high, xhigh, max; available
  levels depend on model), overriding session effort; default inherits from session. Subagents also inherit
  the session's extended-thinking on/off state (v2.1.198+); there is no per-subagent thinking setting.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Choose-a-model section with four-step resolution order, availableModels filtering, effort field, thinking inheritance.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.196 inherit-equals-unset; v2.1.198/2.1.201 extended-thinking inheritance; v2.1.206 provider model defaults.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  Used. Every agent carries model + effort frontmatter, machine-stamped by
  .engine/tools/agent_bindings.py from .engine/policies/model-bindings.json (tiers judgment→opus/high,
  mechanical→haiku/low; per-persona overrides sonnet/high for spec-conformance, technical-integrity,
  usability). Current stamped state: model opus×7 / sonnet×3, effort=high on all 10. Values are bare
  aliases only (opus/sonnet/haiku) — never a full model ID, never `inherit`.
overlap: >
  model-routing.md + execution-state.v1.json describe the posture that governs whether bindings apply;
  agent_bindings.py check() (backing test_agent_bindings.py) is the CI drift gate. Codex mirror:
  codex_gen.py maps the same tier/effort to model_reasoning_effort.
note: >
  The engine uses only resolution step (3), the definition's frontmatter. It never sets
  CLAUDE_CODE_SUBAGENT_MODEL in-repo (step 1), passes no per-invocation model (step 2), and relies on
  no availableModels allowlist. Bindings schema (.engine/schemas/model-bindings.v1.json) restricts
  effort to enum low/medium/high — so the platform's xhigh/max effort levels are structurally
  unreachable through the engine (see models/anthropic/effort-capability-tier).
```

**Assessment:** action mode advises; enforcement advisory; failure mode open; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `CORE`** — Alias+effort stamping from model-bindings is the engine's routing layer over the native fields; effort vocabulary capped by schema (M4 widens it).

### `claude/subagents/nesting-and-spawn-limits`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/nesting-and-spawn-limits
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Nested subagents and spawn limits
what: Subagents can spawn their own subagents up to a depth limit, governed together with per-session and concurrency caps that keep one message from fanning out unbounded agents.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  Three independent limits. Depth: default 3 layers below the main conversation (v2.1.219+), set by
  CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH (1 = nesting off); at the limit the Agent tool is withheld from every
  subagent except a fork (a fork keeps it listed but it errors). History: v2.1.172-2.1.216 nested up to 5
  layers uncapped; v2.1.217-2.1.218 defaulted to 1. Session total: default 200 spawns
  (CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENTS_PER_SESSION, positive int, no upper bound, cannot be disabled, v2.1.212+);
  counts nested, fork, background, and workflow-agent-spawned subagents; /subtask in-session forks count but
  are not blocked; /fork background sessions do not count; reaching it fails with "Subagent spawn limit
  reached"; /clear resets. Concurrency: default 20 running (CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS, v2.1.217+);
  spawning a 21st fails with "Concurrent subagent limit reached"; ultracode sessions are exempt; /subtask
  forks and subagent resumes take slots without the check. Nested subagents resolve from the same scopes as
  top-level ones; the subagent panel shows the tree with a (+N) descendant count.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Let-subagents-spawn-their-own-subagents, session-subagent-limit, and concurrent-subagent-limit sections with all three env vars and defaults.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.216/2.1.217/2.1.219 depth default changes; v2.1.212 session cap; v2.1.217 concurrency cap.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  None. The engine sets none of CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_SUBAGENT_SPAWN_DEPTH / MAX_SUBAGENTS_PER_SESSION /
  MAX_CONCURRENT_SUBAGENTS anywhere in the repo. Its review personas are spawned as a flat fan-out by
  the orchestrator, not nested.
note: >
  The user's MEMORY ("probe before fan-out") is a harness-side discipline, not a repo configuration —
  engine-template commits no spawn-limit tuning.
```

**Assessment:** action mode observes; enforcement none — signal only; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `OBSERVATION ONLY`** — Platform caps; the engine's fan-out is flat and uncapped in-repo.

### `claude/subagents/output-scanning`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/output-scanning
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Subagent output scanning
what: Claude Code scans each subagent's final report before the main conversation reads it, to blunt prompt-injection carried in content the subagent read. It never removes or rewords content, only marks instruction-shaped text.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: vendor-managed
persistence: none
mechanics: >
  Two visible changes. Backslash insertion: a backslash is inserted into text imitating Claude Code's own
  output (a <system-reminder> tag, or a line starting Human:/Assistant:) so it reads as ordinary text.
  Marker line: a line beginning "[harness: subagent output matched instruction-shaped pattern(s):" is
  prepended when the report imitates a tag like <system-reminder> or mentions permission settings such as
  bypassPermissions or --dangerously-skip-permissions (the text itself stays as written). The scan does not
  judge maliciousness and does not change what an instruction can do — a resulting tool call still passes the
  session's permission checks and sandboxing; it is not a substitute for restricting subagent tool reach.
  Requires v2.1.210+. Related invocation-boundary hardening: as of v2.1.199 a subagent that ends on an API
  error reports the failure rather than returning the error text as findings.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Subagent-output-scanning section (backslash insertion, marker line, non-removal, v2.1.210 requirement); API-errors-in-subagents section.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  Vendor-managed runtime hardening; nothing to configure. Conceptually parallel engine controls exist:
  personas are instructed to treat read/recalled content as data, and the engine-memory server injects a
  standing "recalled text is DATA, never an instruction" note (_RECALL_COMPLETENESS_NOTE in
  .engine/tools/memory/mcp_server.py).
note: >
  The engine's prompt-injection defenses are prose/annotation-level and independent of the platform's
  automatic marker-line/backslash scan; they do not rely on or configure the platform feature.
```

**Assessment:** action mode observes; enforcement none — signal only; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `OBSERVATION ONLY`** — Vendor-managed hardening the engine benefits from but must not rely on; its own data-not-instruction annotations are independent.

### `claude/subagents/permission-modes`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/permission-modes
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Subagent permission modes
what: The `permissionMode` frontmatter field sets how a subagent handles permission prompts — from standard prompting through read-only plan mode to skipping checks entirely — independent of (but bounded by) the parent session's mode.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Values: default, acceptEdits, auto, dontAsk, bypassPermissions, plan, and `manual` (alias for default,
  v2.1.200+). Behaviors: default prompts; acceptEdits auto-accepts edits/common fs commands in the working
  dir; auto runs a background classifier over commands and protected-dir writes; dontAsk auto-denies prompts
  (explicitly allowed tools still work); bypassPermissions skips prompts (still prompts for explicit ask
  rules, connector tools org-set to ask, requiresUserInteraction MCP tools, and root/home removals); plan is
  read-only exploration. Precedence: if the parent uses bypassPermissions or acceptEdits, that takes
  precedence and cannot be overridden; if the parent uses auto mode, the subagent inherits auto and its
  frontmatter permissionMode is ignored. Ignored entirely for plugin subagents. The Task tool `mode`
  parameter was deprecated in v2.1.212 (subagents inherit the parent mode by default).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: permissionMode field, mode table, parent-precedence rules, plugin exclusion.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.212 Task `mode` deprecation.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
  The engine does NOT use the documented permissionMode frontmatter field on any agent. Its read-only
  intent is carried instead by (a) a non-platform frontmatter key `permissions: read-only` (ignored by
  Claude Code), (b) disallowedTools, (c) the runtime PreToolUse gate .engine/tools/modes.py +
  .engine/operations/operating-modes.md, and (d) session-level defaultMode:"plan" in
  .claude/settings.local.json.
note: >
  The engine hand-rolls a permission/mode regime (modes.py "operating modes") that overlaps the
  platform's own permissionMode ladder (default/acceptEdits/auto/dontAsk/bypassPermissions/plan) but is
  a separate, engine-owned mechanism rather than the documented per-subagent field.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — Per-subagent permissionMode is unused; read-only intent is carried by denylists and the runtime gate, uniform across runtimes.

### `claude/subagents/persistent-memory`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/persistent-memory
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Subagent persistent memory
what: The `memory` frontmatter field gives a subagent a persistent directory that survives across conversations, so it can accumulate knowledge (codebase patterns, debugging insights) over time.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  `memory` scope is user, project, or local. Locations: user → ~/.claude/agent-memory/<name>/;
  project → .claude/agent-memory/<name>/ (shareable via version control); local →
  .claude/agent-memory-local/<name>/ (project-specific, not committed). When enabled, the subagent's system
  prompt gains read/write-to-memory instructions plus the first 200 lines or 25KB of MEMORY.md, and
  Read/Write/Edit tools are auto-enabled. It is part of auto memory: turning auto memory off
  (autoMemoryEnabled setting or CLAUDE_CODE_DISABLE_AUTO_MEMORY) disables the field entirely. Project is the
  recommended default scope.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Enable-persistent-memory section, scope/location table, auto-memory dependency, injected MEMORY.md limits.
```

### `claude/subagents/preload-skills`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/preload-skills
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Preload skills into subagents
what: The `skills` frontmatter field injects the full content of named skills into a subagent's context at startup, giving it domain knowledge without run-time discovery.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Lists skill names; the full skill content (not just the description) is injected at startup. This controls
  preloading only, not access — without it a subagent can still discover and invoke project/user/plugin
  skills through the Skill tool. To block skills entirely, omit Skill from `tools` or add it to
  disallowedTools. Cannot preload skills with disable-model-invocation:true, including the bundled /verify
  and /code-review skills (v2.1.215+). Missing/disabled listed skills are skipped with a debug-log warning.
  Inverse of a skill's context:fork. Note: the `skills` field is not applied when the definition runs as an
  agent-team teammate.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Preload-skills-into-subagents section, injection vs access distinction, disable-model-invocation exclusion.

    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Note that `skills` and `mcpServers` frontmatter are not applied on the teammate path.
```

### `claude/subagents/resume-subagents`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/resume-subagents
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Resume subagents
what: A completed or stopped subagent can be continued with its full prior context (all tool calls, results, reasoning) instead of starting a fresh instance, by sending it a message addressed to its agent ID or name.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  When a subagent completes, Claude receives its agent ID. Claude resumes via the SendMessage tool with the
  agent's ID or name as `to`; the completed/stopped subagent auto-resumes in the background with no new Agent
  invocation. SendMessage does not require agent teams to be enabled (only structured team-protocol messages
  like shutdown_request do). Built-in Explore and Plan are one-shot (no agent ID, not resumable — use
  general-purpose or a custom subagent). A subagent you stopped yourself (x in /tasks or SDK stop_task) does
  not auto-resume; SendMessage refuses until you type into its transcript (v2.1.191+). SendMessage verifies a
  name still refers to the same agent and refuses if a newer agent took the name, reporting the current
  target (v2.1.199+); check is per-conversation and resets on /clear. A subagent treats messages from the
  agent that launched it as normal task direction including course corrections (v2.1.198+), but no agent
  message grants a pending permission approval or changes another subagent's permission settings/CLAUDE.md/
  config. Transcripts persist per-session in ~/.claude/projects/{project}/{sessionId}/subagents/ as
  agent-{agentId}.jsonl, unaffected by main-conversation compaction, deleted after cleanupPeriodDays
  (30 default). Subagents support auto-compaction with the same logic as the main conversation.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Resume-subagents section, SendMessage semantics, transcript storage/cleanup, launcher-message rule, auto-compaction.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.203 resumed-background-agent restoration fix; v2.1.196 stuck-teammate wake.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: none
note: >
  Pure runtime behavior (SendMessage / transcript persistence); nothing in engine-template configures
  or depends on subagent resume. The personas are one-shot report-and-return by design.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `REJECT`** — Personas are one-shot report-and-return by design.

### `claude/subagents/scoped-mcp-servers`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/scoped-mcp-servers
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Subagent-scoped MCP servers
what: The `mcpServers` frontmatter field gives a subagent access to MCP servers that need not exist in the main conversation, either inline definitions (connected only while the subagent runs) or references to already-configured servers.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Each list entry is an inline server definition (same schema as .mcp.json entries, keyed by server name,
  types stdio/http/sse/ws; connected at subagent start, disconnected at finish) or a string referencing an
  already-configured server (shares the parent connection). Applies both when the file runs as a subagent and
  when it runs as the main session via --agent. Defining a server inline here keeps its tool descriptions out
  of the main conversation's context. As of v2.1.153, main-session MCP restrictions also cover
  frontmatter-declared servers: --strict-mcp-config, --bare, enterprise managed MCP config, and
  allowedMcpServers/deniedMcpServers policies; blocked servers are skipped with a warning. --strict-mcp-config
  does not filter servers passed inline via --agents or the SDK agents option (treated as explicit caller
  input). Ignored for plugin subagents, and not applied on the agent-team teammate path.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Scope-MCP-servers-to-a-subagent section, inline vs reference, v2.1.153 restriction coverage.
```

```yaml
note: >
  Because the 2 engine servers load session-wide, their tool descriptions are always in the main
  context — the record's "keep tool descriptions out of the main conversation" benefit of inline

```

### `claude/subagents/session-agent-mode`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/session-agent-mode
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Run a session as a subagent (--agent)
what: A whole Claude Code session can adopt a subagent's system prompt, tool restrictions, and model as its main thread, via the --agent CLI flag or the `agent` setting, rather than the subagent running only as a delegated helper.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  `claude --agent <name>` starts a session whose main thread takes on that subagent's system prompt (which
  replaces the default Claude Code system prompt entirely, like --system-prompt), tool restrictions, and
  model; CLAUDE.md and project memory still load. Works with built-in and custom subagents; plugin agents by
  name, or scoped name (my-plugin:security-reviewer, my-plugin:review:security) to disambiguate. The agent
  name shows as @<name> in the startup header. The choice persists across resume (system prompt, tools, model
  restored); if the agent no longer exists on resume, the session continues with defaults and warns. Set
  project-wide default with `"agent": "<name>"` in .claude/settings.json; the CLI flag overrides the setting.
  In this mode the `initialPrompt` frontmatter field is auto-submitted as the first user turn (commands and
  skills processed, prepended to any user prompt), the `Agent(agent_type)` tools allowlist governs which
  types the main thread may spawn, and frontmatter hooks/mcpServers run alongside settings.json ones.
  --append-subagent-system-prompt (headless, v2.1.205+) appends text to every subagent's system prompt.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Invoke-subagents-explicitly (Run the whole session as a subagent), --agent flag and `agent` setting, initialPrompt field, resume persistence.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: >
```

### `claude/subagents/tool-access-control`

```yaml
id: claude/subagents/tool-access-control
provider: claude
family: subagents
name: Subagent tool restrictions
what: Per-subagent control over which tools it may use, via an allowlist (`tools`) and a denylist (`disallowedTools`), plus `Agent(type)` syntax to restrict which subagent types a main-thread agent may spawn. Lets a team enforce constraints such as read-only research agents.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: mixed
persistence: committed
mechanics: >
  Subagents inherit the main conversation's built-in and MCP tools, then two filters apply. Filter one
  always removes: Agent (at depth limit), AskUserQuestion, EndConversation, EnterPlanMode, ExitPlanMode
  (unless permissionMode plan), ScheduleWakeup, TaskOutput, WaitForMcpServers, Workflow. Filter two applies
  to background subagents (the default), which keep all MCP tools but only these built-ins: Read, Grep, Glob,
  Bash, PowerShell, Edit, Write, NotebookEdit, WebFetch, WebSearch, TodoWrite, Skill, ToolSearch,
  EnterWorktree, ExitWorktree, Monitor, TaskStop, SendMessage, Artifact. So one definition can resolve to
  different tools in foreground vs background. Forks skip both filters. `tools` is an allowlist;
  `disallowedTools` a denylist; if both set, disallowedTools applies first. Both accept MCP patterns
  (mcp__<server>, mcp__<server>__*, and mcp__* in disallowedTools). If nothing in `tools` resolves,
  the subagent refuses to launch with an error naming the entries (v2.1.208+). `Agent(worker,researcher)`
  in `tools` allowlists which types a `--agent` main thread may spawn; `Agent` alone allows any; omitting
  Agent forbids spawning. In a subagent definition the parenthesized type list is ignored (only presence of
  Agent matters). Permission-rule variant `Agent(model:opus)` (v2.1.178+) can block by input parameter.
  Task tool was renamed Agent in v2.1.63; Task(...) still aliases.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sub-agents
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Available-tools two-filter model, tools/disallowedTools semantics, Agent(agent_type) restriction, zero-tools failure.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: v2.1.63 Task→Agent rename; v2.1.178 Tool(param:value) syntax; v2.1.186/2.1.193 Agent(type) deny-rule enforcement fixes.
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: >
  Partially used — only the disallowedTools denylist leg. All 10 agents set disallowedTools:
  [Edit, Write, NotebookEdit, Bash] (engine-audit + the 4 engine-design-review-*) or
  [Edit, Write, NotebookEdit] (the 5 engine-qa-review-*, which keep Bash). The engine never uses the
  `tools` allowlist, never uses Agent(type) spawn restriction, and never uses MCP patterns
  (mcp__*) in these lists.
overlap: >
  The read-only stance the denylist expresses is also enforced at runtime by the PreToolUse gate
  .engine/tools/modes.py (which additionally denies apply_patch — the Codex edit tool — per
  providers.py), and independently by the codex_gen-rendered "Permissions floor: read-only" +
  "Do not run shell commands" routing lines injected into each .codex/agents/*.toml developer_instructions.
note: >
  Per the record's two-filter model, a background subagent already loses Edit/Write/NotebookEdit, so
  the denylist is partly redundant in background execution and load-bearing only in foreground; the
  engine does not distinguish the two. The engine expresses read-only three separate ways (frontmatter
  denylist, non-platform `permissions: read-only`, runtime modes.py gate) rather than one.
```

**Assessment:** action mode blocks/intercepts; enforcement deterministic where the platform honors it; the engine treats it as best-effort; failure mode open; portability provider-specific behind the engine's adapter seam.

**Disposition: `CORE`** — The disallowedTools denylist leg is one of three stacked read-only enforcements (with the runtime gate and Codex sandbox_mode) — deliberate redundancy.


## Family: worktrees-isolation

### `claude/worktrees-isolation/background-session-isolation`

```yaml
id: claude/worktrees-isolation/background-session-isolation
provider: claude
family: worktrees-isolation
name: Background-session worktree isolation (worktree.bgIsolation)
what: Background sessions automatically move into an isolated git worktree before editing files so parallel background sessions do not collide; the behavior can be disabled by setting. This is the worktree-isolation half of the background-sessions capability.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  A background session starts in the working directory and, before its first file edit,
  moves into `.claude/worktrees/<name>`. Multiple background sessions can read the same
  checkout while each edits its own worktree. Isolation is skipped when already in a linked
  worktree, when the directory is not a git repo (unless a `WorktreeCreate` hook is set), or
  for writes outside the repo. Set `worktree.bgIsolation: "none"` in `.claude/settings.json`
  to disable it. Removed via the same periodic sweep as subagent worktrees. Changelog fixes
  address symlink-canonicalization escape (2.1.217) and undeletable no-git worktrees (2.1.216).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/agent-view
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents automatic pre-edit worktree isolation for background sessions, the skip conditions, and the worktree.bgIsolation:"none" setting.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Records symlink-escape fix (2.1.217) and undeletable no-git background worktree fix (2.1.216).
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml

overlap: none
note: No `worktree.bgIsolation` setting; background sessions are not used (see background-sessions).
```

### `claude/worktrees-isolation/base-ref-setting`

```yaml
id: claude/worktrees-isolation/base-ref-setting
provider: claude
family: worktrees-isolation
name: worktree.baseRef setting
what: A setting that controls which ref new worktrees branch from — the remote default branch or the local HEAD. It applies to --worktree and subagent worktrees.
surfaces: [cli, desktop]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  `worktree.baseRef` in settings accepts `"fresh"` (default — branch from the remote default
  branch, usually `main`) or `"head"` (branch from current local HEAD, carrying unpushed
  commits; inside a worktree resolves to that worktree's HEAD). It cannot be set to a branch
  name. For `"fresh"`, Claude Code keeps `origin/HEAD` current by fetching the default branch
  (capped at five seconds) when the repo hasn't been fetched in 24 hours, falling back to the
  cached ref or local HEAD (2.1.208+). Reuse of a worktree name always reopens at the old tip
  when baseRef is `"head"`.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/worktrees
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents the two accepted values, the branch-name restriction, origin/HEAD freshness handling, and interaction with name reuse.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Version-gates the fresh-base fetch behavior (2.1.208).
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: unused
overlap: none
note: No `worktree.baseRef` key in either settings file; the default "fresh" behavior is accepted implicitly.
```

**Assessment:** action mode n/a; enforcement none — not in use; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

### `claude/worktrees-isolation/cleanup-and-sweep`

```yaml
id: claude/worktrees-isolation/cleanup-and-sweep
provider: claude
family: worktrees-isolation
name: Worktree cleanup, periodic sweep, and locking
what: Claude cleans up worktrees it created on session exit and via a periodic age-based sweep, and locks a worktree while its agent runs so concurrent cleanup cannot remove it. Worktrees holding uncommitted or unpushed work are preserved.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  On interactive exit Claude checks for changed/untracked files and new commits: a clean
  unnamed session's worktree and branch are removed automatically; a clean named session
  prompts first; a worktree with work prompts to keep or remove. `-p` runs have no exit
  prompt and are not cleaned up (remove with `git worktree remove`). A periodic sweep
  removes subagent and background-session worktrees older than the `cleanupPeriodDays`
  setting, skipping any with changed/untracked files or unpushed commits, and never removes
  `--worktree` worktrees. While an agent runs Claude sets `git worktree lock`; the lock
  releases when the agent finishes, and the sweep also releases locks for exited/killed
  session processes (2.1.210+) but never a lock you set yourself. On Windows, removal
  deletes only a link (NTFS junction/dir symlink), not the target (2.1.205+/2.1.207 fixes).
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/worktrees
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents exit-time cleanup prompts, the cleanupPeriodDays-gated periodic sweep, git worktree lock/unlock behavior, and Windows link-deletion behavior.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Records lock-release-on-killed-session (2.1.210), NTFS-junction removal fix (2.1.207), and undeletable no-git background worktree fix (2.1.216).
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: Relied on by default only. The engine leaves worktree cleanup to the platform's exit/sweep behavior and sets no `cleanupPeriodDays`. checkout_health.py / first_run_health.py read `git worktree list` but do not remove worktrees.
overlap: none configured.
note: 11 worktrees are checked out at cdbbc335; the engine neither drives nor tunes the native sweep.
```

**Assessment:** action mode observes; enforcement none — signal only; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.

**Disposition: `OBSERVATION ONLY`** — Left at platform defaults; the engine reads but never drives cleanup.

### `claude/worktrees-isolation/enter-exit-worktree-tools`

```yaml
id: claude/worktrees-isolation/enter-exit-worktree-tools
provider: claude
family: worktrees-isolation
name: EnterWorktree / ExitWorktree tools
what: Built-in tools that let Claude create/enter a worktree mid-session and later return to the original directory, in response to a request like "work in a worktree." Entering moves the session's working directory, write access, and project config to the worktree.
surfaces: [cli, sdk-api]
ownership: user
persistence: session
mechanics: >
  `EnterWorktree` creates an isolated worktree and switches into it, or switches into an
  existing one when passed a `path`; on first entry the target may be a worktree of the
  current repo or of a nested repo in a multi-repo workspace (2.1.203+). A `path` outside
  `.claude/worktrees/` prompts for approval before entering (2.1.206+) and neither an
  `EnterWorktree` permission rule nor "don't ask again" suppresses it — only
  `bypassPermissions` skips it. From within a worktree session or a pinned-directory subagent
  (e.g. `isolation: worktree`), only the `path` form under the session repo's `.claude/worktrees/`
  is available. `ExitWorktree` returns to the original directory and is unavailable to
  subagents that already run in their own working directory. Entering/exiting a git-created
  worktree moves the session transcript with the working directory (2.1.198+).
sources:

    retrieved: 2026-08-02

    retrieved: 2026-08-02

```

```yaml
  2.1.198).
```

### `claude/worktrees-isolation/worktree-hooks`

```yaml
id: claude/worktrees-isolation/worktree-hooks
provider: claude
family: worktrees-isolation
name: WorktreeCreate / WorktreeRemove hooks (non-git VCS)
what: Hooks that replace the default git worktree creation and removal logic entirely, enabling worktree isolation for non-git systems (SVN, Perforce, Mercurial) or placing worktrees outside .claude/worktrees/. The hook's stdout directory becomes the session working directory.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: mixed
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  Configure `WorktreeCreate` and `WorktreeRemove` hooks in `settings.json`. A `WorktreeCreate`
  command hook reads the worktree name from JSON on stdin (e.g. via `jq -r .name`), creates
  the working copy, and prints the directory path to stdout for Claude Code to use as the
  session working directory; a `WorktreeRemove` hook cleans up on session end. Because the
  hook replaces default git behavior, `.worktreeinclude` is not processed — copy local config
  inside the hook. A worktree created by a `WorktreeCreate` hook keeps its transcript at the
  launch directory. If the hook prints something other than the created directory, startup
  errors and exits with code 1.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/worktrees
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents the WorktreeCreate/WorktreeRemove hook contract (stdin name, stdout path), the .worktreeinclude exclusion, and the SVN example.
```

### `claude/worktrees-isolation/worktree-launch`

```yaml
id: claude/worktrees-isolation/worktree-launch
provider: claude
family: worktrees-isolation
name: Run parallel sessions with worktrees (--worktree / -w)
what: Launching Claude with a worktree flag creates an isolated git worktree on a new branch and starts the session inside it, so edits in parallel sessions never collide. Each named invocation in a separate terminal is an independent isolated session.
surfaces: [cli]
ownership: user
persistence: local-config
mechanics: >
  `claude --worktree <name>` or `claude -w <name>` creates a worktree under
  `.claude/worktrees/<name>/` at the repo root on a new branch `worktree-<name>`;
  omitting the name auto-generates one (e.g. `bright-running-fox`). Interactive runs
  require prior workspace trust (run `claude` once to accept the dialog) or the flag
  exits with an error; `-p` non-interactive runs skip the trust check. Passing a PR
  number prefixed `#` or a full GitHub PR URL fetches `pull/<number>/head` from origin
  into `.claude/worktrees/pr-<number>`. Reusing a name opens the existing worktree; with
  default base `"fresh"` a clean, unmodified, self-commit-free (or merged-and-deleted-branch)
  reuse resets to the default branch, otherwise it reopens at the old tip. A worktree
  shares the main checkout's `.git` directory, project-scope plugins (v2.1.200+), and
  saved permission approvals to `.claude/settings.local.json` (v2.1.211+). Resuming a
  session (interactive, `-p --continue/--resume`, Agent SDK) returns to its worktree;
  `--fork-session` starts in the launch directory instead.
sources:
  - url: https://code.claude.com/docs/en/worktrees
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Documents --worktree/-w flag, default path/branch naming, PR-branch form, name reuse behavior, trust requirement, resume behavior, and what a worktree shares with the main checkout.
  - url: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/anthropics/claude-code/main/CHANGELOG.md
    retrieved: 2026-08-02
    note: Version-gates behavior changes (2.1.211 approval save location, 2.1.208 fresh-base fetch, 2.1.212 resume/symlink fixes).
```

**Reconciliation (engine-template@cdbbc335):**

```yaml
repo_usage: The build-isolation law depends on worktrees living at the native `.claude/worktrees/<name>/` layout (11 live worktrees present at cdbbc335), but the engine does NOT itself invoke `claude --worktree`. It CONSUMES the layout: /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.engine/tools/wiring.py (line 309) hard-codes `.claude/worktrees/` as "the platform's per-session worktree dir"; /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.engine/tools/checkout_health.py resolves the main checkout via `git worktree list --porcelain`; /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.engine/tools/instantiator.py (line 962) gitignores `.claude/worktrees/`.
overlap: CLAUDE.md's isolated-copy law ("I work in an isolated copy — never in your project folder's git history"), /Users/shanekidd/Developer/engine-template/.engine/operations/build-orchestration.md (line 62, parallel workers "each in its own isolated worktree"), and set-routine's isolation gate (below) all rest on this.
note: The engine treats worktree creation as the platform's job (see desktop-parallel-sessions) and only reads/validates the result. It couples to the literal `.claude/worktrees/` path convention.
```

**Assessment:** action mode observes; enforcement none — signal only; failure mode n/a; portability provider-specific.
